# Project complete document compilation

> This bundle includes the full text of all listed project narrative/status documents. It is a documentation compilation, not an archive of source code, notebooks, data pixels, model weights, or binary results.

## How to read this bundle

The consolidated overview, historical mid-review/poster transition record, and current decision records appear first. The transition record explicitly separates stage claims from current results. Older protocol/planning/review files are included verbatim afterward for completeness; some preserve superseded instructions. The controlling split decision takes precedence: the current later holdout is non-confirmatory and must not be opened for further outcome analysis.

# Major Project — authoritative context and status

**Status snapshot:** 2026-10-19 UTC  
**Repository:** `chmurali2006/Major-Project`  
**Active branch:** `arena/a9fb47a3-major-project`  
**Purpose:** give a single, evidence-bounded map of the project's methods, results, limitations, and current-safe actions. This is a synthesis of repository and returned artifacts, not a new experiment, independent reproduction, security certification, clinical assessment, or complete literature review.

## Executive status

The project is a set of related but distinct research/engineering strands in dermoscopic-image integrity, tamper localization, and classifier impact. They do **not** form one validated end-to-end system and their row counts or scores must not be pooled into one benchmark.

**Completed T1:** three classifiers (DenseNet121-Base, DenseNet121-Aug, ResNet50-Aug) were evaluated on the fixed 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation cohort and the returned validation records were ingested. Source/operator identity checks passed against retained references; the run reports `test_accessed:false`. The augmented classifiers reached validation BA 0.7927 and 0.7573, respectively. JPEG70 raised accuracy but reduced MEL sensitivity for all three architectures. This is validation/development evidence; validation participated in checkpoint selection and no independent source-pixel/model replay or confirmatory generalization was established. Details: [`deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`](deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md).

**No confirmatory TEST result is available for the current project.** A historical `isic-study-v2` run receipt reports that 1,000 test items were evaluated, but exact membership is unrecoverable from retained evidence; overlap with the later 500-image holdout is unknown. The later holdout is non-confirmatory development evidence, closed to further outcome analysis, and must not be described as untouched, pristine, disjoint, or final TEST. PH2 likewise has historical project use and incomplete membership/source lineage; it is not treated as a clean confirmatory external cohort.

## Historical mid-review/poster and transition boundary

The earlier mid-review deck and poster describe a separate ISIC 2018 fragile-watermarking study; their headline figures are historical reports, not current T1 results. The source PDFs and receipt chronology have unresolved provenance/date issues, and the PDFs are not included in the public package because embedded image pixels have not been screened. Use [`deliverables/MIDREVIEW_POSTER_TRANSITION_RECORD.md`](deliverables/MIDREVIEW_POSTER_TRANSITION_RECORD.md) for the reported numbers, limitations, source links, date caveat, and an evidence-bounded transition script. Do not present the Drive metadata dates as presentation dates: they are later than this status snapshot.

## Research framing

A cautious working framing is: **an evidence-bounded comparison of integrity, localization, and diagnostic-model impact under specified image changes**. Keep these contracts distinct:

1. **Integrity:** does decoded content match trusted evidence?
2. **Localization:** where are changes indicated, at what map resolution/coverage, with what false positives and cost?
3. **Classifier impact:** how do specified operations or marking alter a frozen research classifier's outputs?

Do not claim a new clinically reliable method, clinical benefit/safety, patient-level independence, production deployment, DICOM compliance, SOTA, novelty, or universal robustness. Integrity failure does not prove malicious intent. Classifier changes do not establish clinical harm or mechanism.

## Workstream map and evidence

| Workstream | Current evidence | Appropriate interpretation / limit |
|---|---|---|
| **Track A: detached integrity/localization** | 500-image validation receipts; 15,000 records. `bounded289` sidecar 18,620 B/image vs Full4 524,378 B (96.45% less). Reported copy/blur IoU: bounded289 0.837/0.795; Full4 0.837/0.795; Full8 0.680/0.644. Compact maps available for 0/3,500 tested global-operation rows. | Protocol-specific storage/coverage/latency tradeoff. Returned records were audited; transformations were not replayed from source pixels in this environment. Expanded tile maps are not pixel-exact. Not a universal optimum or deployed PKI claim. Evidence: `deliverables/verification-evidence/adapted-validation500-v1/`. |
| **Track C: original frozen DenseNet impact** | 500 validation images × 12 cases (6,000 rows). JPEG70: accuracy 86.4%→89.2%, MEL sensitivity 33/56 (58.93%)→22/56 (39.29%), BA 74.40%→67.39% (−7.006 pp). | One validation-selected adapted DenseNet and specified operators, not a clinical model or embedding-effect study. Original narrative CI [−13.144, −1.311] pp; the later T1 ingester's baseline interval [−13.042, −1.413] pp uses its recorded bootstrap procedure; keep analysis-specific intervals tied to their source artifacts. |
| **T1: multi-architecture classifier validation** | Shared 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation cohort; 30 standard operator rows and 48 EditGuard contrast rows in ingested tables. Intact BA / MEL sensitivity: Base .7440/.5893; DenseNet-Aug .7927/.7679; ResNet-Aug .7573/.6250. Under JPEG70 accuracy rose +2.80/+2.40/+3.60 pp, while MEL sensitivity shifted −19.64/−7.14/−21.43 pp. DenseNet-Aug ΔBA −1.77 pp [−6.80,+3.04]; ResNet-Aug −7.34 pp [−13.03,−1.87]. | Paradox persists in this validation cohort. Its severity is architecture-dependent; attenuation appears for DenseNet-Aug but not ResNet-Aug. No test access, clinical interpretation, or independent replication. Source hash matched `src/multi_arch_classifier.py` (`510b94df1e79ce1d894ed9643edf3d89c78b95aed84af190633a6a167ee8d587`). Tables: `deliverables/tables/`. |
| **T1 EditGuard paired validation** | `marked_intact` vs each model's own unmarked intact: DenseNet base ΔBA −9.80 pp [−16.06,−3.21], ΔMEL sensitivity −25.00 pp; DenseNet-Aug −4.46 [−9.15,−0.11], −8.93 pp; ResNet-Aug +1.79 [−2.22,+6.03], +1.79 pp. Marking fidelity mean MSE 170.9716, PSNR 29.5999 dB, SSIM .8701. | Architecture-specific validation contrasts; these do not show a clinical benefit or harm. The CSV contains distinct operator-only, embedding-given-operator, and combined contrasts for five key conditions. |
| **Separate matched EditGuard + DenseNet validation** | Return audit checked archive/rows/maps/paired summaries and 500 Signed4 signatures under a separately supplied public key. EditGuard had positive maps on 310/500 intact images (62%); operation-specific performance varied. | Receipt and signature consistency only; no original-pixel/model replay, issuer identity proof, or equal-storage comparison. See `deliverables/MATCHED_EDITGUARD_VALIDATION_RETURN_REVIEW.md`. This return is separate from T1's own paired outputs. |
| **Track D: diagnostic-constrained QIM** | 12-image guide-TRAIN feasibility return (10 nonMEL/2 MEL), 72 rows, receipt/signature checks. One corrected MEL example increased MSE .0764→5.8181; no class-decision benefit; JPEG70 payload BER about 50%. | Unchanged candidate's small feasibility stop-gate is closed. Do not scale unchanged design or generalize failure to the field. No independent image/operator replay. See `deliverables/DIAGNOSTIC_EMBEDDING_PILOT_REVIEW_ADDENDUM.txt`. |
| **Protocol-v2 RGB-HMAC / learned localization** | Separate fragile per-block RGB-HMAC protocol, tests, synthetic TRAIN-only diagnostic (7 examples), and an incomplete real-image sanity trace (4/12 receipts; `complete=false`). | Not T1. No complete multi-seed ISIC learned-localization result is established. Synthetic learning metrics are not ISIC results; incomplete sanity trace is not full-ISIC performance. Legacy `heldout` commands are revoked. See `PROTOCOL_V2.md`. |
| **Historical `isic-study-v2`** | Retained receipt reports `test_n=1000`; expected manifest hash cannot be recomputed because membership/per-image verdict artifacts are absent. | Test access occurred; exact historical IDs and overlap with the later cohort are unknown. Recovery against available artifacts is closed unless genuine original evidence appears. |
| **PH2** | Historical project notes describe a 120-image stand-in split by lesion, 24 held out/opened once; exact source/membership lineage is unavailable. | Not a clean confirmatory external cohort. Do not claim guaranteed disjointness or treat a proposed PH2 N=200 evaluation as clean without new provenance evidence. |

## Provenance, chronology, and reproducibility

- The adapted cohort manifest SHA-256 is `c524a43fce2951dbd68514a94d60610a8daa4f88fd40b8b93c53ee1f03f5aa42`.
- The T1 results archive ZIP passed integrity check; its summary/protocol records report no test access; the returned classifier module SHA matched the checked-in module. Ingestion also checked membership/source/operator references and prediction structure. These checks support artifact consistency, not proof of the original environment or an independent replay.
- Historical receipts contain timestamps `2026-10-20T23:46:07+00:00` and `2026-11-03T10:42:53`, both later than this 2026-10-19 status date. Older docs include a 2026-11-24 addendum claiming those timestamps were not future-dated. That chronology is invalid relative to the authoritative date; treat the run timestamps/addendum as future-dated and unauthenticated. This is a provenance concern, not proof of fabrication.
- The local sandbox previously lacked Torch, authorized source-image inputs, and reviewed weights; do not claim independent source-pixel or model replay from the local audit. T1 used an external Kaggle run; no TEST access was part of it.
- This active branch contains a large untracked/modified working set. It is not a clean release snapshot or evidence that a normal clone contains all workspace files. Do not bulk-add/delete or claim release readiness without curation and clean-clone checks.
- Literature details, data/source licenses, upstream code/checkpoint redistribution rights, and project attribution require formal review. Keep raw medical pixels, restricted model weights, credentials, and private keys out of public/package uploads.

## Safe current actions and hard boundaries

- Start with [RUNNING.md](RUNNING.md) for synthetic demonstrations, test commands, and T1 ingestion. Its prior holdout instructions were replaced with safe guidance.
- `FREEZE_PROTOCOL.md` is a revocation notice, not an active test protocol.
- Do not access the later 500-image holdout for additional performance analysis. Do not construct a substitute membership manifest and claim it is historical.
- PH2 is not clean confirmation by default. A future external evaluation must audit source and prior membership first.
- T1 results are validation-only and must never be labelled TEST results.
- Before any confirmatory study, obtain a new authorized cohort, audit source identity and exact/perceptual duplicates against all recoverable prior data, use verified lesion/patient grouping where available, prespecify metrics/thresholds, and freeze artifacts before outcome access.

# Complete project context package — read me first

**Snapshot date:** 2026-10-19 UTC  
**Repository:** `chmurali2006/Major-Project`  
**Branch:** `arena/a9fb47a3-major-project`

This is a curated, integrity-manifested context snapshot of the project: authoritative project status, study protocols, source code, tests, sanitized notebooks, key narrative documents, derived result tables, and retained text/JSON audit receipts. It is designed so a reviewer can understand the whole project and inspect implementation/evidence boundaries without receiving restricted or unnecessary medical data.

## Start here

1. `PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md` — authoritative cross-strand status, results summary, restrictions, and document index.
2. `deliverables/MIDREVIEW_POSTER_TRANSITION_RECORD.md` — historical reported results and the current-vs-historical transition narrative; original PDFs remain at linked source locations because their embedded pixels are unscreened.
3. `deliverables/PROJECT_FULL_OVERVIEW_AND_GAPS.md` — detailed study/evidence inventory.
4. `deliverables/CLAIMS_EVIDENCE_LIMITS.md` — claim language and caveats.
5. `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` — completed validation-only multi-architecture results.
6. `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md` and `deliverables/GATE0_PROVENANCE_STATUS.md` — historical TEST access, unresolved membership, and provenance/chronology limits.
7. `RUNNING.md` — safe usage; no holdout commands are authorized.

## Scope and important exclusions

This package deliberately excludes original medical-image pixels and metadata spreadsheets that could expose source records, model/checkpoint weights, private keys/credentials, the 40 MB T1 return ZIP containing visual panels, result ZIP archives whose contents were not individually reviewed for redistribution, opaque NPZ/map binaries, image and media files, third-party full-text papers/PDFs, `.git` history, local environments, caches, and build products. The source mid-review and poster PDFs are also not embedded: their presentation graphics have not been screened for image pixels or redistribution rights; the transition record preserves extracted text, reported results, source links, and the date/provenance caveat. Historical claims are not current results. Generated notebooks are included as source-only copies with outputs and cell execution state stripped.

Therefore this is a **complete project context + source + text/derived-evidence package within privacy, licensing, and scope safeguards**, not a byte-for-byte clone of the workspace or a full data/model reproducibility kit. The generated `PACKAGE_MANIFEST.csv` lists included paths, byte sizes, and SHA-256 hashes; excluded categories are recorded in `PACKAGE_EXCLUSIONS.md`.

## Evidence boundaries

- T1 is validation-only on 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation images and reports `test_accessed:false`. No T1 result is a TEST result.
- Historical `isic-study-v2` test access is recorded; exact membership is unrecoverable from retained artifacts, and overlap with the later 500-image holdout is unknown. The later holdout is closed to further outcome analysis and is non-confirmatory.
- PH2 has prior project use and incomplete exact membership/source lineage. Do not call it a clean confirmatory external cohort.
- Receipt/metric/hash checks are not source-pixel/model replay, security certification, clinical validation, or proof of authorship.
- Dates in retained run receipts that are later than 2026-10-19 are future-dated and unauthenticated in this snapshot; do not present them as proven execution timestamps.
- Verify dataset, upstream-code, model, paper, and image licenses before any public redistribution.

The package builder is `scripts/build_context_package.py`. Build and re-run its inventory check before re-uploading after any material source changes.
```

# T1 — Multi-architecture validation results

**Status:** Returned Kaggle run ingested; fixed-cohort validation experiment complete. These are validation/development diagnostics, not TEST results and not an estimate of external generalization.

## Protocol and integrity

- Fixed adapted-ISIC2019 cohort: 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation; manifest SHA-256 `c524a43fce2951dbd68514a94d60610a8daa4f88fd40b8b93c53ee1f03f5aa42`.
- The returned run reports `test_accessed: false`. Ingestion found matching source-image hashes, received-image hashes, operator seeds, labels, and membership against retained baseline references for all three models.
- Returned `code/src/multi_arch_classifier.py` SHA-256 matches the checked-in module: `510b94df1e79ce1d894ed9643edf3d89c78b95aed84af190633a6a167ee8d587`.
- The two augmented protocols report ImageNet initialization, 20 epochs, batch size 8 with accumulation 2, and TRAIN-only geometric/color augmentation. Checkpoint selection used validation balanced accuracy; consequently, these same-cohort operator results are descriptive validation analyses, not an independent test.
- Source archive: `google_drive/t1-results-v1.zip` (ZIP integrity checked). Ingest with `python scripts/ingest_t1_multi_arch.py --results-zip <archive>`.

## Intact validation performance

| Model | Accuracy | Balanced accuracy | MEL sensitivity |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| DenseNet121-Base | 0.864 | 0.7440 | 0.5893 |
| DenseNet121-Aug | 0.812 | 0.7927 | 0.7679 |
| ResNet50-Aug | 0.860 | 0.7573 | 0.6250 |

Both augmented models clear the plan's validation BA criterion (>0.75) and show non-trivial MEL sensitivity. This does not imply TEST performance.

## JPEG70 accuracy–sensitivity pattern

All three models show higher accuracy but lower MEL sensitivity than their own intact validation condition:

| Model | Accuracy change | BA change (paired 95% CI) | MEL sensitivity change |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| DenseNet121-Base | +2.80 pp | −7.01 pp [−13.04, −1.41] | −19.64 pp |
| DenseNet121-Aug | +2.40 pp | −1.77 pp [−6.80, +3.04] | −7.14 pp |
| ResNet50-Aug | +3.60 pp | −7.34 pp [−13.03, −1.87] | −21.43 pp |

Thus the accuracy–sensitivity paradox persists in this validation experiment. Its magnitude is smaller for DenseNet121-Aug than for the historical baseline, but not for ResNet50-Aug; the paired BA interval for DenseNet121-Aug includes zero. Do not generalize this architecture-specific pattern beyond the fixed validation cohort.

## EditGuard paired validation contrasts

The table below reports the `marked_intact` condition relative to each model's own `operator_without_embedding_intact` condition:

| Model | Marked BA | Δ BA (95% CI) | Marked MEL sensitivity | Δ MEL sensitivity |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| DenseNet121-Base | 0.6460 | −9.80 pp [−16.06, −3.21] | 0.3393 | −25.00 pp |
| DenseNet121-Aug | 0.7481 | −4.46 pp [−9.15, −0.11] | 0.6786 | −8.93 pp |
| ResNet50-Aug | 0.7753 | +1.79 pp [−2.22, +6.03] | 0.6429 | +1.79 pp |

The returned table also includes the required paired `embedding_given_operator` and combined `total_embedding_plus_operator` rows for intact, copy32, JPEG70, JPEG85, and Gaussian05; inspect `deliverables/tables/table_editguard_ssim_and_multi_arch.csv` for all metrics, class-stratified paired intervals, and conditions. EditGuard image-marking fidelity averaged MSE `170.9716`, per-image PSNR `29.5999 dB`, and SSIM `0.8701` over the returned marked validation images. The direction and size of the classifier contrast varies by architecture; it is not evidence that marking improves clinical classification.

## Artifacts

- `deliverables/tables/table_multi_arch_diagnostic_drift.csv` — 30 model/operator summary rows.
- `deliverables/tables/table_editguard_ssim_and_multi_arch.csv` — EditGuard fidelity and paired-condition summaries.
- `google_drive/t1-results-v1.zip` — returned Kaggle outputs, including per-image predictions and visual panels.
- `deliverables/kaggle_t1_multi_arch_validation.ipynb` — reproducible Kaggle runner.

No TEST images, labels, predictions, or outcomes were accessed in this T1 run.

# Historical mid-review and poster record — transition evidence, not current status

**Prepared:** 2026-10-19 UTC  
**Purpose:** preserve what the project reported at mid-review/poster stage so the later change in scope can be explained without mixing historical and current results. This is an archival synthesis, not a new experiment or verification of the old image-level results.

> **Read this before quoting the mid-review or poster.** Their metrics belong to an earlier fragile-watermarking system and an ISIC 2018 Task 1 evaluation. They are not results from current T1 classifier validation, the detached-signature/localization comparison, or the current RGB-HMAC protocol-v2 study. Keep the studies, datasets, metrics, and evidence levels separate.

## Historical source artifacts

The source PDFs are retained in their original Google Drive locations. Text was extracted for this record, but the PDFs themselves are not copied into the public context folder or ZIP because their embedded visual content has not been screened for medical-image pixels, source licensing, or redistribution permission.

| Stage artifact | Original Drive file | Original Drive link | Size | Drive created/modified metadata returned |
|---|---|---|---:|---|
| Mid-review | `Major_Project_MidReview_PPT - Murali Cherapaka.pdf` | [Open original mid-review PDF](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1sC0mhoLs7VRvLcfPmuhtvVkZUJeHymAq/view?usp=drivesdk) | 432,031 bytes | 2026-10-21 08:36:44 UTC |
| Poster | `Team06-Final-Poster - Murali Cherapaka.pdf` | [Open original poster PDF](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1GB5OCnoylz-htXmQJsNwEL6lg6zwmM6x/view?usp=drivesdk) | 1,510,060 bytes | 2026-10-25 10:27:35 UTC |

Drive search also found same-size copies named `Major_Project_MidReview_PPT.pdf` and `Team06-Final-Poster.pdf`; their extracted text matched the author-labelled versions. This record cites the author-labelled copies as the chosen references, not as proof that they are the only or final presentation files.

**Date/provenance warning:** both Drive timestamps fall after the authoritative project snapshot date and after today's date (2026-10-19 UTC). They cannot establish when the presentations actually occurred. Treat them as returned metadata anomalies; do not put those dates on a presentation timeline as event dates. No verified event date/time, agenda, or presentation schedule was recovered from these files. Confirm the actual dates with the team/course records before describing when the mid-review or poster presentation happened.

## What the mid-review reported

The deck framed the project as **“Diagnosis-Safe Watermarking for Medical Image Tamper Localization”** and described a keyed, position-bound fragile mark on 8×8 blocks, using two-level integer Haar detail coefficients and a 60-bit QIM tag. The receiver used the returned PNG and key to produce `INTACT`, `TAMPERED`, or `CANNOT_SAY` block-level decisions. The deck explicitly said the system checks post-protection changes, not whether the original image was truthful; it was not a disease-diagnosis system.

The deck reported a primary ISIC 2018 Task 1 evaluation (`n=2,594`, 22 conditions) and cited `runs/isic-full-v8/receipt.json`, code commit `28e4e6e`, and a dataset receipt. Its headline reported values were:

- Copy-move detection: **12,969 / 12,970** cases, reported as 99.99%, at detection IoU threshold ≥0.10 for conditions 1–5.
- Mean localization IoU: **0.9936** for conditions 1–6.
- Benign handling false claims: **0 / 12,970**; clean images claimed tampered: **0 / 2,594**.
- Mean PSNR: **40.47 dB**; mean coverage: **98.7%**.

The deck also retained important failures and trade-offs, which must travel with any use of those numbers:

- Burned-in text (condition 21): mean IoU **0.272**; detection at IoU ≥0.50 **0.0%**; reported silent failures in 98.9% of images and 16.8% of blocks. Detection at a lenient threshold was not precise localization.
- Telea inpainting (condition 22): mean IoU **0.907**, with 6.4% silent changed blocks.
- JPEG Q90 certified only about 0.1% of blocks; Q75/Q50 certified none. Zero false claims did not mean useful coverage under those transformations.
- A separate 300-image JPEG-envelope result reported **2/300 (0.67%)** false claims at Q100, above the stated ≤0.50% ceiling. This was a separate probe, not part of the frozen 22-condition battery.
- The deck's own ablations documented changes to the reader, block-scale claim, content binding, scattered-mismatch abstention, and disabling Carrier B. Their denominators differed and should not be pooled.

These figures are **what the historical deck reported**. Although corresponding receipt files are present in the workspace, their timestamps are later than the 2026-10-19 status date; source pixels and the original execution environment are not available here for replay. The current project record flags those timestamps as future-dated/unauthenticated. Therefore do not present the figures as independently reproduced or as current validated performance. Use the original receipt and protocol only after provenance and chronology are resolved.

## What the poster condensed

The author-labelled poster used the same diagnosis-safe watermarking title and team identity. Its extracted abstract repeated the **99.99% detection** and **0.9936 localization IoU** headline on ISIC 2018 Task 1 (`n=2,594`) and the separate **2/300 false claims at JPEG Q100** result. It also said a learned refinement stage was planned but not yet validated. This is a historical poster statement, not current project status or authorization to claim that the refinement was completed.

The poster is not a new independent result: it is a presentation artifact summarizing that earlier work. Its extracted text is shorter than the mid-review deck, so refer to the deck/receipts for limitations and denominators.

## Transition to the current evidence record

A defensible transition narrative is:

1. **Earlier emphasis:** build and report a fragile-watermark verifier for local tamper evidence. The mid-review deck made the method concrete and reported strong results on its declared ISIC 2018 battery, while also exposing a Q100 false-claim ceiling miss, weak burned-text localization, and near-zero coverage for several JPEG transformations.
2. **Refinement of the research question:** separate integrity, localization, and classifier impact rather than treating them as one end-to-end claim. Compare trust/evidence, distortion, localization coverage, storage, latency, and diagnostic-model effects explicitly.
3. **Current state (2026-10-19 snapshot):** the repository contains multiple distinct strands, not a single successor system. Current T1 is a three-architecture classifier validation study on TRAIN/validation only; detached integrity/localization comparisons and the separate RGB-HMAC protocol have their own protocols and evidence. The unchanged small diagnostic-constrained QIM candidate was evaluated at a 12-image TRAIN feasibility stop-gate and was not scaled.
4. **Current headline from T1:** on the shared validation cohort, JPEG70 increased accuracy while reducing MEL sensitivity across all three architectures. For DenseNet121-Aug, intact validation balanced accuracy was 0.792712 and MEL sensitivity 0.7679; JPEG70 paired BA change was −1.77 pp [−6.80, +3.04]. This is a validation-only classifier-impact result, not a tamper-localization score and not a TEST result.

The transition above is a **safe synthesis of the documented research strands**, not proof that a particular historical failure caused a later design choice. The retained files do not establish a complete dated decision trail from presentation feedback to each subsequent experiment. Avoid claiming causal continuity unless meeting notes, dated commits, or supervisor feedback substantiate it.

## Current-vs-historical claim guardrails

| Topic | Historical mid-review/poster | Current controlling interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| Project unity | One fragile-watermarking story | Multiple distinct study lines; do not pool cohorts or scores as one end-to-end system. |
| Main data/result | ISIC 2018 Task 1, `n=2,594`, v8 receipt claims | T1 is a separate classifier validation on fixed 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation; it did not access TEST. Historical ISIC 2018 performance is not T1 evidence. |
| Headline metric | Detection 99.99%, mean IoU 0.9936 | Historical reported numbers with unresolved receipt chronology and no independent pixel/model replay in this workspace. Not a current validated claim. |
| Known weakness | Q100 0.67% false claims; poor text localization; coverage failures | Keep these limitations visible whenever the old headline is shown. Do not use only its strongest number. |
| Learned refinement | Poster says planned, not validated | Do not imply completion from the old poster. Current candidate work is separate, narrowly scoped, and stopped at its feasibility gate. |
| Presentation dates | PDF names indicate stages; Drive metadata returns future dates | Actual event dates/times remain unverified. Confirm with course/team records. |

## Recommended short oral transition (evidence-bounded)

“At mid-review, we presented a fragile block-watermarking verifier for detecting and localizing image edits, with reported ISIC 2018 results. The deeper record showed that its headline detection did not remove important limitations: the separate JPEG-Q100 probe exceeded our false-claim ceiling, burned-in text localization was weak, and some benign JPEG settings had almost no committed coverage. We then organized the work into separate integrity/localization and classifier-impact questions. Our current T1 evidence is a distinct validation-only three-model study: JPEG70 increased accuracy but reduced melanoma sensitivity across architectures. We are careful not to present that as a TEST result, as a replication of the old watermark study, or as proof of clinical harm. The historical deck and poster are retained as stage evidence, while their dates and original image-level receipts still need provenance confirmation.”

## Archival handling

- This text record is included in the curated context package and is the safe portable summary for transition discussion.
- The source PDFs remain linked above in their original Drive folders. Do not copy them into the public context folder until embedded image pixels, licenses, and permissions are screened.
- Keep the mid-review/poster metrics in a **historical** section only. They must not overwrite the current master status or be silently relabelled as current T1 results.

# Project overview, evidence inventory, and next research plan

**Purpose:** one consolidated view of the research project as represented in this checkout: what the project is trying to do, which experimental strands exist, what results are presently defensible, what remains unverified or missing, and what would be required for a strong academic submission.

**Scope note:** this is a synthesis of repository documentation, retained receipts, code/protocol descriptions, and the documented audit decisions. It is not a new experiment, independent reproduction, security certification, clinical assessment, or external literature review. Receipt checks and software tests are not treated as scientific outcome evidence.

**Status date:** 2026-10-19 UTC. **Current branch:** `arena/a9fb47a3-major-project` (active). This report describes the working tree as it stands; it does not imply that every file is tracked, released, or reproducible from a clean clone. Use [`PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md`](../PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md) as the current document index and cross-strand status. A few retained historical records contain dates later than this status date; those timestamps are future-dated/unverified and are not accepted as proof of when a run occurred.

---

## 1. Executive summary

This repository contains a broad medical-image integrity and tamper-localization research effort with **multiple related but distinct study lines**. The coherent domain is dermoscopic imagery, especially ISIC 2019, but the work should not be reported as one unified experiment or one proven new method.

The most defensible present story is a **controlled, validation-stage comparison of distinct protection contracts**:

1. **Exact integrity:** can a receiver establish that decoded image content still matches trusted evidence?
2. **Localization:** where did image content change, with what storage, latency, map-coverage, and false-positive costs?
3. **Diagnostic-model impact:** how do specified image operations or an embedding change a frozen classifier’s class-sensitive outputs?

The strongest completed evidence is currently **receipt-audited validation evidence**, not a complete independent replay from original pixels and model weights. The adapted ISIC cohort’s later 500-image holdout is **not confirmatory**: a different historical `isic-study-v2` run evaluated a 1,000-image test partition, its exact IDs cannot be recovered from the retained evidence, and overlap with the later holdout is unknown. Do not call the later holdout untouched, analyze it further for outcomes, or use it for confirmatory claims. Reopen recovery only if authentic original membership evidence appears. Any future confirmatory evaluation needs a new provenance-audited cohort and a frozen protocol.

**No SOTA, clinical-safety, patient-independence, universal robustness, or new-algorithm novelty claim is supported by the current record.** The academic opportunity is to make a careful domain-specific comparative study—or, after substantially stronger evidence, a new-method paper. More unit tests, model names, or methods do not by themselves close the scientific gaps.

---

## 2. Project identity and the main scope problem

### Recommended research framing

A defensible working title is:

> **Integrity and Tamper Localization in Dermoscopy: A Controlled Evaluation of Cryptographic, Compact, and Learned Methods**

A suitable central question is:

> **How do image-integrity and localization methods trade off exactness, map coverage, storage, and latency under specified image changes, and what class-sensitive classifier changes accompany those operations?**

This frames the contribution as an **audited, domain-specific evaluation**. It does not claim that the project has invented the cryptographic primitives, watermarking, segmentation, or medical image signatures it uses. If a new-method contribution is later pursued, novelty must be established feature-by-feature against relevant literature and through matched experiments.

### Why the repository can be confusing

The checkout includes, among other things:

- a protocol-v2 RGB HMAC authentication and learned-localization line documented in `PROTOCOL_V2.md` and `RUNNING.md`;
- an adapted 2,500-image ISIC cohort used for detached-signature/localization and classifier studies;
- EditGuard adapter, pilot, and matched validation work;
- a small diagnostic-constrained QIM embedding pilot;
- historical `isic-study-v2` receipts and older experimental artifacts.

These lines have different protocols, cohorts, methods, and evidence levels. **Do not combine their counts or scores as if they came from one matched benchmark.** In particular, the RGB-HMAC protocol and the detached Ed25519 tile-manifest baselines are different systems with different key/trust models and pixel costs.

---

## 2.1 Historical mid-review/poster and transition story

The earlier mid-review deck and poster describe an ISIC 2018 fragile-watermarking experiment, not the current T1 classifier-impact study. They report headline detection/localization figures but also document poor burned-text localization, JPEG coverage failures, and a separate JPEG-Q100 false-claim rate above the stated ceiling. The source artifacts' Drive timestamps postdate the authoritative 2026-10-19 snapshot, and their claimed receipt timestamps are not independently authenticated here. The source PDFs are not redistributed in the public context package because embedded image pixels and licenses have not been screened. See [`MIDREVIEW_POSTER_TRANSITION_RECORD.md`](MIDREVIEW_POSTER_TRANSITION_RECORD.md) for exact historical claims, current-vs-historical guardrails, source links, chronology caveats, and an oral transition script. Treat the old values only as historical reported results, never as current T1 metrics.

---

## 3. Research strands and current status

| Strand | What it studies | Current status | Defensible boundary |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Protocol-v2 RGB HMAC authentication** | A fragile embedded per-block authenticator plus diagnostic/learned localization pipeline for 8-bit RGB images. | Protocol and implementation are documented; engineering/wiring diagnostics exist. A complete multi-seed ISIC study, full ablations, and a valid confirmatory evaluation are not established here. | The HMAC carrier modifies RGB low bits, requires a secret key and trusted version context, and breaks under loss-changing codecs. It is not encryption, a DICOM signature profile, or a clinical deployment. |
| **Detached integrity/localization (Track A)** | Ed25519-signed evidence at Full4/Full8 grids and compact `bounded289`, evaluated on a fixed validation cohort and controlled operators. | 500-image validation receipts reviewed across 15,000 records. Useful measured storage/coverage/latency tradeoffs. No full pixel/operator replay from original images in this runtime. | A tile failure expanded to pixels is not pixel-exact localization. Abstention is not a successful map. This is not a universal Pareto result or a deployed PKI system. |
| **Frozen classifier/operator impact (Track C)** | Class-sensitive changes in the original adapted DenseNet121 under a fixed set of image operations. | 500-image validation return reviewed; 6,000 stored condition rows. Its checkpoint was selected using validation. T1 later added two architectures in a separate 10-operator analysis; see next row. | Operator-associated changes for the stated model/cohort only—not clinical harm, causal biology, robust generalization, or a watermark effect. Stored outputs were not rerun on source pixels here. | 
| **T1 multi-architecture validation** | Original DenseNet121 baseline plus DenseNet121-Aug and ResNet50-Aug on fixed TRAIN/validation; ten standard operators and paired EditGuard conditions. | Returned Kaggle archive passed ZIP integrity and ingestion checks; 30 standard-condition summaries and 48 EditGuard contrast rows. Model source hash matched checked-in code; source/operator hashes, seeds, labels and received-image hashes matched retained references. `test_accessed:false` reported. | Validation-only, same validation cohort used in checkpoint selection; no TEST access, independent source-pixel/model replay, external cohort, or confirmatory generalization. Full metrics/caveats: `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. |
| **Matched EditGuard + classifier validation** | Operation-by-operation localization and paired classifier contrasts for EditGuard and a Signed4 reader. | Archive structure, row arithmetic, maps, paired summaries, and 500/500 Signed4 signatures passed the documented return audit using a separately supplied raw public key. | Receipt/signature audit only. Source pixels and models were not independently replayed; the public-key issuer is not independently authenticated. Signed4 adds a large sidecar and is not a cost-matched comparison. |
| **Diagnostic-constrained QIM (Track D)** | A small guide-TRAIN pilot of fixed-carrier embedding and non-carrier correction. | 12-image pilot reviewed; unchanged candidate’s stop-gate is closed. | A small negative feasibility result, not a successful method and not evidence that the broader field fails. Do not scale the unchanged candidate or present it as a core result. |
| **Learned refiner and other pilots** | Synthetic diagnostics, small image pilots, capacity/profiling and implementation studies. | Mixed exploratory/engineering artifacts are retained. Some are synthetic-only or use mirror data with non-official provenance/preprocessing. | Keep out of headline efficacy claims unless a specific artifact is independently validated under a declared protocol. |
| **Historical `isic-study-v2` evaluation** | An older run whose receipt reports a 1,000-image test evaluation. | Test access is confirmed; exact membership is unrecoverable from the retained run directory and uploaded collection ZIP. | Later holdout overlap is unknown. The recovery attempt is closed unless authentic original membership evidence appears. |

### 3.1 Protocol-v2 RGB authentication line

`PROTOCOL_V2.md` specifies an 8×8 RGB fragile carrier. In outline, the method canonicalizes 192 RGB sample low bits, computes a 24-byte truncated HMAC-SHA256 over versioned context, an image/version identifier, dimensions, block coordinates, and canonicalized block content, then embeds the tag in low bits. Verification is strict: any block failure rejects the image-level integrity check. The standard profile also includes a semi-robust DCT carrier; the legacy Haar profiles remain historical.

Important limitations encoded in the protocol:

- any JPEG/re-encoding/resizing breaks exact RGB integrity and is reported as content change, not automatically malicious intent;
- embedded low bits change the image and must be measured for fidelity;
- benchmark keys are not production secrets; the receiver needs trusted key and image/version identity handling to prevent replay;
- inputs are 8-bit RGB, not 16-bit or DICOM-native;
- neural scores/counts do not override failed cryptographic authentication;
- the method is explicitly not a reproduction of a published paper.

The protocol-v2 runner documentation belongs to this separate RGB-HMAC/learned-localization line. Its earlier small TRAIN-only diagnostic and incomplete real-image sanity trace do not establish completion of the full multi-seed study. Any legacy `heldout` instructions are withdrawn by the current safe-run guide and `FREEZE_PROTOCOL.md`; do not execute them on the current cohort. This protocol-v2 status is unrelated to completed T1 classifier validation, which used a separate runner and did not access TEST.

### 3.2 Track A: detached exact-integrity evidence and localization

The adapted validation work compares `Full4`, `Full8`, and `bounded289` on 500 validation images with deterministic operators. The retained review reports:

| Method | Additional evidence / image | Local copy / blur IoU | Fine maps on global-operation rows | Copy verification time (approx.) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Full4 | 524,378 B | 0.837 / 0.795 | Available on tested rows | 0.0567 s |
| Full8 | 131,162 B | 0.680 / 0.644 | Available on tested rows | 0.0214 s |
| `bounded289` | 18,620 B (96.45% below Full4) | 0.837 / 0.795 | 0 / 3,500 rows | 1.869 s |

These numbers support a **protocol-specific tradeoff**: the compact method sharply reduces sidecar size and matches Full4 on the two reported local cases, while taking longer and abstaining on the tested global operations. They do not establish that it is the best method overall. The receipts include 15,000 rows; an unchanged blur-surrogate no-op on one image generated one verified no-op row per method. Preserve and describe that case as a no-op, not as successful manipulation detection.

### 3.3 Track C: classifier impact under operators

The returned analysis covers a 500-image validation cohort and 12 conditions (6,000 rows). The central illustrative result is JPEG70 on one frozen adapted DenseNet121:

- Accuracy: **86.40% → 89.20%**.
- Melanoma sensitivity: **58.93% (33/56) → 39.29% (22/56)**.
- Balanced accuracy: **74.40% → 67.39%**, change **−7.006 percentage points**; reported paired image-bootstrap 95% interval **[−13.144, −1.311] points**.
- Always-nonMEL reference: **88.8% accuracy, 50% balanced accuracy, 0% MEL sensitivity**.

The finding is that overall accuracy can conceal class-sensitive changes on this validation comparison. This is the original single-model Track C analysis; T1 is a later, separate three-model validation analysis, summarized in `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. The correct boundary is the stated models and same validation cohort; no patient-level inference, independent model replay, clinical endpoint, biological mechanism, or causal embedding effect is established. Validation participated in checkpoint selection. The original Track C narrative reports a paired interval of [−13.144, −1.311] points; the later T1 ingestion reports [−13.042, −1.413] for the same rounded baseline JPEG70 point estimate using its recorded bootstrap procedure. Do not silently combine these analysis-specific intervals. White-box FGSM/PGD records, where cited, are adapted development attacks with label/model-gradient access, not black-box robustness evidence.

### 3.4 Matched EditGuard validation return

The completed return contained 500 images × 10 cases and two readers, with 10,000 localization rows, 5,000 paired diagnostic rows, 10,000 map comparisons, and 500 signed packets. The reviewer checked archive structure and CRC, notebook/source identity, row uniqueness and arithmetic, map comparisons, paired summaries, and signatures. All 500 Signed4 packet signatures verified using the separately provided 32-byte public key.

Selected receipt-reported findings:

| Condition | EditGuard pixel recall |
|---|---:|
| Copy32 | 0.714 |
| Local-blur32 surrogate | 0.865 |
| JPEG70 / JPEG85 | 1.000 / 1.000 |
| JPEG95 | 0.010 |
| Resize90 then restore | 1.000 |
| Contrast90 / Contrast110 | <0.001 / 0.049 |
| Gaussian noise 0.05 | 0.811 |
| Intact images | 310/500 positive maps (62% image-level false-positive rate) |

A positive map is not necessarily useful localization: some operation cases have very low recall despite frequent positive maps. Signed4 reported full pixel recall on the tested tampered cases by expanding failed 4×4 tiles, but incurs false-highlight area and adds **524,378 bytes per image** on top of the marked image. It is therefore not an equal-storage end-to-end comparison.

For the intact marked-vs-original classifier contrast, the return reports accuracy 86.4%→88.4%, balanced accuracy 74.4%→64.6% (−9.8 points; paired class-stratified image-bootstrap interval −16.2 to −3.8), MEL sensitivity 58.9%→33.9%, and 40/500 label flips. In the paired embedding-given-operator contrasts, the reported balanced-accuracy changes range from −4.8 to −11.1 points, with unadjusted descriptive bootstrap intervals below zero for all ten conditions. Treat these as receipt-checked contrasts for one validation-selected classifier, not clinical harm or independent replication; there is no multiplicity adjustment or patient-level inference.

**Provenance boundary:** the return audit proves consistency of the received artifacts with the audited protocol and verifies signatures relative to the supplied key. It does not prove the identity of the key issuer, recreate the original pixel inputs, rerun the models/operators, or establish that the returned predictions are independently reproducible.

### 3.5 Track D: QIM stop-gate

The small pilot used 12 guide-TRAIN images (10 nonMEL, 2 MEL). Guided correction matched the fixed output on 11/12. The one changed MEL image’s reported RGB-level MSE rose from approximately **0.0764 to 5.8181**; compared variants did not change predicted classes. A strict equal-distortion gate returned the baseline for all examples. JPEG70 payload bit error was about 50%, so clean decoding does not imply codec robustness.

The fixed-carrier geometry gives a structural bound: if the carrier samples are locked, any nonzero correction outside the carriers adds squared distortion to the fixed baseline. This candidate is closed as a small negative/limited feasibility result. It is not an argument that all diagnostic-aware watermarking fails.

---

## 4. Evidence quality: what is known and what is not

| Evidence class | What is present | What it establishes | What it does not establish |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Implementation/unit tests** | Many repository tests and fixture checks across cryptography, data handling, metrics, notebooks and models. A focused review-test subset recently passed. | Specific code paths satisfy stated test assertions. | Medical performance, effectiveness, novelty, security certification, data provenance or generalization. Do not headline test counts to faculty. |
| **Synthetic/wiring checks** | Synthetic authentication, model wiring, refiner and pilot fixtures; a small T4 wiring execution is reported in the v2 notes. | Some execution paths run on designed fixtures; selected engineering failures can be detected. | Real-image clinical evidence, full GPU reproducibility, SOTA comparison, or test-set validity. |
| **Receipt/metric audits** | Row counts, arithmetic, hashes, map consistency, stored metrics and paired summaries for validation returns. | Internal consistency of the retained records against the audit code and supplied files. | Independent replay from original images/models, trustworthy source execution, issuer identity, or causal interpretation. |
| **Signed-packet verification** | 500/500 packet signatures verify against an externally supplied raw public key. | Packets are cryptographically consistent with that public key. | Who owns the key or whether its issuer is trusted. |
| **Source/data replay** | Original ISIC image pixels and frozen weights were not available in this runtime for the current audits. | Nothing beyond documented receipt-level results. | Reproduction of image operators, inference, or independent numerical confirmation. |
| **Confirmatory evaluation** | No valid independent final evaluation for the current later holdout. Historical v2 test use is confirmed, but membership is unavailable. | The current project must not claim untouched-test generalization. | Disjointness between historical v2 IDs and later holdout, or patient independence. |

The available reviewer scripts and artifact paths are important, but tool consistency does not turn a receipt into a replay. All paper/presentation tables should label the evidence class explicitly.

---

## 5. Data, split, provenance, licensing, and security

### Cohort and split decision

- The adapted cohort manifest contains 2,500 selected records, partitioned as 1,500 TRAIN, 500 validation, and 500 later internal holdout. Preparation receipts report unique source-decoded and prepared RGB hashes with no exact cross-split duplicates **within that cohort**.
- The reported selection is lesion-disjoint, but patient-level linkage/independence is not established. Source-ID ranges are proxies, not verified institutions.
- The separate old `isic-study-v2` receipt reports `test_n=1000`. Its membership manifest/verdict array is missing from both the retained seed-0 run directory and the uploaded membership-evidence ZIP. The ZIP has only aggregate receipts and a collection manifest; the receipt’s expected manifest hash cannot substitute for the absent manifest.
- Exact overlap with the later 500-image holdout is unknown—not proven to overlap, and not proven disjoint. Recovery attempts against the available artifacts are closed. Reopen only if genuine original membership evidence or a source/input bundle that regenerates the exact recorded manifest hash appears.
- Keep the later holdout closed to additional outcome analysis and classify it as non-confirmatory development evidence. A protocol freeze cannot restore its pristine status.

### Source and model provenance

The repository contains some unavailable legacy commit identifiers, receipts whose execution identity cannot be independently validated, and at least one scope mismatch noted in the Gate 0 provenance report. Do not silently repair those historical records. Label unresolved records legacy/unverified and exclude them from headline findings unless original source bundles and run metadata become available.

**PH2 is not a clean unused external test by default.** Historical project files describe PH2 as an earlier pilot/instrument-validation corpus; `scripts/prepare_ph2.py` describes a third-party mirror rather than an official download, and `scripts/build_results.py` describes a lesion-grouped 60/20/20 PH2 split whose heldout portion was opened once. The corresponding `runs/ph2-v1` directory and exact prior membership are not present in the current tree. This is evidence of prior PH2 use, but it does not identify every prior image or establish which of the 200 images were exposed. Therefore the proposed PH2 N=200 external test in the uploaded overhaul plan is **blocked as confirmatory** until genuine prior-use membership and source provenance are reconciled. Do not call PH2 guaranteed-disjoint, and do not calibrate a threshold on all PH2 images and then report those same images as test data.

The uploaded `project-context/FINAL_PROJECT_OVERHAUL_MASTER_INSTRUCTIONS.md` is retained as a user-provided proposal, not a scientific protocol or evidence record. Its prospective model thresholds, PH2 assumptions, capacity claims, and promised outcomes must be treated as hypotheses until independently checked and, where applicable, experimentally observed.

The matched EditGuard return’s exact Drive notebook/archive hashes, key provenance caveat, and audit outputs are recorded in `deliverables/MATCHED_EDITGUARD_VALIDATION_RETURN_REVIEW.md` and the v3 verification-evidence directory. The classifier checkpoint and cohort hashes are recorded in their respective returns. Those hashes identify artifacts; they do not, by themselves, prove who generated them or that source pixels were used as claimed.

### Licensing and privacy

The project uses ISIC 2019 and acknowledges source datasets. The relevant dataset terms are non-commercial and attribution obligations apply. Verify ISIC and upstream method/checkpoint licences before public release or redistribution. Do not put original medical pixels, restricted checkpoints, private keys, or credentials in Git/Drive deliverables. Existing audit returns intentionally exclude original pixels and model weights.

### Security and clinical boundaries

- HMAC-on-image and detached Ed25519 signatures are distinct trust constructions; neither should be described as a DICOM-compliant workflow here.
- A public benchmark key is not a production secret. Freshness, key distribution, signer identity, and trusted image/version identifiers require an external workflow.
- Integrity failure means a mismatch to expected authenticated content; it does not establish malicious intent.
- Distortion/perceptual receipts are not clinical equivalence. The classifier is a research probe, not a clinical system.

---

## 6. What the project can safely claim now

1. **Engineering tradeoff:** on the retained validation protocol, `bounded289` sharply reduces detached sidecar bytes versus Full4 while trading verification time and global-operation fine-map availability.
2. **Class-sensitive evaluation lesson:** in the original single-model Track C and the later three-model T1 validation analysis, JPEG70 increased overall accuracy while reducing MEL sensitivity and BA. T1 also shows architecture-dependent attenuation; all claims remain specific to the validation cohort and are not clinical or test-performance claims.
3. **EditGuard validation observation:** in the returned validation records, intact images had a high positive-map rate and localization recall varied substantially by operation; the Signed4 comparison reported full recall at a large additional-storage cost.
4. **Bounded negative result:** the unchanged QIM correction did not demonstrate a useful advantage in a 12-image TRAIN feasibility pilot; this closes that candidate, not the research area.
5. **Evidence practice:** the project has distinguished receipt consistency, signature verification, and independent pixel/model replay rather than treating them as equivalent.

Every claim above must be labeled by dataset split, method, denominator, and evidence status. Use “reported in the returned receipts” where appropriate.

## 7. Claims that are not supported

Do **not** claim:

- SOTA, novelty, superiority, a universal optimum, or a completed matched benchmark against EditGuard/WAM/OmniGuard;
- that EditGuard, the classifier, or any method was independently reproduced from source pixels/models in this audit;
- untouched, pristine, disjoint, final-test, or confirmatory generalization for the later holdout;
- patient-independent data, verified institutional balance, or cohort representativeness;
- clinical harm, clinical safety, preserved diagnosis, patient benefit, or biological mechanism;
- robust authentication through JPEG/re-encoding for the fragile RGB HMAC protocol;
- DICOM 16-bit support, DICOM-standard compliance, production PKI/key management, or replay prevention;
- exact pixel localization from expanded tile maps;
- claims based only on unit-test counts, synthetic fixtures, the 8-image mirror pilot, or cross-paper scores from incompatible protocols.

---

## 8. Gaps and risks that still matter

### Scientific gaps

- **No valid confirmatory cohort:** the later holdout cannot serve; no new external/prospective cohort is documented here.
- **No independent full replay:** source ISIC pixels and pinned model weights were absent from this audit environment; returned metrics remain receipt-level.
- **T1 multi-architecture validation:** completed and ingested on the fixed TRAIN/validation cohort, with no TEST access. The validation results and per-architecture EditGuard contrasts are summarized in `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` and the two CSV tables. They are validation/development evidence, not confirmatory generalization or independent pixel/model replay.
- **Narrow model evidence:** T1 adds two more architectures, but all three share one fixed validation cohort and validation-based checkpoint selection. No source-pixel/model replay, institution-level external cohort, or confirmatory generalization is established.
- **No verified patient grouping:** lesion-disjoint/exact-hash checks do not prove patient-level independence.
- **No matched-cost SOTA comparison:** methods use different storage, model/checkpoint, runtime and trust costs. Cross-paper reported numbers are not comparable baselines.
- **Threat-model distinction:** exact integrity, benign re-encoding, localized edits, and malicious manipulation must have separately defined expected outcomes. JPEG/contrast changes are not automatically malicious.
- **Multiplicity and uncertainty:** future analyses should predeclare primary metrics and comparisons, account for repeated conditions and group dependence, and preserve failures/abstentions in denominators.
- **No established clinical endpoint:** no prospective workflow, clinician reader study, patient outcome, or clinical utility assessment is documented.
- **Novelty review incomplete:** relevant prior work includes fragile authentication, DICOM signatures, EditGuard, WAM, OmniGuard, medical image watermarking and non-embedding authentication. The literature file itself warns that several bibliography details need verification.

### Reproducibility and project-management gaps

- **Parallel narratives remain by design:** the root RGB-HMAC/protocol-v2 line and the adapted-cohort validation studies are different workstreams. `PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md` now serves as the current crosswalk; do not merge protocols or results. Some append-only/historical notes retain older planning language and must be read under their dated status corrections.
- **Execution boundary corrected:** `RUNNING.md` now gives safe current instructions, and `FREEZE_PROTOCOL.md` has been replaced by a revocation notice with no runnable TEST switches. Older protocols/notebooks remain historical artifacts; their presence does not authorize executing a holdout stage.
- **Untracked working tree:** the snapshot currently contains hundreds of untracked project files and modified files. A clean-clone build, tracked-file manifest, attribution audit and reproducible release snapshot are not established. Do not bulk-add or delete artifacts without an explicit curation/ownership review.
- **Full regression not verified in this runtime:** a focused audit/documentation subset passed, but the complete Torch-backed suite was not collected because Torch was unavailable. Historical full-suite counts in old documents are not current claims.
- **Packaging and citations:** verify source/checkpoint licenses, DOI/publication metadata, dataset attribution, artifact hashes, and exclusion of sensitive pixels/weights before release.

---

## 9. Recommended next plan, in order

### Phase 0 — Lock the evidence boundary (now)

- Keep the old membership recovery closed; do not inspect the later 500-image holdout for outcomes.
- Treat the decision report and Gate 0 status as controlling; mark older instructions clearly historical or superseded.
- Keep the revised faculty deck focused on integrity/localization/class-sensitive impact. Keep software test counts in engineering QA material only.

**Exit condition:** no slide, abstract, README, or execution guide calls the current cohort untouched or authorizes new analysis on it.

### Phase 1 — Reproducibility and provenance audit

- Build a single inventory of each study line: cohort/protocol, code hash, checkpoint hash, returned artifact hash, evidence class, and allowed claim.
- Recover authentic source commits/logs where possible; otherwise label records legacy/unverified.
- Resolve bibliography and licensing before any public release.
- Curate a clean, tracked source/release snapshot without data pixels, keys, restricted weights, or duplicate stale artifacts.

**Exit condition:** a clean clone can identify which protocol produced every headline number and reproduce the non-data packaging/audit steps.

### Phase 2 — New confirmatory data design

- Obtain a genuinely new authorized cohort, preferably independently sourced or prospectively collected. A new random split of previously used images is not enough.
- Preflight source identity and exact/perceptual duplicates against recoverable development images; if patient/lesion IDs exist, split by verified groups and describe missing linkage honestly.
- Freeze inclusion/exclusion, operations/threat model, methods, primary metrics, sample-size rationale, subgroup plan, confidence intervals, and stop/failure handling before outcome access.

**Exit condition:** a signed, immutable protocol and auditable cohort manifest exist before test outcomes are generated.

### Phase 3 — Independent matched benchmark

- Re-run methods on source pixels and frozen checkpoints, with pinned environments and logs. Verify all numerical metrics from per-image outputs.
- Include strong non-embedding controls (whole-image signature and signed tile-hash manifest) and appropriate learned baselines only when implementations/protocols can be made comparable. Treat incompatible published scores as literature context.
- Compare at declared storage/compute budgets, report pixel/tile localization, map availability, false-positive maps on intact images, per-operation recall/precision/IoU, latency, bytes, and distortion.
- Repeat classifier-impact evaluation with at least one independent architecture and, if possible, another external cohort. Use paired/group-aware uncertainty and distinguish natural image operations from intentional edits.

**Exit condition:** independent source/model replay, complete denominators, uncertainty estimates, costs, failures, and provenance are all auditable.

### Phase 4 — Academic paper and defense package

- Write around the question, protocol, data lineage, matched baselines, primary findings, failure cases, uncertainty, and limitations—not code/test volume.
- State the narrow contribution that the evidence earns. Claim a new method only if prior-art comparison and matched experiments establish a distinct advantage.
- Put unit/integration test details in a short reproducibility appendix. Keep the 12-image QIM result as a bounded negative appendix unless a redesigned study earns a main-text role.
- Release only permitted code/receipts, include environment/source manifests, and obtain supervisor/owner approval for the final scope and licensing.

---

---

## 11. Bottom line

The project is not empty and it is not made stronger by inflating claims. It has meaningful implementation, controlled validation receipts, informative failure cases, and a useful domain-specific problem. Its academic strength will come from **one coherent research question, trustworthy data lineage, independent replay, fair matched baselines, patient/group-aware evaluation where possible, transparent uncertainty, and disciplined claims**.

For now, present the completed work as an evidence-calibrated validation study. Keep the later holdout closed. Do not claim SOTA. The decisive next scientific investment is a new provenance-audited cohort and an independently reproducible benchmark—not another software-test headline or a duplicate GPU run.

# Test-split recovery and disposition decision

**Current disposition recorded:** 2026-10-19 UTC. An older header carried the future date 2026-11-24; that date is not accepted as a valid chronology as of this record.  
**Decision:** Treat exact membership of the historical `isic-study-v2` 1,000-image test evaluation as **unrecoverable from the retained project artifacts reviewed**. Stop attempting to recover it from the searched seed-0 folder and collection bundle. Reopen only if a genuine original run archive, manifest, verdict-ID artifact, or source/input bundle is later found.

## Evidence reviewed

- The retained `results/isic-study-v2/seed-0/C0_count.json` records `n=1000`, `test_n=1000`, protocol `isic-study-v2`, commit `6dc18a4`, and expected manifest hash `47cf4f949c3005556da3b06df28f90eeba8e0138efdf305ddd0ea9a7aac339c9`.
- `C0_B.json` carries the same run-identifying fields.
- The uploaded collection ZIP `isic-study-v2-seed-0-membership-evidence.zip` has SHA-256 `8cc66bad8fe9e75bfccb1e02b3c5432bfd5f79d8747482350b0bdf0793d26315`; its CRC passes. It contains only `C0_count.json`, `C0_B.json`, and `collection_manifest.json`. Those receipts are semantically consistent with the local copies.
- Neither the matching retained run folder nor the collection ZIP contains `manifest.json`, `experiment.json`, `C0_count_verdicts.npz`, `C0_B_verdicts.npz`, or a run-specific execution/source manifest. Therefore the expected manifest hash cannot be recomputed and the 1,000 test IDs cannot be extracted or matched against the later cohort from the available evidence.

This conclusion is an operational disposition of the available evidence, not a claim that no external backup could ever exist. The prior manifest hash inside a receipt is not a substitute for the missing manifest.

## Consequences for claims and evaluation

1. Record that the **ISIC-study-v2 test partition was evaluated**. Do not say that the project has never accessed test data.
2. Historical membership overlap between those 1,000 images and the later 500-image internal holdout is **unknown**—neither overlap nor disjointness has been established.
3. Retire “Final TEST untouched,” “pristine holdout,” and confirmatory/generalization claims based on the later 500-image holdout. For project reporting, classify it as **non-confirmatory development evidence with historical overlap unresolved**.
4. Do not open that 500-image split for additional performance analysis or model/threshold selection. Preserve existing artifacts unchanged and label their evidence status; do not delete or rewrite history.
5. If a confirmatory estimate is required, define a new evaluation cohort/protocol only after auditing it against recoverable historical image and lesion membership. Do not imply patient-level independence unless patient linkage is actually available and verified.

## Reopening condition

Reopen this decision only if new original evidence appears—for example, the actual v2 manifest or verdict NPZ from an archived run output, or a source/input bundle that regenerates the manifest with the receipt’s exact hash. A reconstructed candidate that fails the exact hash check is not the historical membership list.

Evidence/tables: `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` and the two T1 tables under `deliverables/tables/`. |
| **Diagnostic-constrained embedding (Track D)** | Receipt-reviewed 12-image guide-TRAIN pilot (10 nonMEL/2 MEL); guided correction changed 1/12, with that MEL image’s RGB-level MSE increasing 0.0764→5.8181; all compared predicted classes stayed the same; JPEG70 payload BER about 50%. Evidence: `deliverables/verification-evidence/diagnostic-embedding-train12-return-v1/`. | The unchanged candidate did not demonstrate a useful advantage in this small TRAIN feasibility pilot; the stop-gate is closed. | No pixel/operator replay from returned pixels; no independent evaluator, clinical benefit, transfer, robustness, generalisation, or novelty result. Do not scale or reframe the negative result as universal failure. |
| **Matched EditGuard + DenseNet validation** | 500 validation images; 10 fixed operators; 10,000 localization rows, 5,000 paired diagnostic rows and 10,000 map comparisons were checked for completeness and arithmetic; all 500 Signed4 packet signatures verified with the separately supplied key. Full per-case results: `deliverables/MATCHED_EDITGUARD_VALIDATION_RETURN_REVIEW.md`. | **Validation/development comparison.** EditGuard intact positive-map rate was 62% (310/500); results vary sharply by operation. | We did not rerun source-image operations or classifier inference locally. One validation-selected adapted classifier; not a clinical model. Signed4 adds 524,378 bytes/image and is not equal-cost. We verified signatures against the supplied key but did not establish its organizational owner. |
| **Cohort integrity and independence** | 2,500 prepared records; 2,500 unique source-decoded RGB hashes and prepared hashes; no exact cross-split duplicates in the receipts. | No exact duplicates detected by the reported RGB hash checks. | pHash not run; patient-level independence and institutional balance are not established. ID-range source labels are descriptive proxies only. |
| **Later 500-image internal holdout** | Fixed cohort receipt exists, but the earlier 1,000-image v2 test-ID list is unrecoverable from retained evidence; overlap status is unknown. Historical v2 already evaluated test. | **Non-confirmatory development evidence only; do not describe as untouched TEST or inspect further for a confirmatory claim.** | Recovery attempt is closed by decision. A future confirmatory estimate requires a new provenance-audited cohort and preregistered protocol. No patient-level independence claim absent linkage. |

## Presentation rule

Label each number by split, method, and evidence status. Distinguish **receipt verification** from **pixel/operator replay**. Do not call validation an independent test, do not infer patient independence, and do not make clinical, novelty, SOTA, or universal-robustness claims. This handout is a summary of the cited repository receipts, not new experimental evidence.
```

### 09. deliverables/MATCHED_EDITGUARD_VALIDATION_RETURN_REVIEW.md

**Role:** Matched EditGuard returned-record audit  
**Status note:** Receipt/signature audit; not pixel/model replay.

```text
# Matched EditGuard validation return — audit and results

**Audit date:** 2026-11-24 UTC  
**Status:** **receipt and signed-packet audit passed using the subsequently uploaded public key.** This is validation/development evidence only.

## What was received and checked

The three named Google Drive parts were downloaded and concatenated in order. The 277,001,270-byte ZIP passed the ZIP CRC test and contained 1,019 entries / 289,685,056 uncompressed bytes. All 10,000 localisation rows, all 5,000 paired diagnostic rows, all 500 map archives, all 500 signed4 packets’ lengths/headers, the exact cohort, returned Python source files, row-to-map confusion arithmetic, paired probability summaries and completion counts passed the existing return reviewer.

The exact Drive notebook was downloaded and supplied to the audit (`SHA-256 9107ca9bead8b9770a562a9375ba990f432e3b96dfb524696847f318e1193b9e`). The returned source bundle’s Python files match the notebook bundle, fixed cohort and the local reviewed protocol. The archive hash is `33ac519371e373081ae60ddd7f44ff8c55168e6fea43458c6a395297764997a9`. Part-level Drive IDs, sizes and hashes are recorded in `deliverables/verification-evidence/matched-editguard-validation-return-v2/input-manifest.json`.

**The ZIP omitted `public-key.bin`** because the executed notebook’s packaging cell did not include it, although the experiment runner writes it to the output directory. The user uploaded the raw 32-byte Ed25519 public key separately. I reran the strict audit with that file supplied externally: **all 500 packet signatures verified**, in addition to the structural, map and probability checks. The key is bound to the returned packets by those signatures, but its issuer identity is not independently authenticated. I also patched the local notebook copy’s packaging cell to include `OUT/'public-key.bin'` for future returns; the exact Drive notebook used for this audit remains preserved separately. The earlier partial audit is retained at `deliverables/verification-evidence/matched-editguard-validation-return-v2/`; the completed strict audit is at `deliverables/verification-evidence/matched-editguard-validation-return-v3/`.

## Headline results from the returned receipts

The CSV tables are the source for complete per-case values: [`table_editguard_metrics.csv`](tables/table_editguard_metrics.csv) and [`table_editguard_diagnostic_impact.csv`](tables/table_editguard_diagnostic_impact.csv). The final summary and strict row-to-map/signature review are in [`tables/summary.json`](tables/summary.json) and [`verification-evidence/matched-editguard-validation-return-v3/review.json`](verification-evidence/matched-editguard-validation-return-v3/review.json).

| Case | EditGuard pixel precision | EditGuard pixel recall | EditGuard pixel IoU | EditGuard image-positive rate / misses | Signed4 pixel precision | Signed4 pixel recall |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Intact | 0.000 (no positive pixels) | — | 0.000 | **62.0% false-positive images** (310/500) | no positive pixels | — |
| Copy32 | 0.871 | 0.714 | 0.646 | 90.4% positive; 128 targets have zero pixel overlap | 0.835 | 1.000 |
| Local blur32 surrogate | 0.971 | 0.865 | 0.843 | 100% positive | 0.835 | 1.000 |
| JPEG quality 70 | 0.999 | 1.000 | 0.999 | 100% positive | 0.999 | 1.000 |
| JPEG quality 85 | 0.998 | 1.000 | 0.998 | 100% positive | 0.998 | 1.000 |
| JPEG quality 95 | 0.991 | **0.010** | **0.010** | 100% positive, but very poor localization | 0.992 | 1.000 |
| Resize 90% then restore | 0.990 | 1.000 | 0.990 | 100% positive | 0.990 | 1.000 |
| Contrast 90% | 0.999 | **<0.001** | **<0.001** | 94.6% positive; 27 targets have zero overlap | 0.999 | 1.000 |
| Contrast 110% | 1.000 | **0.049** | **0.049** | 61.0% positive; 195 targets have zero overlap | 0.996 | 1.000 |
| Gaussian noise 0.05 | 0.981 | 0.811 | 0.798 | 100% positive | 0.983 | 1.000 |

Image-positive means only that a map contains at least one predicted pixel; it is **not** a claim of useful localization. Likewise, a positive image with near-zero recall does not constitute successful localization. Signed4 expands failed 4×4 tiles to pixels, so its apparent full recall comes with false-highlight area (for example, precision about 0.835 on the two local-edit cases), and it adds 524,378 bytes per image—about 262 MB decimal across this 500-image validation set—on top of the EditGuard-marked container. This is not a cost-matched end-to-end comparison.

For intact images, EditGuard marked at least one pixel on 310/500 images. The clean checkpoint therefore has a substantial false-alarm issue under this protocol. The local blur is explicitly a synthetic surrogate, not semantic lesion removal. These are ten predeclared cases—not twelve.

## Distortion and diagnostic-classifier contrasts

The returned per-image setup receipts give mean PSNR **29.60 dB** (median 30.91 dB), with mean per-image MSE 170.97 (median 52.74); all 500 marked images have nonzero MSE. These values are receipt-checked, **not recomputed from retained source/marked pixels**. The embedding is therefore not zero-distortion relative to the original image. It is inappropriate to describe it as imperceptible without a separately justified perceptual evaluation.

For the frozen adapted DenseNet on this validation cohort, the intact comparison (marked vs original) reports:

- Accuracy: 86.4% → 88.4% (+2.0 percentage points).
- Balanced accuracy: 74.4% → 64.6% (−9.8 points; paired class-stratified image-bootstrap 95% interval for the change: −16.2 to −3.8 points).
- Melanoma sensitivity: 58.9% → 33.9% (−25.0 points).
- 40/500 predicted labels flip.

The most relevant control is the **embedding-given-operator** contrast: operated marked vs the same operation applied to the original, with paired seeds/parameters. Across the ten cases, the observed balanced-accuracy change ranges from −4.8 to −11.1 points; all ten unadjusted descriptive paired bootstrap intervals are below zero. Melanoma sensitivity drops by 10.7–28.6 points across those cases. These comparisons suggest a consistent class-sensitive shift in this one imperfect classifier, not a clinical outcome or proof of patient harm. Validation was used for checkpoint selection; the image bootstrap is not patient-level, has no multiplicity adjustment, and does not establish calibration or clinical safety.

## Limits and next action

This is a careful audit of returned records, not source-pixel or model replay. We did **not** independently re-run EditGuard, the image operations, the classifier, or compute SSIM. The 500-image set is validation/development data, not a final independent test. None of these findings resolve the separate old-test membership/overlap question.

**Packaging fix for future returns:** update the notebook’s archive creation cell to include `OUT/'public-key.bin'` alongside the other root metadata files. For this completed run, the detached key was verified without editing the original archive or rerunning the GPU job. The uploaded public-key file SHA-256 is `eb896a22b9129d19bb50d4da64073b396265ab512d88b5092b3334e4951bf741`.

### Audit artifacts

- Completed strict reviewer output and full input/hash manifest: `deliverables/verification-evidence/matched-editguard-validation-return-v3/`
- Earlier partial reviewer output (superseded): `deliverables/verification-evidence/matched-editguard-validation-return-v2/`
- Per-case masks and timing: `deliverables/tables/table_editguard_metrics.csv`
- Paired diagnostic effects: `deliverables/tables/table_editguard_diagnostic_impact.csv`
- Summary with caveats and provenance hash: `deliverables/tables/summary.json`
- The original result parts and public key remain in Google Drive; local downloaded result parts and assembled ZIP were removed after the audit to avoid retaining large transient copies in the repository workspace.
```
