```text
# Presentation defense pack — evidence-led version

**Status date: 2026-10-19 UTC.** T1 multi-architecture validation has been ingested on the fixed 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation cohort; it reports `test_accessed:false`. The separate matched EditGuard validation return was also audited. Neither return was independently replayed from original image pixels/models in this environment. An earlier `isic-study-v2` receipt reports 1,000 TEST items, but exact membership is unavailable; overlap with the later 500-image holdout is unknown. The holdout is non-confirmatory development evidence and closed to further outcome analysis. `FREEZE_PROTOCOL.md` is now a revocation notice, not a usable TEST plan. Historical records with dates after 2026-10-19 are future-dated and not validated timestamps.

## Historical transition framing — use before current results

The earlier mid-review and poster describe a **different ISIC 2018 fragile-watermarking study**. The mid-review reported 99.99% copy-move detection and mean localization IoU .9936, but it also documented 2/300 (0.67%) false claims in a separate JPEG-Q100 probe, weak burned-text localization (mean IoU .272; 0% at IoU ≥.50), and near-zero JPEG Q75/Q50 coverage. Present those only as historical reported results with their limitations, not as current T1 metrics. Their source PDF metadata dates are future-dated relative to this 2026-10-19 snapshot, and the receipt timestamps are not authenticated; the actual event date remains unverified. See `deliverables/MIDREVIEW_POSTER_TRANSITION_RECORD.md` for citations and the full transition script.

## 60-second opening

“At mid-review we presented a fragile watermark verifier for local tamper evidence. Its reported headline results came with a Q100 false-claim miss and weak text localization. We then organized the project around separate integrity, localization, and classifier-impact questions. Our current validation evidence is not a replication of that earlier study. We compare three contracts in dermoscopic image protection: exact integrity, tamper localization, and diagnostic-model impact. In validation-only studies, compact signed evidence reduced storage while trading off localization coverage and latency. T1 evaluated three classifiers on the same 500-image validation cohort: under JPEG70, accuracy rose for all three while melanoma sensitivity fell; the drop was smaller for DenseNet121-Aug than the baseline but not for ResNet50-Aug. EditGuard localization varied by operation and had positive maps on 62% of intact images in a separate returned validation study. These findings remain specific to the methods and validation protocols studied and require a new provenance-audited cohort for confirmation.”

## Experimental protocol and provenance

We checked the returned validation records against the declared cohort, operation settings, row arithmetic, and packet signatures; this environment did not rerun the original image transformations or classifier inference. The historical v2 test membership remains unidentified, so the later ISIC holdout is excluded from confirmatory analysis. PH2 also has prior development use in this repository and is not a clean external test until that history is reconciled.

## Slide-by-slide speaking cues

1. **Title and status:** We frame the work as a dermoscopy-focused controlled comparison and name the three technical contracts.
2. **Transition (historical vs current):** distinguish the old ISIC 2018 QIM watermark report from current studies; pair headline results with Q100/text/JPEG-coverage failures; state that date and receipt chronology are unresolved. Do not imply the old numbers are T1 or that historical failures caused later changes without a dated decision record.
3. **Research question and contracts:** We separate exact integrity, localization, and diagnostic impact. A failed signature does not establish intent; a localization map is not authentication; a classifier shift is not clinical harm.
4. **Cohort and protocol:** We report the TRAIN pilot pool and validation cohort used by the analyses. We explain that earlier test IDs are unavailable and the later holdout is excluded from confirmatory claims. Lesion-disjoint selection does not establish patient-level independence.
5. **Track A:** We report both sides of the tradeoff: 18,620 versus 524,378 bytes per image (96.45% less), local-edit IoU matching Full4 in the two tested local cases, 0/3,500 fine maps on seven global cases, and approximately 1.87 s versus 0.057 s verification for copy. Full8 has copy/blur IoU .680/.644 and about .021 s verification. We count abstentions separately from successful maps.
6. **Classifier impact (Track C + T1):** Keep the original single-model Track C result distinct from T1. Track C's original DenseNet121 JPEG70 comparison: accuracy 86.4%→89.2%, MEL sensitivity 58.93% (33/56)→39.29% (22/56), and BA change −7.01 pp. T1 intact validation BA/sensitivity were 0.7440/0.5893 (base), 0.7927/0.7679 (DenseNet121-Aug), and 0.7573/0.6250 (ResNet50-Aug). JPEG70 accuracy rose for all three (+2.80/+2.40/+3.60 pp), while MEL sensitivity fell (−19.64/−7.14/−21.43 pp). This shows the paradox persisted on this shared validation cohort, with augmentation architecture-dependent; it does not establish test or clinical performance. Report source-image/model replay as not independently performed.
7. **Matched EditGuard validation:** We report positive maps on 310/500 intact images (62%) and operation-specific recall. Signed4 reported full recall on tested tampered cases but adds 524,378 bytes/image and expands tiles; our comparison is not equal-cost. We describe these as results checked from returned records.
8. **Evidence matrix:** We use the matrix to distinguish contracts and costs, not to claim a leaderboard or matched-cost SOTA comparison. We keep the small QIM feasibility result in the appendix.
9. **Close:** We summarize the measured storage/coverage/latency tradeoff, operation-dependent localization, and class-sensitive classifier result. We identify a new, provenance-audited cohort as a requirement for future confirmation.
10. **References:** We use DICOM/EdDSA and prior watermarking papers as context, not as reproduced results. We verify bibliography details and permissions before public release.
11. **Backup appendix:** We describe the 12-image QIM pilot as a bounded negative feasibility result, not as the main contribution or evidence against the broader field.

## Likely questions and concise, defensible answers

**What is the main contribution?**  
“The strongest completed contribution is an audited comparison of exact signed-integrity/localisation methods under explicit storage, latency, coverage, and diagnostic-impact reporting. The embedding extension is implemented and experimentally bounded, but we do not claim it is advantageous or novel.”

**Why not just use the full signed manifest?**  
“It is a strong zero-distortion baseline with dense coverage and faster verification in our measured implementation. Compact evidence uses much less storage, but is slower and abstains on several global changes. The results support a tradeoff, not a universal winner.”

**Does JPEG70 show that watermarking harms melanoma diagnosis?**  
“No. That study applied JPEG to original validation images; it measures operator-associated changes in one adapted classifier. It does not isolate an embedding effect or establish clinical harm.”

**Why did accuracy improve when sensitivity worsened?**  
“The validation set is imbalanced (444 nonMEL, 56 MEL). More correct nonMEL predictions can raise overall accuracy while melanoma recall falls. That is why we report sensitivity and balanced accuracy alongside accuracy.”

**Is your cohort patient-independent?**  
“We established lesion-disjoint selection and found no exact cross-split RGB hash duplicates in the preparation receipts. We did not establish patient-level independence, perceptual-hash separation, or source institution balance.”

**Is your method novel or state of the art?**  
“We do not claim either. The cryptographic primitives and fixed-carrier QIM are established components; prior-art overlap needs a feature-by-feature review. We report what our adapted experiments actually measured.”

**Why include a negative embedding result?**  
“It documents a predeclared feasibility boundary: the unchanged candidate did not provide a useful demonstrated advantage under this small TRAIN pilot. Keeping that result prevents us from presenting only favourable cases.”

**How did we verify the matched EditGuard results?**  
“We checked the returned records for completeness, metric arithmetic, map consistency, and valid Signed4 signatures under the supplied key. We did not rerun the image transformations or classifier inference, and we have not established who controlled the signing key.”

**What is the status of the final-test evidence?**  
“An earlier ISIC-study-v2 run evaluated 1,000 test images, but the exact IDs are no longer available in the retained run files or uploaded collection. We cannot determine whether those IDs overlap the later 500-image holdout. We therefore do not call that holdout untouched, do not use it for confirmatory claims, and will not inspect it further. A confirmatory result would require a new cohort and frozen protocol.”

**Can we use all 200 PH2 images as a fresh external test?**  
“Not on the current record. Earlier project files document PH2 development and a held-out evaluation, but the original membership is not present here. We must reconcile prior image exposure before treating any PH2 subset as external confirmation; threshold selection cannot be performed on the final test set.”

**Is this clinically safe or deployment-ready?**  
“No. The classifier is a limited research probe, not a clinical model. The work does not establish patient-level generalisation, clinical utility, trusted key infrastructure, or a DICOM deployment workflow.”

## Claim guardrails for the final slides and report

| Safe wording | Avoid |
|---|---|
| “96.45% less sidecar storage than Full4 in this protocol” | “96% more efficient overall” |
| “No fine map available for 3,500 global-operation rows” | “Robust to benign global changes” |
| “MEL sensitivity fell on this validation comparison” | “JPEG/watermarking causes melanoma misses in practice” |
| “Small TRAIN feasibility pilot; no useful advantage demonstrated” | “Our novel method failed universally” or “clinical preservation achieved” |
| “Lesion-disjoint; patient independence unverified” | “Patient-independent cohort” |
| “We checked the returned records against the declared validation protocol; we did not rerun image transformations or model inference locally” | “EditGuard independently reproduced” / “clinically validated” |
| “Historical v2 test was evaluated; later holdout overlap is unknown and non-confirmatory” | “Final test untouched” / “Final test confirms generalisation” |

## Test-split disposition and next study

We preserved the matched EditGuard return and checked its records against the declared protocol. The separate v2 recovery bundle contains aggregate records but no per-image test membership. We have closed recovery against the available artifacts, classified the later 500-image holdout as non-confirmatory development evidence, and will not inspect it for further outcomes. The draft in `FREEZE_PROTOCOL.md` does not authorize evaluation of this cohort. A future confirmatory study requires a new cohort with auditable provenance and a protocol frozen before outcome access.
```

### 11. deliverables/INDEPENDENT_REVIEW_REPORT.md

**Role:** Independent-review findings and updates  
**Status note:** Historical findings are retained; current disposition is cross-referenced.  
**Source SHA-256:** `2ae297e09df35040f95218d5a178feb87466355a11d982c9f72e1b812026bac2` (17906 bytes)

```text
# Independent technical review of Major-Project

**Original review date recorded:** 2026-10-19 (UTC). The report contains a later-dated addendum that must be read with the correction below.  
**Scope:** current checkout source, retained run artifacts, and selected validation-return rows. This review treats summaries as claims to check, not ground truth.  
**Not performed:** no original ISIC pixels were available in this audit environment; no full source-pixel/model replay or security certification was performed. Re-parsing rows/recomputing metrics checks artifact arithmetic, not that the claimed source images or predictions were genuinely produced by the stated run.

## Current date/provenance correction (2026-10-19 UTC)

The addendum below is headed 2026-11-24 and states that 2026-10-20 / 2026-11-03 receipt timestamps were not future-dated. That chronology is impossible relative to the authoritative current date 2026-10-19 UTC. Treat the 2026-11-24 addendum/date claim as invalid for current status; the retained receipt timestamps are future-dated and unauthenticated, and do not establish execution times. This does not by itself prove artifact fabrication; it means chronology/source identity remain unresolved pending primary logs. A later-reviewed T1 Kaggle return is documented separately in `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`; it is validation-only and does not resolve other provenance issues.

## Test-membership disposition (current status 2026-10-19)

The uploaded `isic-study-v2-seed-0-membership-evidence.zip` was checked (SHA-256 `8cc66bad8fe9e75bfccb1e02b3c5432bfd5f79d8747482350b0bdf0793d26315`). It contains the matching aggregate `C0_count.json` and `C0_B.json` receipts plus a collection manifest, but no test-ID manifest or per-image verdict NPZ. The exact 1,000-image membership therefore cannot be recovered from the retained evidence. The project disposition is to stop recovery attempts against available artifacts and treat the old membership as unrecoverable unless an original archive later appears. Overlap with the later 500-image cohort remains unknown; that cohort is non-confirmatory and must not be described as an untouched final TEST. See `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`.

## Bottom line

**The work is a substantial engineering prototype plus several carefully scoped comparative experiments. It is not a completed demonstration of a novel, clinically reliable tamper-localisation method.** Since the original review, T1 added a receipt-ingested three-model TRAIN/validation comparison; see `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. It strengthens the classifier-impact discussion but does not constitute independent source-pixel/model replay, a new external cohort, or confirmatory test performance. The strongest defensible story remains a measured engineering tradeoff plus validation-stage classifier impact, accompanied by a small negative embedding-feasibility pilot. The central learned HMAC-guided localiser has not been shown to outperform its controls in a complete, independently auditable medical-image study.

My recommendation is **major revision and scope reduction**, not abandonment. A defensible B.Tech project can be built from the existing work, but only if the primary contribution is stated narrowly, test-set history is reconciled, and provenance issues below are resolved before reporting results as final.

## What I checked independently

- Read the actual active runner and core RGB authentication code (`scripts/run_isic_study.py`, `scripts/run_suite.py`, `src/authentication.py`, `src/isic_classifier_training.py`) instead of taking prose descriptions as evidence.
- Parsed all 15,000 records in `deliverables/verification-evidence/adapted-validation500-v1/rows.jsonl`; recomputed method-level map coverage, IoU, and timing means.
- Parsed all 6,000 rows in `deliverables/verification-evidence/classifier-impact-return-v1/predictions.jsonl.gz`; recomputed intact/JPEG70 accuracy, melanoma sensitivity, balanced accuracy and AUC from probabilities.
- Ran a fresh deterministic synthetic 512×512 RGB input through `src.authentication.embed` and checked the resulting pixel distortion and verification. This is an implementation check, not medical evidence.
- Inspected the checked-in result receipts and the files actually retained beside them. I did not run returned code or infer missing image pixels from hashes.

## Findings, by severity

### Critical: “TEST untouched” is not true for every study in this checkout

The current `scripts/run_isic_study.py` constructs `test` and evaluates the `C0_count` and `C0_B` controls on it in the ordinary run path (lines 272–307); it also predicts and scores learned models on `test` (lines 329–333). There is no explicit one-time approval/freeze gate protecting those code paths. `scripts/run_suite.py` defaults to three seeds and `test_n=1000`, so the same test partition is available to repeated study runs.

Consistent with that code, `results/isic-study-v2/seed-0/C0_count.json` is a stored test evaluation: it reports `n=1000`, `test_n=1000`, and metrics. This means the **ISIC-study-v2 test split has already been evaluated**. A different, later 500-image cohort protocol may have a separate holdout, but I cannot verify that it is disjoint from the earlier test images: the retained results and subsequently uploaded collection ZIP lack the per-image verdict archive and test manifest needed to compare IDs. At the original review date, the slide wording “Final TEST untouched” was therefore too broad. The current disposition treats the old membership as unrecoverable and the later holdout as non-confirmatory development evidence; see `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`. This does not prove overlap, only that disjointness cannot be established.

### Critical: run provenance has unresolved timestamp and code-identity questions

- The retained `C0_count.json` records `provenance.started = 2026-11-03T10:42:53` and split seed `20261103`. Both dates are later than the current 2026-10-19 status date. Treat them as future-dated and unauthenticated; a split seed is not proof of when an evaluation ran. The contrary 2026-11-24 addendum is invalid for current chronology.
- `runs/isic-full-v8/receipt.json` records `created_utc = 2026-10-20T23:46:07Z`; this is later than the current 2026-10-19 status date and is future-dated, with chronology unverified. Check original run logs and source records before accepting it as an execution timestamp. It names its dataset `datasets\isic2018` while marking `dataset_is_primary=true`; that conflicts with the current checkout’s ISIC2019 primary-study framing and should be labelled as a separate legacy ISIC2018 run, not used as ISIC2019 evidence.
- The v2 result says it came from commit `6dc18a4`, but that commit object is not available in the current Git object database. The v8 receipt cites `28e4e6e`; its tree is marked dirty. These are not independently source-reproducible from this checkout.
- The v2 results directory retains aggregate JSON/interval files but not the per-image verdict NPZ or manifest that the runner says it writes. The aggregate values cannot be independently re-analysed at the image level or used to recover the evaluated membership here.

Until the timestamp and source-identity questions are checked against source archives, hashes, logs, and run records, **quarantine those run numbers from headline claims**. The unavailable commits, dirty tree, and missing membership artifacts independently prevent full reproduction. This is a reproducibility/provenance concern, not proof of misconduct.

### Major: the main learned-method result is missing

The repository’s main `isic-rgb-auth-v2` program is a keyed LSB authentication channel with classical controls and learned C8/C12 localisation variants. The only retained `results/isic-study-v2/seed-0` results are `C0_count` and `C0_B` controls and their aggregate comparison; there are no C8/C12 trained outputs there. The full three-seed/ablation study described in the protocol is not evidenced by those files.

Therefore the core claim that the learned model adds value over the classical/control system is **unanswered**, not negative and not positive. The 540 passing software tests (one CUDA-only test skipped in the recorded full run) are a real engineering strength, but tests establish implementation checks, not medical efficacy or learned superiority.

### Major: the embedded RGB HMAC changes the image and is fragile by design

`src/authentication.py` clears each 8×8 RGB block’s 192 LSBs, computes a 192-bit HMAC over the upper seven bits plus geometry, version ID and block address, and writes the tag into those cleared bits. A deterministic fresh synthetic check found approximately **50% of channel samples changed by ±1**, approximately **87.5% of RGB pixels changed in at least one channel**, MSE 0.5000 and PSNR 51.14 dB; the embedded output verified successfully. This agrees with the construction, but it means the method is **not zero-distortion and does not preserve the exact original RGB values**. The overwritten source LSBs cannot be recovered from this marked image alone.

That can be an acceptable fragile watermark design, but its contract must be stated as integrity of the marked representation, not exact preservation of the source. Lossy JPEG/re-encoding is expected to fail the MAC. The runner also uses the literal public benchmark key `b'isic-study-key'` (`scripts/run_isic_study.py`), so its scores do not demonstrate resistance to an adversary who knows or obtains the key. The implementation comments acknowledge this; any security claims must retain that limitation. Signed sidecar baselines are the necessary zero-distortion comparator.

### Major: one published aggregate statement disagrees with the raw validation rows

My row-level count found **501 `VERIFIED` records per method**, not 500, because one `local_blur32_surrogate` application to `ISIC_0069696` is a no-op (`changed_pixels=0`, unchanged RGB hash). Repeated across three methods, the raw rows contain 1,503 verified and 13,497 failed records. `docs/DESIGN_REVIEW.md` says “All 13,500 manipulated rows FAILED_INTEGRITY.” The three rows labelled as a blur condition are in fact unchanged inputs. This is a small, explainable reporting discrepancy, not a failure of the cryptographic methods; fix the denominator/wording and report the no-op explicitly.

### Moderate: the auxiliary validation evidence is numerically coherent, but not replay evidence

The validation rows support a useful and reproducible **receipt-level** storage/coverage/latency comparison. My independent arithmetic reproduces:

- `bounded289` vs `Full4`: 18,620 vs 524,378 bytes/image (96.45% lower); copy/blur IoU 0.8373/0.7946 for both; mean copy verification 1.8695 s vs 0.05674 s.
- `Full8`: copy/blur IoU 0.6797/0.6436; copy verification 0.02135 s.
- `bounded289` has fine maps on 1,500 total rows (intact and the two local cases) and abstains on the 3,500 global-operation rows.

I also recomputed the classifier impact directly from stored probabilities. On 500 validation images, intact accuracy/sensitivity/BA/AUC are 86.40% / 58.93% / 74.40% / 0.8454; JPEG70 is 89.20% / 39.29% / 67.39% / 0.8045. This is a sound example of why accuracy alone can mislead under class imbalance. It is **not** evidence that an embedding caused the change, and it is not a clinical endpoint.

For both studies, the raw rows/probabilities let me check arithmetic and internal pairing, but no original source images were available to replay transformations or predictions. The strongest defensible wording is “independently recomputed from returned receipts,” not “independently reproduced from pixels.”

### Moderate: the QIM extension is a negative pilot, not a demonstrated contribution

The 12-image guide-TRAIN receipt reports that guided correction changed only one image, substantially increasing its distortion, with no predicted-class changes; JPEG70 message errors are near chance. That is useful negative feasibility evidence if described honestly. It does not establish diagnostic benefit, independent-model transfer, clinical preservation, robustness, or novelty. The returned receipt has no original/received pixels for replay, so its MSE and pixel claims remain receipt-checked rather than independently image-verified.

### Moderate: the work is too broad and the public-facing scope is inconsistent

The workspace contains separable lines: (1) HMAC-LSB plus learned-localiser work, (2) detached signed/compact evidence, (3) classifier operator impact including the now-ingested T1 three-model validation return, (4) the small QIM pilot, and (5) matched EditGuard validation. These are related but not one validated end-to-end system. T1 and matched EditGuard returns are validation evidence, not confirmatory or clinical findings. The project needs one explicit thesis question and a clear map of which tracks answer it; otherwise it risks appearing as many prototypes rather than one completed study.

### Moderate: the thesis/report release is not ready on citations and permissions

The local literature file itself says that the PFF-WM citation needs full bibliographic verification and that dataset licences need formal review. This checkout has no root `LICENSE` file. ISIC 2019’s non-commercial terms and required source acknowledgements must be followed, and upstream EditGuard code/checkpoint redistribution terms remain unresolved in the project notes. Do not publicly package their data/weights or claim a complete related-work review until the exact permissions and references are checked. I did not independently verify the papers online in this review.

### Moderate: the Git branch does not yet contain the whole deliverable set

`git status --short` shows many central source additions, notebooks, evidence artifacts, and deliverables as untracked in this checkout. They are present in the shared workspace, but a normal GitHub clone/PR from the branch would omit untracked files. Before claiming the branch is a complete reproducibility package, explicitly curate and commit the intended source/evidence/docs (excluding caches, data and model weights), then verify a clean clone can run the documented checks.

## Strengths worth preserving

- Good separation between strict integrity outcomes and learned scores; learned decisions do not silently override a failed MAC.
- Clear threat-model caveats around key secrecy, version freshness, lossy operations and clinical limits.
- Negative experiments are retained instead of being hidden; the QIM stop-gate is appropriately conservative.
- The sidecar/compact-evidence validation and classifier-impact returns contain row-level data that allowed me to independently recompute key metrics.
- Strong software test discipline and numerous fail-closed input/checkpoint checks. This supports confidence in plumbing, not in efficacy.

## Required next actions, in order

1. **Freeze claims, not just slides.** Relabel `isic-study-v2` test metrics as already-accessed development/prototype results. Locate the exact old split membership from the original archive/logs, or state that membership cannot be recovered. Reconcile overlap with the later 500-image cohort before describing that holdout as untouched.
2. **Make test access impossible by default.** Split the runner into validation/development and a distinct, explicitly authorised final-evaluation command; no baseline/model/test scoring in default study runs. Hash and approve the evaluation protocol before opening any genuinely fresh holdout.
3. **Repair provenance.** Verify receipt timestamps against original run records; as of 2026-10-19, the 2026-10-20 and 2026-11-03 receipt timestamps are future-dated and unauthenticated. Preserve source bundles for cited commits and label ISIC2018 separately. Historical test membership is unrecoverable from retained evidence; do not create a synthetic replacement and represent it as original.
4. **Choose the primary contribution.** Either present a careful comparison of integrity-evidence cost/coverage and diagnostic operator effects, or complete a rigorous learned-localisation study against classical block expansion and image-only controls. Do not market the current code combination as a novel method without that discriminating result.
5. **For a learned-method claim:** use a defensible lesion/patient grouping policy, source-verified inputs, prespecified thresholds and metrics, multiple training seeds, a frozen independent evaluation, and a complete auditable per-image return. The T1 and matched EditGuard records add validation comparisons, but receipt audits cannot repair test contamination or provenance gaps and do not substitute for source-pixel/model replay.
6. **Finish bibliography, licensing, authorship and reproducibility packaging.** Include a project licence, dependency/model/data attribution, exact non-commercial restrictions, and one authoritative protocol/version table. Keep patient images and model weights out of public links unless explicitly permitted.

## Final assessment

**Current status: promising and unusually self-critical prototype; not yet a trustworthy final research result or a demonstrated novel/clinical system.** The best near-term presentation is a bounded engineering comparison with transparent negative findings, after the test/provenance contradictions are resolved. If presented as “a new clinically reliable AI medical-image tamper detector,” the evidence does not support it. If presented as “an audited prototype study of integrity/localisation tradeoffs, with a limited classifier-impact analysis and a negative embedding feasibility pilot,” it can be credible—provided the test split history and timestamp/source provenance questions are handled openly.
```

### 13. README.md

**Role:** Repository landing page

````text
# Medical Image Tamper Detection

Research prototype for image integrity and tamper localisation, targeting ISIC2019.

**Research focus:** We study exact image integrity, tamper localization, and class-sensitive diagnostic-model impact as separate contracts in dermoscopic imaging. We do not assume that watermarking or deep learning is superior; comparisons must account for their different distortion, evidence, storage, and trust costs.

## Scope and experimental protocol

Our repository contains multiple study lines and their outcomes are not pooled as one benchmark. We evaluated an adapted ISIC 2019 cohort and checked returned validation records against the declared row/protocol structure; we did not rerun source-image transformations or classifier inference locally. A separate historical `isic-study-v2` run evaluated 1,000 test images, but its exact IDs cannot be recovered from retained evidence. Overlap with the later 500-image cohort is unknown, so we exclude that holdout from confirmatory claims and further outcome analysis. The recovery decision and claim limits are in [`deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`](deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md), [`deliverables/GATE0_PROVENANCE_STATUS.md`](deliverables/GATE0_PROVENANCE_STATUS.md), and [`deliverables/CLAIMS_EVIDENCE_LIMITS.md`](deliverables/CLAIMS_EVIDENCE_LIMITS.md).

Historical PH2 work is also present in this repository; we do not describe PH2 as an unused external test cohort until its earlier membership/protocol history is reconciled.

**Project structure:** the repository contains a protocol-v2 embedded RGB-HMAC prototype, detached signed-evidence/localization studies, and separate classifier/embedding experiments. Their protocols and evidence levels differ; refer to the consolidated project overview before combining or comparing their results.

## Start here

**Authoritative project state (as of 2026-10-19 UTC):** [Project master context](PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md). It reconciles the separate experimental strands, evidence levels, critical limits, document map, and safe next steps. Detailed tables and caveats are in [Project overview](deliverables/PROJECT_FULL_OVERVIEW_AND_GAPS.md), [Claims/evidence limits](deliverables/CLAIMS_EVIDENCE_LIMITS.md), and the [T1 validation report](deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md).

**T1 status:** the fixed 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation multi-architecture study was returned and ingested. Results are validation-only; no TEST data were accessed. See the T1 report for metrics, audit checks, and limitations. This T1 classifier notebook is separate from the RGB-HMAC protocol-v2 runner below.

**Historical transition record:** the earlier mid-review/poster concerned a different ISIC 2018 fragile-watermarking study. Its reported figures are archived with limitations and unresolved date/provenance warnings in [the transition record](deliverables/MIDREVIEW_POSTER_TRANSITION_RECORD.md); do not mix them with current T1 results.

- [Current safe run instructions and boundaries](RUNNING.md)
- [Protocol-v2 method specification](PROTOCOL_V2.md) — separate research line; its historical stage plan is not authorization to access any holdout.
- [Open technical questions](docs/DESIGN_REVIEW.md#5-open-questions)
- [References and bibliography caveats](docs/LITERATURE.md)
- [Complete narrative compilation](deliverables/PROJECT_COMPLETE_DOCUMENT_COMPILATION.md) (documentation-only compilation).
- [Curated full project context/source bundle](deliverables/PROJECT_CONTEXT_BUNDLE_2026-10-19.zip) with included-file hashes and documented exclusions; not a raw-data or weights package. See [package guide](deliverables/PROJECT_CONTEXT_PACKAGE_README.md).

The current fragile verifier uses block-local RGB HMAC tags. The main learned
model estimates pixel localisation; the attention-enhanced model and seven
constituent controls are experiments, not demonstrated improvements.

## Simple current-method demonstration

**Protect → alter → verify**, on CPU without a neural checkpoint:

```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
python scripts/showcase.py --synthetic --out /tmp/keyswm-showcase
# Open /tmp/keyswm-showcase/index.html in your browser.
```

For your authorised, de-identified ISIC image, replace `--synthetic` with
`--image /outside/repo/ISIC.jpg --resize 512`. Resizing is explicit, recorded
preprocessing, not preservation of the original medical acquisition. Output
pixels must stay outside this repository. The HTML works offline, shows all
eight controlled scenarios, and links the numerical receipt and protected PNG.

This is the **classical RGB authentication path**, not a demonstration of learned
pixel segmentation. Red overlays mean failed 8×8 blocks. The measured pixel
change mask is shown separately and is never supplied to verification. Wrong-key,
wrong-identifier and JPEG examples explain why failure does not establish intent.
`scripts/demo.py` is historical and does not demonstrate this current method.

## Answering the signature objection with a measured comparison

```sh
python scripts/compare_integrity.py --synthetic --n 4 --out /tmp/integrity-tradeoffs.json
# Open /tmp/integrity-tradeoffs.html; retain the matching JSON receipt.
```

Use `--image /outside/repo/prepared-RGB.png` for one authorised image, or
`--data /outside/repo/images --n 4` for an explicitly nonrepresentative convenience
subset. Inputs are not silently resized. This is separate from the frozen ISIC
learned-model study and does not evaluate a trained model.

| Mechanism | Alters pixels? | Localises changes? | Required external trust/evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Whole-image Ed25519 signature | No | No map | Trusted public key, version ID, signed evidence |
| Signed tile-hash manifest | No | Failed 8×8 tiles | Trusted public key, version ID, signed manifest |
| Embedded HMAC watermark | Yes | Failed 8×8 blocks | Shared secret key, trusted version ID |

Signatures can localise when a tile manifest is signed. This baseline must not be
omitted to make watermarking look necessary. No blockchain is required for these
checks; freshness and key trust still need an external workflow. Reported sidecar sizes exclude key
distribution, certificates and trusted version-registry overhead for all methods. Invalid/missing
signed evidence means `CANNOT_AUTHENTICATE`, not an invented tamper map. Our
signature baseline covers decoded RGB, not DICOM metadata or a DICOM profile.

The candidate learning contribution is **better sub-block localisation than block
expansion**, compared against an image-only model. This is not established. A high
image detection score, extra attention layers or a favourable whole-image JPEG
mask cannot substitute for small-edit pixel localisation.

## Status

Targeted CPU checks cover authentication edits, checkpoint recovery and synthetic
training of all variants. A tiny ISIC T4 wiring run has executed, but learned
localisation failed there. Full ISIC three-seed results, strict GPU reproducibility
and clinical validity are not established. Raw check outputs are in `deliverables/*.json`;
historical performance receipts remain in `results/`. Neither is a paper-parity
claim. See each output's source fingerprint and scope before using its numbers.

## Files

- `src/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`: implementation, runners and tests.
- `notebooks/keyswm_isic2019.ipynb`: generated standalone Kaggle notebook.
- `deliverables/`: portable ZIPs, notebook copy and machine-readable checks.
- `project-context/`: concise project context and historical facts.

Use a fresh `isic2019-rgb-auth-v2` study. Old checkpoints are incompatible.
Protected images require lossless storage. Public benchmark keys are not
production secrets; learned predictions cannot override failed authentication.

Historical `src/pipeline.py` and `scripts/bench.py` retain the earlier classical
implementation through `legacy_carriers`/`legacy_metrics`; they are not v2 runners.
````

### 14. PROTOCOL_V2.md

**Role:** RGB-HMAC protocol specification  
**Status note:** Separate study line; not the detached Ed25519 system.  
**Source SHA-256:** `86cf3715cf30b598c0bd416e731b44612aa74aa7238d686abbbe4be97cba53a1` (10668 bytes)

````text
# Protocol amendment: ISIC RGB authentication v2

**New protocol identifier:** `isic-rgb-auth-v2`  
**New study directory:** `isic2019-rgb-auth-v2`  
**Scheme:** `rgb-lsb-hmac-sha256-192-v1`

This is a method change, not a repaired measurement of the old Haar-only fragile
carrier. All earlier weights/caches/results are incompatible. Source/data/settings
fingerprints reject incompatible resumes. Keep earlier negative results as history.
No published-paper reproduction/parity claim is made.

## Full-RGB fragile authentication

For each 8×8 RGB uint8 block:

1. Clear all 192 sample low bits to obtain canonical content. This is what can
   be recovered exactly after embedding; no approximate wavelet/DC code is used.
2. HMAC-SHA256 authenticates a versioned domain, trusted unsigned-64-bit image/
   version identifier, full image dimensions, block row/column and every canonical
   RGB sample. Truncate to 24 bytes (192 bits).
3. Embed all 192 tag bits in the RGB sample low bits, one bit per sample.
4. Verification reconstructs the canonical content, recomputes the HMAC and
   checks every received tag bit. `authentication.failures` is block-local;
   `authentication.verify` rejects if **any** block fails. It is independent of
   neural scores/count boundaries and cannot be overridden by a neural prediction.

The default `authenticated` profile writes the semi-robust DCT carrier first,
then this RGB fragile tag. `authenticated-single` omits the DCT carrier.
`single` and `dual` are explicitly **legacy Haar profiles**, retained only for
historical regressions; they still have the documented brightness/colour failures.
The low-level historical `evidence.embed/read` APIs are legacy too.

The new fragile carrier replaces—not supplements authentication with—the old
periodic Haar tag. In the existing 13-channel evidence tensor, `E` is now binary
RGB authentication failure. The remaining nine wavelet channels are diagnostic
features; the last three are DCT features. Their legacy PCG/selector construction
is not the security basis. Full-tensor channel count is unchanged but meaning is
versioned: old checkpoints MUST NOT be reused.

### Explicit limits

- Lossless storage/transport is required: save protected images as PNG or another
  exact RGB representation. JPEG, resizing and recompression break integrity and
  are labelled changed-pixel tamper events in this research protocol, not evidence
  of malicious intent. The semi-robust carrier cannot override a failed RGB MAC.
- Low-bit replacement is irreversible and changes original samples. Fidelity is
  measured, not assumed; no diagnostic safety claim follows from PSNR.
- HMAC forgery/collision resistance is a cryptographic assumption, not a proof
  that finite tests detect every possible attack. This is not a formal security
  audit, encryption, robust authentication through codecs, or clinical approval.
- A secret, high-entropy key is required for adversarial use. Benchmark keys in
  source are public and **not production secrets**; a key holder can re-tag edits.
- The receiver must obtain the expected image/version identifier and geometry
  context from a trusted workflow. A previously valid block at the same address
  with the same identifier/key can be replayed. Freshness requires an externally
  trusted unique version identifier/registry, not something guessed from pixels.
- The supported input is 8-bit RGB with dimensions divisible by 8. No DICOM,
  16-bit or clinical deployment capability is claimed.

## Labels and degradation

Pixel truth remains exact before/after byte differences. Block truth now marks
**any changed pixel**, including a one-bit edit; the previous >5% occupancy rule
is retired. Both image and block labels therefore include sparse edits. Cross-head consistency
uses max pixel probability as a block-presence surrogate, not mean pixel occupancy;
the old average was incompatible with an any-pixel block target.

Default sharpness is mean Sobel gradient magnitude divided by
`4*255*sqrt(2)`, a fixed analytic upper bound for uint8 luma with 3×3 Sobel kernels.
It varies with input and uses no VAL/TEST or corpus-fitted statistic. An explicitly
supplied finite positive reference still supports deliberate alternative studies;
it is not used by this frozen default recipe.

## Matched classical/learned operating-point selection

Both use the same validation objective: balanced image accuracy, then tampered
**pixel** IoU, then lower threshold/count for deterministic ties. The common grid
is thresholds .05…2.00 in .05 steps and counts 0,1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256. Classical
fusion also prefers smaller semi-carrier weight on ties. Model epoch selection
uses validation tampered-pixel IoU. No test label is used for selection. Learned pixel masks estimate sub-block
boundaries; they are not cryptographic pixel-level certificates.

The trained model's count prediction and empirical abstention remain experimental
outputs; they are NOT permission to assert that an image with a failed MAC is
verified. Receipts separately report strict authentication counts and whether they
match measured edit labels. Learned models may still be worse than the classical
baseline. No improvement or probability calibration is presumed.

## Runnable constituent removals

All variants start from identical shared weights and consume the **same cached
protected images and measured masks**:

| Variant argument | Actual removed computation/input |
|---|---|
| `without-ae` | AE pretraining and residual feature; zero placeholder |
| `without-saliency` | Learned multi-scale saliency path; neutral constant input |
| `without-degradation` | All four degradation conditioning inputs |
| `without-pixel` | Pixel decoder/head; block output upsampling, block/class losses only |
| `without-fragile` | First ten fragile/diagnostic feature channels before AE and localiser |
| `without-semi` | Three semi-robust feature channels before AE and localiser |
| `rgb-only` | All watermark features and AE residual; image-derived paths remain |

Removed modules can remain frozen in the checkpoint for exact shared initialization
and storage compatibility; they are not run. Both stored and trainable parameter
counts are reported. Feature removals do not pretend to remove embedding: physically
removing a carrier would change protected pixels and attack truth and requires a
separately declared protocol, not a falsely strict paired comparison.

Enable/freeze the full model list from the first job:

```sh
python scripts/run_suite.py --data <images> --metadata <labels.csv> \
  --out <new-study> --cache <outside-repo-cache> --device cuda \
  --seeds 0 --ablations --execute-configs C8 --probes
```

Later jobs use the **same command/settings/source**, `--resume`, and
`--execute-configs without-ae` (or another registered removal). Internal `C8`
means the Main watermark-verification model; `C12` means Attention-enhanced model.
Use seeds 0,1,2 and the planned main-model heldout run. These are planned experiments,
not completed results. The summary expects ablations when `--ablations` was frozen
in `study.json` and verifies their strict paired comparisons against the main model.

In the notebook choose `STAGE='ablation'` and `ABLATION='<variant>'`, one saved job
at a time. Main/attention/check/heldout stages freeze the same full model list.
The initial check executes only the two principal models; the CPU integration
validator can additionally exercise all seven removals.

## Reproduce engineering checks

```sh
python -m pytest -q
python scripts/review_from_source.py --exercise-crash --out /tmp/review-v2.json
python scripts/validate_rgb_auth.py --images 16 --out /tmp/auth-synthetic.json
python scripts/validate_rgb_auth.py --data <local-jpegs> --images 32 --out /tmp/auth-images.json
python scripts/validate_notebook.py --execute-wiring --exercise-ablations
```

Temporary paths may be changed for Windows. These checks do not replace the full
ISIC three-seed/ablation/heldout study, GPU preflight, or independent security review.

## Separate engineering comparison: integrity-tradeoffs-v1

This profile does **not** change the frozen ISIC learned study, its loss weights,
thresholds, partitions or existing results. `scripts/compare_integrity.py` compares
whole-image Ed25519 signing, Ed25519-signed per-tile SHA-256 digests and embedded
RGB HMAC. Only the embedded method alters the reference pixels. The other two
are standard non-embedding baselines, not new cryptographic contributions.

All methods bind dimensions and an externally expected version identifier. Tile
hashes also bind coordinates. The signed envelope authenticates all digests and
schema fields using a trusted externally supplied public key. An invalid signature,
wrong identifier/geometry or missing evidence returns `CANNOT_AUTHENTICATE` with
no localisation. Only valid signed evidence can support a received-pixel integrity
verdict. Whole-image signatures never manufacture a localisation mask.

Matched operators/coordinates do not imply identical ground truth: each method's
received RGB is differenced against its own reference. Context/evidence failures
are reported separately from changed-pixel cases. Empty-union localisation IoU is
null, not an inflated perfect score. Report block confusion counts and expanded-
block pixel IoU separately; a one-pixel edit can have perfect block IoU and only
1/64 pixel IoU. Stratify local edits and global JPEG changes instead of hiding
small-edit failures inside a pooled accuracy. Single-execution timings, encoded
sidecar size, PNG size, embedded payload bits and distortion describe engineering
trade-offs, not a statistically stable runtime or security ranking. JSON sidecar
size is representation-dependent and is not a storage lower bound.

The baselines authenticate 8-bit decoded RGB only. They do not sign file metadata,
implement DICOM signatures, PKI, key rotation, freshness, revocation or clinical
validation. Public benchmark signing seeds are not deployment credentials.
An old valid image/manifest pair can be replayed if an old expected version ID is
accepted. Neither this comparison nor a ledger hash alone solves that workflow.

Before a revised learned study: obtain actual ISIC TRAIN-only diagnostic evidence;
freeze any new localisation operating-point rule before evaluation; compare block
expansion, image-only and authentication-guided localisation on the same protected
samples. Do not reuse the seven test images for tuning or silently call the old
shared-threshold failure a successful result. Medical/lesion relevance, clinical
fidelity and the candidate contribution remain open questions.
````

````text
# Running and reproducing the project

**Current status (2026-10-19 UTC):** T1 multi-architecture classifier validation has been returned and ingested. Its scope is the fixed 1,500 TRAIN / 500 validation cohort only. No TEST pixels, labels, predictions, or outcomes were accessed in T1. See [`deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`](deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md).

> **Critical boundary:** this repository contains several distinct runners and cohorts. Do not treat this guide, `PROTOCOL_V2.md`, the T1 Kaggle notebook, historical receipts, or a plan as interchangeable. The historical `isic-study-v2` run accessed 1,000 test records; its exact membership is unrecoverable from retained evidence. The later 500-image holdout is non-confirmatory development evidence, and must not be opened for more outcomes or described as untouched. A new confirmatory study requires a genuinely new, provenance-audited cohort and a frozen protocol. PH2 also has prior project use and is not a clean confirmatory external cohort.

## What is safe to run now

### Synthetic CPU demonstration

```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
python scripts/showcase.py --synthetic --out /tmp/keyswm-showcase
# Open /tmp/keyswm-showcase/index.html.
```

This demonstrates the classical RGB authentication path, not learned pixel segmentation, and synthetic examples are not medical-image performance evidence. Use `--image` only with an authorized image stored outside the repository; resizing is explicit preprocessing and is not preservation of original acquisition pixels.

### Tests and local plumbing

The project includes CPU tests and optional Torch-backed tests. Run the test suite only in an environment with the dependencies specified in `requirements-test.txt`; report exact environment and failures. Unit tests establish code-path assertions, not scientific effectiveness or model performance.

```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements-test.txt
python -m pytest -q
python scripts/preflight.py
```

Training extras are separate. Do not install or upgrade the managed Kaggle Torch/CUDA stack for a notebook execution without checking that notebook's pinned requirements and protocol.

## T1 — completed validation run

- Notebook: `deliverables/kaggle_t1_multi_arch_validation.ipynb`.
- Returned archive: `google_drive/t1-results-v1.zip` in the connected workspace; it includes visual case panels and should not be redistributed without a separate image/licensing review.
- Tables: `deliverables/tables/table_multi_arch_diagnostic_drift.csv` and `deliverables/tables/table_editguard_ssim_and_multi_arch.csv`.
- Report: `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`.
- Ingestion command for a **new** archive/output location:

```sh
python scripts/ingest_t1_multi_arch.py --results-zip /path/to/t1-results.zip --out /path/to/new-output
```

The ingester is intentionally fail-closed and refuses to overwrite existing result tables. It checks returned predictions and integrity/protocol identity against retained validation references. Ingestion is not an independent replay from source pixels/models. Do not add TEST access or reinterpret these results as an independent test.

## Separate protocol-v2 line

`PROTOCOL_V2.md`, `notebooks/keyswm_isic2019.ipynb`, and `deliverables/keyswm_isic2019.ipynb` describe a distinct fragile RGB-HMAC / learned-localization project line. Existing instructions for its planned full multi-seed or `heldout` stages are historical plan material, not a current execution approval. A previous small TRAIN-only diagnostic and a separate incomplete real-image sanity trace exist; neither establishes complete ISIC performance. Do not point this runner at a holdout or use the old `heldout` stage on the current cohort.

Safe synthetic runner checks are permitted. For any future TRAIN/validation-only diagnostic, follow the exact runner's preflight and keep all inputs/caches outside Git. Do not restore a prior protocol checkpoint into a different protocol or study.

## Inputs, privacy, and release boundaries

- Do not put original medical-image pixels, restricted model weights, private keys, credentials, or unreviewed patient metadata in Git or public Drive shares.
- The context bundle is a documentation/source/evidence snapshot; it intentionally omits raw image inputs, model weights, credentials, and some image-bearing result archives.
- Dataset counts and hashes do not prove source authenticity, patient independence, or institutional representativeness. Lesion-disjoint splitting does not establish patient-level independence.
- Verify dataset, upstream code, checkpoint, and paper licences before public release or redistribution. The repository literature file still flags bibliography verification needs.
- A classifier output is a research probe, not a clinical decision. Integrity failure is a content mismatch, not proof of malicious intent.

## Current analysis map

| Workstream | Safe entry point | Status / evidence limit |
|---|---|---|
| T1 multi-architecture classifier impact | `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` | Completed on TRAIN/validation only; validation-selected models; no confirmatory inference. |
| Detached integrity/localization | `deliverables/PROJECT_FULL_OVERVIEW_AND_GAPS.md` and referenced receipts | Validation receipts audited; no full original-pixel replay in this runtime. |
| Matched EditGuard | `deliverables/MATCHED_EDITGUARD_VALIDATION_RETURN_REVIEW.md` | Return structure/arithmetic/signatures checked; no source-pixel/model replay or issuer identity proof. |
| Protocol-v2 RGB-HMAC/learned localization | `PROTOCOL_V2.md` | Separate protocol; full multi-seed study and confirmatory evaluation not established. |
| PH2 | `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`, project master context | Prior project use and incomplete lineage; not clean confirmatory external evidence. |

For current claims and remaining blockers, always start at [`PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md`](PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md).
````

```text
# Reference starting points

These are research references, not evidence that this implementation reproduces
or matches them. Verify author lists, publication details and dataset licences
before a formal submission. No cross-paper score table is maintained here.

- Yeung and Mintzer, *An invisible watermarking technique for image verification*, ICIP, 1997.
- Wong, *A public key watermark for image verification and authentication*, ICIP, 1998.
- Fridrich, *Security of fragile authentication watermarks with localization*, SPIE, 2002.
- Celik et al., *Hierarchical watermarking for secure image authentication with localization*, IEEE Transactions on Image Processing, 2002.
- PFF-WM, Khaldi et al., 2026 — reference architecture; full bibliographic details require verification.
- WAM — https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07231
- Zhang et al., *OmniGuard: Hybrid Manipulation Localization via Augmented Versatile Deep Image Watermarking*, CVPR 2025, pp. 3008–3018.
  https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2025/html/Zhang_OmniGuard_Hybrid_Manipulation_Localization_via_Augmented_Versatile_Deep_Image_Watermarking_CVPR_2025_paper.html
- ISIC 2019 — cite the authorised dataset source and applicable licence when reporting results.

The current RGB HMAC fragile carrier is an explicit method amendment. It must not
be described as an exact PFF-WM reproduction. Compare only matched protocols,
mask definitions, sampling, model-selection rules and metric granularity.

## Scope check for the simplified demonstration

- Zhang et al., *EditGuard: Versatile Image Watermarking for Tamper Localization
  and Copyright Protection*, CVPR 2024, pp. 11964–11974.
  [Proceedings and abstract](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2024/html/Zhang_EditGuard_Versatile_Image_Watermarking_for_Tamper_Localization_and_Copyright_Protection_CVPR_2024_paper.html).
  Proactive watermark-based tamper localisation is already prior art.
- Sander et al., *Watermark Anything With Localized Messages*, ICLR 2025.
  [Proceedings and abstract](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/c5ee1911dffe4886367eee7fca314235-Abstract-Conference.html).
  Learned localisation of watermarked regions is also prior art, not a novelty
  established by adding a segmentation network here.

These papers are related work, not matched medical-image benchmark baselines.
The simplified CPU showcase contributes an inspectable implementation and evidence
presentation, not a new cryptographic construction or proven research novelty.
A possible research question remains whether verified-block evidence improves
small-edit pixel localisation over block expansion on matched ISIC splits. It is
unanswered; the current failed learned results cannot support a superiority claim.

## Standards and strongest non-embedding controls

- DICOM PS3.15, Annex C, Digital Signature Profiles:
  https://dicom.nema.org/medical/dicom/current/output/html/part15.html
  The standard already describes signatures for pixel-data integrity. Our RGB
  Ed25519 sidecars do **not** implement those DICOM profiles.
- Josefsson and Liusvaara, RFC 8032 (2017), EdDSA:
  https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc8032/
  Use the cryptography library rather than implementing curve arithmetic; the
  regression suite includes RFC test vector 1. This is not a novel primitive.
- *A Non-Embedding Watermarking Framework Using MSB-Driven Reference Mapping for
  Distortion-Free Medical Image Authentication*, Electronics, DOI:
  https://doi.org/10.3390/electronics15010007
  Related non-embedding medical-image authentication exists. This reference is not
  evidence for that paper's clinical/compliance claims or a matched score baseline.

The defensible research gap must be established by matched experiments, not the
assertion that signatures cannot localise or that combining existing modules is
novel. Digital signatures protect integrity; watermarking is an alternative
packaging/trust trade-off. An external ledger does not itself supply pixel-level
localisation. Image-only versus authentication-guided learning remains unevaluated.

## Verified task distinctions for the next experiment

| Work | Verified scope | Consequence for our claim |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WAM, ICLR 2025 | Watermarked-region segmentation and localised message decoding | Segmentation plus watermarking is not itself new; watermark presence is not our changed-RGB-pixel target. |
| OmniGuard, CVPR 2025 | Proactive/passive localisation with degradation-aware extraction | Adding degradation inputs or a hybrid network is not a sufficient novelty claim. |
| DICOM PS3.15 | Signature profiles for pixel-data integrity | Do not claim signatures cannot authenticate medical images. Our RGB sidecars are not a DICOM implementation. |

The next decision is empirical: first establish trainability, then compare identical
image-only and authentication-guided networks against block expansion. Keep strict
lossless integrity separate from robustness-to-compression tasks. No evidence yet
shows our candidate guidance is novel, generalises, or adds a useful benefit.
```

```text
# Design notes

> **Current status overlay — 2026-10-19 UTC:** this file is a long, partially append-only protocol-v2 design/experiment log, not the project-wide status source. T1 is a separate classifier study completed on TRAIN/validation only; see `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. Follow `PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md` for cross-strand state. Any later-dated receipt timestamps remain unauthenticated.

## 1. Current method

[Protocol v2](../PROTOCOL_V2.md) defines the RGB HMAC carrier, evidence channels,
labels, decision fitting and matched constituent removals. Code and runtime
contracts determine the executed settings.

## 2. Retired design

Legacy Haar profiles have demonstrated brightness-period and luma-preserving
colour collisions. They remain available only for labelled historical probes.
The default profile replaces that fragile carrier; old results are not v2 results.
Original check outputs remain in `deliverables/verification-evidence/` as JSON.

## 3. Checks

Run the tests and source-first probes in [RUNNING.md](../RUNNING.md). Authentication,
synthetic training and CPU recovery checks are not clinical or full-study evidence.
Read the source fingerprint on saved outputs; do not relabel old checks.

## 4. Reporting

Keep negative outcomes. Distinguish strict block authentication, statistical pixel
localisation and experimental learned/count/abstention predictions. No matched
paper comparison, adaptive-attack resistance or clinical approval is established.

## 5. Open questions

- Independent security/composition review of the RGB carrier and its key handling.
- Deployment trust and freshness: secret keys, expected image/version identifiers,
  and prevention of valid old-block replay require an external trusted workflow.
- Full ISIC three-seed, constituent and held-out results, including negative
  effects, key-change behaviour and empirical selective-risk gates.
- Whether physical carrier-removal experiments are required beyond feature
  removals; changed protected pixels need a separately declared evaluation.
- T4 sanity execution is now observed. Strict CUDA attention backward, GPU
  recovery, full-profile runtime and VRAM remain unverified.
- Why localisation and block-count operating points diverge: the 20-epoch
  synthetic TRAIN-only diagnostic still has zero pixel IoU at the shared fitted
  boundary. Whether the models can memorise actual ISIC training images is open;
  do not change the frozen decision rule or tune on the seven test images.
- Authorised complete inputs, patient/lesion identifiers and group independence.
- Clinical preservation, novelty and literature parity; fidelity alone proves none.

- Whether embedded authentication has an application advantage over signed tile
  manifests after accounting for zero-distortion alternatives, external-evidence
  storage and asymmetric versus shared-secret trust; no preferred winner assumed.
- Whether learning improves sub-block localisation over block expansion and an
  image-only control. Digital signatures, tile hashing and segmentation are prior
  art; a new research contribution has not yet been demonstrated.

- Distinguish learned authentication guidance from trivial post-masking: a future
  revised study needs an image-only network with the same final authentication
  mask, not only an ungated image-only control. This amendment is not yet run.

## Direct2026 literature check: PFF-WM (read publisher method/results)

Primary: Khaldi et al., “Explainable and proactive fragile watermarking for medical
Deepfake detection”, Intelligent Systems with Applications31(2026)200684,
DOI10.1016/j.iswa.2026.200684.
https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2667305326000591
Read publisher sections3.1–3.6,4.1–4.8,Tables2–13 and data availability. Not just
abstract or inherited summary. Publisher data availability: “Data will be made
available on request.” Targeted title/PFF-WM/GitHub search did not locate verified
author code/weights. This is not proof none exists. No author implementation run.

Important novelty restriction: DWT fragile + attention-guided DCT reference,
authentic-only stacked AE, lightweight refinement and classifier-gradient clinical
explanation are already claimed here. We cannot call that combination our novelty.
Paper3.4 explicitly uses RefineNet on E,A,Downsample(I); whether receiver I is original
or received needs clarification. AE authentic-only claim must be distinguished
from full pipeline3.6 detection BCE and synthetic attack/mask supervised training.

Metric attribution: Table2 accuracy97.6%,AUC.989 is CHEXPERT. Table4 IoU.832±.015 is
LiTS500manipulated images, NOT our ISIC test or an ISIC localisation target.
Section4.8.7 reports ISIC detection97.1%; exact split IDs/grouping not established
from inspected text. Section4.1.1 uses2500ISIC images,60/20/20split; not our32image
subset. Table13 full44ms is512grayscale A100 and includes explainability; no
comparison against our CPU timings. Table8 benign JPEGQ85 FPR.2%,Q70.4%,chain.7%
is a semantic-tamper decision, not exact-RGB integrity. Our JPEG fragile failure
cannot be called a robustness win against this operating contract. No cross-table
numerical superiority claim. No CheXpert/LiTS runs proposed or authorised.

Reproduction ambiguities, not allegations:
- Eq4 spatial softmax sums1; Eq16 uses A>.1. At most9locations can pass strictly,
  uniform512attention leaves empty ROI. Actual gate/normalisation needs code.
-3.2.4 gives258048available3level grayscale detail coefficients;3.3 describes
  64*65536=4194304fragile bits as subset. Feature reuse versus independent payload
  is unresolved; Table5 units64kbits differ from per-location64channels.
- Eq10 lower/upper=.15/.35*512 yields76.8..179.2; local8x8DCT u+v<=14, empty
  literal band. Need actual index scaling before implementing a reproduction.
Arithmetic checked locally; durable numeric evidence pff-wm-literature-arithmetic.json.
No claim that ambiguities disprove published experiments. Do not silently fix them
and label our implementation “official PFF-WM”.

Other relevant primary works located, not reproduced:
Arham et al., “Reversible watermarking for medical images using ROI-based tamper
localization and recovery”, Franklin Open15(2026)100545,
DOI10.1016/j.fraope.2026.100545,
https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2773186326000617 .
Publisher title/abstract/outline inspected: ROI/RONI recovery/reversibility contract,
not equivalent to our irreversible RGB HMAC. Full method still to review.
“Secure and reversible fragile watermarking for accurate authentication and tamper
localization in medical images” (2025),
https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0045790625000151 .
Publisher search abstract reports4x4DWT/DFT control; full implementation, rounding,
bitdepth, recovery and PSNR reference not verified. Reported>88dB cannot be compared
against our integerRGB before checking conventions. This further rules out claiming
small blocks alone as novelty. DeepMark DOI10.1016/j.ipm.2025.104600 located in
PFF-WM bibliography; must independently read/verify before treating as runnable SOTA.

Decision: retain paper-reported literature results separate from reproduced
EditGuard and our controls. Current differentiator is explicit exact authentication
versus semantic localisation with distortion/security trade-offs, still not proven
novel. A new method must demonstrate added value against both learned and simple
grid controls under a matched protocol; no next blind refiner training justified.

## Sparse authenticated reconciliation feasibility executed

Implemented src/sparse_refinement.py: signed fixed-size IBLT-style sketch of
(index,SHA256tilehash) entries, plus signed commitment to entire ordered leaf
vector. Receiver subtracts own sketch, peels differences and reconstructs original
leaf vector. A fine map is exposed ONLY after reconstructed commitment matches;
nonempty residual/mismatched pairs/root/over-capacity return FAILED_INTEGRITY,
fine map unavailable. Untrusted/missing/context-mismatched evidence returns
CANNOT_AUTHENTICATE. No original pixels used by verifier, no embedding distortion,
no freshness guarantee or formal security proof. Missing fine map is NOT a pass.
This prototype is a detached alternative, not an invented reason to add a watermark.

Known prior art: Eppstein/Goodrich/Uyeda/Varghese2011 Difference Digest,
https://dl.acm.org/doi/abs/10.1145/2043164.2018462 ; Goodrich/Mitzenmacher2011 IBLT,
DOI10.1109/ALLERTON.2011.6120248. Set reconciliation, XOR peeling and sparse-difference
communication are established. Signed-root composition is not asserted novel.
Focused image-specific prior-art comparison still needed before contribution claim.

## Classical bounded-error syndrome baseline implemented/executed

src/bounded_syndrome.py implements standard2tGF16power moments per16hash lanes,
Berlekamp–Massey error-location recurrence, Chien-style root search and Vandermonde
value recovery. Reconstructs original full leaf vector and checks signedSHA256root
before fine map. Uses unchanged receivedRGB, trusted publickey/version, no original
pixels at verifier. Prototype limits65535tiles,t<=64. All header/root/signature
included:124+64tbytes. Standard algebraic coding, NOT a novel error-correcting code.
<=terror correction is the established distinct-position power-syndrome model;
finite tests are not a proof, code audit or cryptographic availability guarantee.
No full pixel recovery is performed: recovers leaf digests and changedtile positions.

## Adapted adversarial operators and paired diagnostic impact (implementation only)

Implemented `adversarial_operators.py`, `adversarial_evaluation.py` and
`diagnostic_impact.py` while the classical validation notebook runs externally.
The running notebook/cohort/operators have not changed. FGSM and PGD are separate
opt-in components; no real ISIC adversarial results or classifier selected yet.

Explicit adaptation: epsilon 4 in uint8 RGB units (4/255), PGD 3 steps of 1/255,
clean start, cross-entropy ascent for untargeted labels or descent for explicitly
supplied targets. Mean/std are mandatory; no implicit resize or augmentation.
All classifier modules must be eval and parameters frozen. Uses input-only
autograd, checks finite logits/gradients and re-evaluates quantized uint8 outputs.
Records exact changed-pixel truth and measured per-image Linf. Paper author
checkpoint, objective/target policy, epsilon units, normalization and start rule
are not established; these choices do not resolve faithful-reproduction gaps.

Connected evaluation returns paired clean/received probabilities, RGB hashes,
all-image and clean-correct-only attack-success numerators/denominators. Impact
includes confusion matrices, balanced accuracy, macroF1, per-class sensitivity,
correct-to-wrong/wrong-to-correct counts and probability shifts. Paired
class-stratified image bootstrap is descriptive and conditional on class counts,
not patient-independent inference. Missing declared classes yield null balanced
accuracy/macroF1 rather than dropping those classes. No classifier training or
provenance is established by accepting probabilities/model objects.

Eleven focused tests passed: analytic gradient direction, FGSM4/PGD3 bounds,
clipping, source/parameter preservation, frozen-eval enforcement, malformed
inputs, quantized-output evaluation, hand-computed confusion matrices, complete
failure retention, missing classes and bootstrap repeatability. Full suite passed
with one pre-existing skip. No clinical safety, novel-method or SOTA result.

## Adapted classifier trainer implemented; not yet trained on ISIC

Added `src/isic_classifier_training.py` and
`scripts/train_adapted_isic_classifier.py`: DenseNet121 binary MEL/rest head,
local torchvision-compatible ImageNet1000 initialization with mandatory SHA256
and source, no download/random-initialization fallback. This is explicitly NOT
the paper's CheXpert-initialized checkpoint. Missing author checkpoint remains
unresolved. Raw official legacy-key state dicts may require documented conversion
to torchvision-compatible keys; no silently relaxed state loading.

Explicit adapted settings: prepared512 RGB, ImageNet mean/std, no augmentation,
Adam1e-5 for20epochs, batch8, train-derived inverse-frequency class weights,
argmax decision. Highest validation balanced accuracy selects checkpoint with
earliest-epoch ties; no claim of an independent validation result after selection.
Train/validation image and lesion overlap rejected, test split forbidden by data
loader, input RGB hashes rechecked on load. Exports protocol, loss/validation
history, selected validation probabilities and hashed selected checkpoint.
No real classifier weights acquired or ISIC training launched. Class weighting,
normalization, objective and initialization are adaptations, not author settings.

Four focused tests passed: actual two-epoch fixture training/checkpoint selection
with deliberately absent test pixels, mismatch/provenance rejection, initialization
hash rejection, DenseNet121 state round-trip and binary forward pass. Fixture
training initially revealed torch.__version__ TorchVersion was incompatible with
weights_only loading; serialize plain string instead, safe reload then passed.
Full suite passed before the final additional architecture test; focused four
passed afterwards. No real-data diagnostic utility or learned advantage measured.

Added checkpoint-pinned classifier-impact notebook BUILDER. It rejects absent or
placeholder hashes; the runnable deliverable is intentionally not issued until the
actual checkpoint is reviewed. Saved-output discovery and missing-preparation
recovery are implemented; tests execute decode/discovery/export cells with explicit
fixtures. No automatic approval of an unreviewed checkpoint or hidden test run.

Executed the existing seven-image synthetic TRAIN-only20epoch memorisation probe
with full epoch traces for both refiner models, plus a new shared-image-stem
objective-gradient probe. This does NOT amend the frozen study or establish

pixel maps in each. A lower TRAIN-only pixel diagnostic threshold gives mean IoU
```

### 18. docs/REVIEW_READINESS_PLAN.md
