**Source SHA-256:** `603f45fa4006969682e763d4b3b37750ea7bbeeedac1e061a62eb8aef834d90f` (17350 bytes)

```text
# Review-readiness assessment and execution priorities

**Current status: 2026-10-19 UTC.** This file retains an older planning log; later pending/running statements are historical and superseded by `PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md`, `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`, and the return reviews. T1 is now returned/ingested on TRAIN/validation only; matched EditGuard is receipt-audited; the 12-image diagnostic-correction pilot is complete and negative/limited. No TEST access is authorized. The former 2026-11-24 dated notes are future-dated relative to this status, so their timestamp assertions are not accepted.

## Executive decision

The best defensible project today is an **evidence-backed comparative study with an implemented experimental extension**, not a demonstrated new SOTA watermark. Present the measured evidence-budget tradeoff as the main completed finding; present payload-locked diagnostic correction as an implemented but limited/negative experimental result. T1 adds validation-stage multi-architecture classifier-impact evidence; it does not close the embedding-efficacy gap, establish clinical utility, or provide confirmatory testing.

A strong review needs a coherent question and fair comparisons more than additional model names. Do not hide null outcomes, retrofit a clinical motivation, or spend the final test repeatedly trying to obtain a positive result.

## One coherent research question

**What can we guarantee about image integrity, what can we localise under an evidence budget, and what diagnostic effects remain when we modify protected images?**

Three separate outputs answer different parts:

1. Signed integrity verification: exact RGB consistency with a signed reference under a trusted public key, not proof of patient identity, acquisition authenticity or freshness.
2. Localisation: exact/expanded block evidence versus learned estimates, with coverage and abstention reported separately.
3. Diagnostic impact: paired predictions and label-based performance from a frozen, imperfect research classifier; not a clinical guarantee.

The diagnostic-correction extension is **not required** for the zero-distortion signed method. It is an additional embedding experiment. Do not draw one pipeline diagram that falsely makes it part of every completed experiment.

## Current evidence and how far it supports us

| Component | Evidence available | Defensible statement | Missing before stronger claim |
|---|---|---|---|
| Compact signed evidence | 500 validation images, 15,000 records; returned archive review | 96.45% smaller than full4; matching maps on copy/blur; slower verification; no compact maps on seven global operators | Independent operator/pixel replay; final frozen test; deployment key trust |
| Learned refiners | Development failures and seven-fixture synthetic diagnostic | Current selected rule fails; attention has not solved localisation | Actual revised training and independent validation, only under a new versioned protocol |
| EditGuard | Matched validation return and T1 paired validation contrasts; receipt structure/arithmetic and supplied-key signatures audited | Operation-dependent localization and architecture-dependent classifier contrasts are reported in validation returns | Source-pixel/model replay, issuer identity, equal-cost end-to-end comparison, new confirmatory cohort |
| Original DenseNet121 | Reviewed 20-epoch return, epoch 3 selected; used in Track C and T1 comparisons | Baseline validation BA .74397, AUC .84540, MEL sensitivity .58929 | Independent prediction replay; external cohort; no test access to later holdout |
| T1 multi-architecture | Returned/ingested 500-image validation, 3 models × 10 operators plus paired EditGuard cases | DenseNet121-Aug BA .7927; ResNet50-Aug BA .7573; JPEG70 accuracy rose while MEL sensitivity fell across all three | Shared validation cohort, validation-based checkpoint selection, no independent replay or confirmatory generalization |
| Diagnostic correction | Synthetic mechanism checks and receipt-reviewed 12-image TRAIN pilot; stop-gate closed for unchanged candidate | Carrier preservation works in tested settings; pilot shows no useful demonstrated class benefit and one distortion-increasing change | Do not scale unchanged candidate; independent evaluator/generalization not established. A redesign needs new protocol and prior-art review. |

Evidence paths: `adapted-validation500-v1/analysis.json`, `adapted-classifier-return-v1/review.json`, `diagnostic-embedding-mechanism-v1.json`, and `refiner-failure-diagnostic-v3/` under `deliverables/verification-evidence/`.

## Most consequential weaknesses, ranked

### P0: Claims must match actual experiment contracts

**Risk:** Calling message recovery authentication, calling absent maps zero error, or claiming diagnostic preservation from one guide confidence.

**Action:** Put an explicit contract next to every result. A decoded payload may survive non-carrier edits. A signature detects changes but does not guarantee diagnosis or legitimate origin without key/identity infrastructure. Global exact-pixel changes are integrity violations even when visually benign. Report compact map coverage as 0/3500, not successful tolerance.

**Review-ready condition:** Every headline has evidence provenance and a scope statement; no unsupported novelty, clinical safety or universal robustness language.

### P0: The new extension needs a genuinely discriminating experiment

**Risk:** Guide KL improvement is partly guaranteed by candidate selection; it is not a useful discovery alone. Our synthetic random control ties the guided correction and the unseen model deteriorates.

**Action:** First run the bounded first-N TRAIN pilot without modifying the running notebook. Record fixed and weaker QIM, energy-matched random and guided outputs, failures, added distortion and full authentication packet cost. If the guide barely changes under fixed QIM, report a floor effect rather than enlarge the perturbation until it produces a dramatic result.

**Go/no-go:** Carrier invariants and clean recovery must hold. If the candidate produces no change, fails recovery, or buys guide agreement only with greater distortion and no advantage over random, do not headline efficacy. An independent evaluator is required before a transfer-preservation claim. A second seed of the same architecture is useful but weaker than architecture/dataset shift.

**Fairness improvement required before final claims:** Add best-of-K random candidates with the same guide-query budget and selection rule, plus a distortion-constrained comparison. Current random control is energy matched but not compute/query matched. The weaker baseline changes recovery margin and is not robustness matched. Added corrections can alter robustness under subsequent attacks even when clean carriers match.

### P0: Main method versus signed-manifest baseline

**Risk:** A standard signed full tile manifest may already be the practical winner: no image distortion, faster verification and complete coverage, at larger storage cost.

**Action:** Present a Pareto analysis, not a winner score. Compare packet bytes, signing/verification time, localisation quality conditional on availability, unconditional changed-pixel coverage, and whether an external reference is needed. Keep image dimensions, tile sizes and threat model explicit. An equal-container comparison isolates the reader; it does not establish equal cost or end-to-end superiority.

**Review-ready condition:** Include the inconvenient latency and global-coverage results beside storage savings.

### P1: Clinical probe quality and interpretation

The classifier misses 23/56 melanomas on validation. Its raw accuracy (.864) is below always-nonMEL (.888), while balanced accuracy is much better (.744 vs .5). Show all of those together. Validation selected the checkpoint: bootstrap intervals are descriptive and selection-biased.

The running notebook evaluates original-image operator effects. It does not show that the watermark caused those effects. For causal embedding attribution, compare original, watermarked, operated original and operated watermarked images using the same operator parameters. Partition images by whether clean predictions were originally correct; count wrong-to-right and right-to-wrong changes separately. This is a future protocol requirement, not a completed experiment. Do not tune thresholds after observing validation changes and then call them frozen.

### P1: Close prior-art overlap before naming a new method

Known overlap: faculty coauthored ROI/RONI DWT-SVD protection, PFF-WM saliency/explanation alignment, and MedSign pathology-aware preservation. Fixed carriers plus classifier-guided correction may be a narrower engineering formulation, but constrained optimisation and classifier-preserving watermarking require further primary-source review.

Additional search leads, **not established novelty conclusions**:
- *Adversarial Watermarking for Face Recognition*, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.16056 — search excerpt describes watermark/perturbation interaction and recognition failures; different task and adversarial objective. Full methods not reviewed here.
- *Conceptwm: A Diffusion Model Watermark for Concept Protection*, https://arxiv.org/html/2411.11688v1 — search excerpt describes fidelity-preserving perturbation modulation balancing watermarking and adversarial objectives; generated/concept setting differs. Full methods not reviewed here.

Search results are leads only, not reproduced evidence. Avoid “first”, “unique” and “novel” until a feature-by-feature primary-source comparison supports the exact claim. Do not train another architecture solely to make the system look complex.

## Execution sequence while the impact notebook runs

1. Complete this claim audit and review narrative; do not change sent notebook sources.
2. Prepare a return reviewer for the impact ZIP before inspecting outcomes: source/cohort/hash checks, exact 500-image × declared-case matrix, same labels/order, complete summary, finite probabilities and independently recomputed paired metrics. This is the next highest-value implementation task.
3. Review the returned impact evidence, including sensitivity, not only prediction agreement. Report failure/completion status explicitly.
4. Execute the bounded TRAIN embedding pilot on the existing reviewed guide when original prepared images/GPU capacity are available; no concurrent unrequested Kaggle workload.
5. Add matched-query and distortion controls before an efficacy study; obtain independent evaluator evidence.
6. Run actual matched baseline validation. Freeze all methods and reporting choices before opening the held-out test.

Do not delay review preparation waiting for a positive extension result. A well-explained negative extension is acceptable evidence; it is not proof of a useful new method.

## Suggested eight-slide technical story

1. **Problem and contracts:** authentication, localisation and diagnostic effects are distinct.
2. **Prior work:** faculty ROI/RONI paper, PFF-WM, MedSign, EditGuard and signed-manifest control; specify reproduced versus literature-only.
3. **Protocol:** cohort 1500/500/500, lesion-disjoint not proven patient-independent; frozen operator definitions and model selection.
4. **Completed tradeoff study:** evidence bytes, latency, map coverage and local IoU on the same slide. Include abstentions.
5. **Failed approaches:** refiner failure, threshold false positives and concrete lessons; no invented rescue.
6. **Implemented extension:** fixed carriers, bounded non-carrier correction, blind recovery and separate signatures. Label synthetic evidence visibly.
7. **Diagnostic evidence:** reviewed baseline first; add impact results only after ZIP review. Show unseen-guide counterexample, not merely lower guide KL.
8. **Conclusion:** demonstrated findings, candidate status, unresolved novelty, and exact next verification gates. Do not end with a generic “future work” list that implies undone experiments are completed.

## Concise answers for a critical review

**What is new?** “We have implemented a fixed-carrier, diagnostic-constrained correction prototype and controlled tests. Its useful advantage and novelty are not yet established. Our completed contribution so far is the audited comparative tradeoff study.”

**Why not use signatures alone?** “Full signed evidence is a strong zero-distortion baseline. Compact evidence saves bytes but costs latency and localisation coverage. Our results do not establish that embedding is always preferable.”

**Did your studies lead to this design?** “They motivated separating integrity from localisation and testing diagnostic constraints explicitly. They did not measure clinical harm from embedding; that motivation remains a prospective hypothesis.”

**Does high PSNR prove diagnostic preservation?** “No. Neither does guide agreement. We report label-based effects and independent-model checks where available, and do not claim clinical safety.”

**Can you claim SOTA?** “No. Published and adapted experiments differ; a matched, complete-cost comparison is still required.”

## Deliverable standard

The strongest final package contains: a one-page claims table, versioned protocols, reviewed result receipts with hashes, per-image paired data, reproducible analysis code, a brief demo of both success and failure, and a short limitations slide. Software test counts support implementation reliability only; they do not substitute for medical, security or novelty evidence.

## Implemented follow-through: impact return audit

`scripts/review_classifier_impact_return.py` now reviews the external notebook's ZIP without executing returned code. It checks ZIP membership/CRC, bundled Python source bytes against the actual sent impact notebook, cohort/preparation membership, reviewed checkpoint identity/protocol, declared operator and attack settings, the 500×12 matrix, labels, probabilities, seeds, hash/change-count consistency and attack Linf bounds. It independently recomputes all summary metrics and paired bootstrap intervals from the stored probabilities and compares them with the returned summary. It also flags clean-probability differences versus the training receipt for investigation rather than silently treating environments as identical.

This is still **receipt validation, not image/attack replay**: it cannot independently prove that claimed output pixels, transforms, or probabilities came from the declared model. Fine-grained per-row transform parameters are not regenerated. Source identity does not provide third-party trust. Eleven new tests cover malformed/missing/duplicate rows, probabilities, labels, attack bounds, forged summary values, a complete synthetic 500×12 return and altered returned source rejection. The synthetic return is a test fixture, not a new medical result.

**Historical pre-return note (superseded):** the prior draft said the notebook was still running and no return had been reviewed. That statement is no longer current. The completed Track C return review is documented immediately below; T1's separate validation return is documented in `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. Historical test counts in this plan are not a current full-suite result.

## Impact return is now reviewed (supersedes pending-impact notes above)

Downloaded Drive `1YF8N-hbbx_oQURalTj4_BYHge_ezwdja`, 1,001,846-byte ZIP; SHA256 `ab49421ea76a2d193be4d7e0cdf79f835ce2d4bc1f76e70680c298d913cc1e20`. Review passed on 500 images ×12 cases, all summary metrics/intervals recomputed. Maximum clean probability discrepancy from training receipt: 9.5367431640625e-07. Evidence: `deliverables/verification-evidence/classifier-impact-return-v1/`. Human-readable report: `deliverables/classifier-impact-validation500-review.html`.

JPEG70 drops BA 74.40%→67.39% and melanoma sensitivity 33/56→22/56, despite accuracy increasing 86.4%→89.2%. Thus raw accuracy is demonstrably misleading in this comparison. The local copy and blur surrogate cause 4 and 1 prediction flips; these are not verified lesion-removal operators. White-box adapted PGD makes 430/432 initially correct images incorrect (99.54%); recorded overall errors are 498/500. These are results of a ground-truth-label, model-gradient-access attack, not black-box robustness or embedding-effect results. This analysis used validation only; however, a historical ISIC-study-v2 test evaluation exists, and membership overlap with the later holdout is unrecoverable. The later holdout is non-confirmatory under the current disposition in `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`. Do not retrospectively claim this experiment originally motivated code implemented earlier.

The TRAIN12 embedding feasibility pilot is complete and the unchanged candidate is closed at its stop-gate; see `deliverables/DIAGNOSTIC_EMBEDDING_PILOT_REVIEW_ADDENDUM.txt`. T1 multi-architecture validation is also complete on TRAIN/validation only; see `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. Medical benefit, novelty and SOTA superiority remain unestablished.
```

```text
# Adapted finalisation roadmap — evidence-first, presentation-ready

**Status date:** 2026-10-19 UTC. **Scope:** adapt the earlier roadmap to evidence retained in this checkout. Current branch: `arena/a9fb47a3-major-project`. A former 2026-11-24 date on this note is future-dated relative to the current status and is not accepted chronology. An earlier `isic-study-v2` receipt reports 1,000 test evaluations; exact IDs are unrecoverable from retained evidence, and overlap with the later 500-image cohort is unknown. That cohort is non-confirmatory and closed to further outcome analysis. The old freeze is revoked; future confirmation requires a new provenance-audited cohort. T1 is now complete on TRAIN/validation only; see `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`.

## Executive decision

The proposed organising frame is sound: keep **integrity**, **localisation**, and **diagnostic impact** as separate contracts, compare their costs/limits, and retain failed experiments as bounded negative results. Do not claim the thesis has established an exact global Pareto frontier, causal smoothing mechanism, clinical utility, SOTA status, or publication readiness. The existing comparative work is the defensible centre; the medical QIM pilot is a limited TRAIN feasibility/stop-gate result.

**Do not spend time or compute scaling the unchanged QIM correction.** Its fixed-carrier distortion barrier and TRAIN12 result are sufficient to close that candidate, not to establish performance on unseen patients/models.

## Status of roadmap items

| Item | Status | Evidence / remaining boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Track A detached signed evidence | **Completed on VAL** | 15,000 rows, exact RGB integrity; compact method saves bytes but trades coverage/latency. Not a DICOM workflow or PKI deployment claim. |
| Track B learned localisation | **Synthetic diagnostics only** | Failure modes retained. Do not imply medical-image training/validation. |
| Track C original frozen DenseNet impact | **Completed on VAL** | 6,000 operator rows; validation is development data because checkpoint selection used VAL. Adapted model/operators, not author reproduction or clinical model. |
| T1 multi-architecture classifier validation | **Returned and ingested on TRAIN/VAL** | Three models and 10 operators; both augmented models exceed validation BA 0.75. JPEG70 accuracy rose while MEL sensitivity fell across all three; augmentation effect is architecture-dependent. No TEST access or external/confirmatory claim. See `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. |
| Track D correction | **Closed as a TRAIN stop-gate** | 12 TRAIN images (2 MEL), 72 variant rows, 720 operator-recovery entries and 72 packet signatures checked. No independent evaluator, no pixel replay, no class flips; one costly changed output. See pilot addendum. |
| Class-specific probability shift / fixed clean threshold | **Completed exploratory analysis** | `deliverables/verification-evidence/class-sensitive-drift-v2/`; plot and JSON saved. Post-hoc VAL analysis, not preregistered operating point or causal diagnosis. |
| Cohort source-range/exact-hash audit | **Completed from receipts** | 2,500 source-decoded/prepared RGB hashes unique; no cross-split exact duplicates. Source attribution uses ID-range proxies only. `pHash` not run because source pixels are absent. |
| `bounded289` capacity curve on 50 VAL images | **Blocked** | Original/prepared pixels are not in this workspace. Existing VAL rows cover the pinned operators, not a capacity sweep. Do not substitute synthetic measurements as an ISIC result. |
| `bounded289` cProfile | **Synthetic profile only** | `bounded289-cprofile-synthetic-v1.json`, random RGB and clustered 64×64 edit (256 changed 4×4 tiles); not medical or directly comparable to prior timings. Python locator/moment recovery dominated profile. |
| 10-image deterministic Track A + C replay | **Blocked** | Requires prepared RGB pixels and the validated per-image replay inputs; saved probability receipts alone cannot replay image transforms/model predictions. |
| Matched EditGuard + frozen DenseNet on VAL | **Receipt-audited validation return** | Structural, row-arithmetic, map-comparison and 500/500 Signed4 signature checks passed. No source pixels or models were replayed; see `deliverables/MATCHED_EDITGUARD_VALIDATION_RETURN_REVIEW.md`. |
| Later 500-image internal holdout | **Non-confirmatory development evidence; closed to further outcome analysis** | Earlier v2 evaluated 1,000 test images; exact IDs are unrecoverable and overlap is unknown. Recovery is closed under `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`. Do not describe as untouched or confirmatory. |
| Literature/pHash, final matrix, licensing | **Open/partial** | Literature matrix needs final cited-source review; pHash needs pixels; master table must mark unmeasured fields N/A/pending. Confirm ISIC and upstream licensing before redistribution. |

### Newly completed exploratory VAL analysis

A single probability threshold was selected from intact VAL probabilities to target at least 90% empirical nonMEL specificity, then held fixed for all operations. It is a post-hoc descriptive operating point. Threshold is **0.506985545158**; actual intact specificity is **90.09%** due to discrete counts. At that threshold, MEL sensitivity is intact **58.93%**, JPEG70 **39.29%**, JPEG85 **51.79%**, Gaussian05 **48.21%**, resize90-restore **50.00%**, contrast110 **62.50%**. Actual specificity changes by operation and is reported, not refitted.

Mean change in MEL probability for true MEL / true nonMEL: JPEG70 **−0.11360 / −0.03939**; Gaussian05 **−0.06967 / −0.03461**; JPEG85 **−0.06573 / −0.02538**. These are scores from the same adapted classifier, not evidence that a specific feature was destroyed or that calibration caused the shift. The paired class-conditional bootstrap intervals are in `analysis.json`; they are image-level, conditional on VAL class counts.

Source-range proxy counts (not an authoritative source label):

| Split | 0012–0014 ID range | 0024–0034 ID range | 0053–0073 ID range |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| TRAIN | 102 (6.8%) | 963 (64.2%) | 435 (29.0%) |
| VAL | 38 (7.6%) | 314 (62.8%) | 148 (29.6%) |
| TEST | 36 (7.2%) | 291 (58.2%) | 173 (34.6%) |

This confirms representation across approximate acquisition ID families, not balance of institutions or acquisition conditions. There are **zero exact source-decoded RGB hash duplicates across the three splits** in the preparation receipts. Perceptual-hash similarity and patient identity remain unverified.

## Corrected central thesis and title

**Working thesis:** *An audited comparison of detached exact-integrity evidence, compact tamper localisation, and class-sensitive diagnostic effects on an adapted ISIC2019 cohort, with a bounded feasibility analysis of a diagnostic-constrained embedding extension.*

Possible concise title: **“Integrity, Tamper Localisation, and Diagnostic Impact in Dermoscopic Image Protection: An Audited Comparative Study.”** This does not imply all methods were compared at equal storage/compute, nor that the adaptation is a clinical benchmark.

## Presentation-safe findings (with claim boundaries)

1. **Exact evidence is a systems tradeoff, not a universal winner.** In the VAL protocol, bounded289 reduces additional storage versus full4 while exact RGB remains unchanged; it gives useful fine maps for tested local edits but abstains on global changes. Quantify latency and abstention beside storage. State that these are pinned deterministic operators and a Python implementation, not a universal optimum.
2. **Accuracy alone concealed class-specific validation changes.** JPEG70 accuracy rose from 86.4% to 89.2% while MEL sensitivity fell from 58.93% to 39.29% and balanced accuracy fell by 7.01 points. The exploratory frozen-threshold analysis confirms lower MEL scores/sensitivity for several operators. Do not say the experiment proves a biological or feature-level mechanism.
3. **The QIM extension is a negative feasibility result.** On 12 already-classified guide-TRAIN images, all variants kept the same predicted classes; guided correction changed 1/12 images and sharply raised MSE there. The strict fixed-carrier gate returned baseline output. JPEG70 BER is around 50%. This closes the candidate, not the broader field of diagnostic-aware robust watermarking.

## Immediate path to presentation

1. **Use existing validation evidence.** Build slides around the three findings above, fixed protocols, and limits. The matched EditGuard return is receipt-audited; label it validation-only and distinguish receipt checks from independent source-pixel/model replay.
2. **Propagate the split decision.** State that the old v2 test IDs are unrecoverable, overlap with the later 500-image cohort is unknown, and that cohort is non-confirmatory. Do not make untouched/pristine or confirmatory-generalization claims and do not inspect it for additional outcomes.
3. **Plan any future confirmatory study as a new study.** Use a new provenance-audited cohort with frozen membership, endpoints, code/checkpoint hashes, and documented authorization. The current cohort cannot be rehabilitated into confirmatory TEST by protocol freeze.
4. **No repository cleanup by moving “legacy” code now.** Existing imports/tests depend on a broad research workspace; archiving is cosmetic and risks breaking reproducibility. Instead label active vs historical code in documentation.
5. **Final package:** verify full tests; add a one-page claim/evidence/limit table, bibliography, reproducibility/version list and dataset/repository licences. Do not tag a final release or use “publishable-grade” until those are actually complete.

## Suggested 8-slide structure

1. Research question and the three contracts (integrity / localisation / diagnostic effects).
2. Protocol/cohort and adaptation boundary (lesion-disjoint; patient-disjoint not established).
3. Track A storage–latency–coverage table, including abstentions.
4. Track C baseline and JPEG70/Gaussian sensitivity; paired VAL examples, not clinical claims.
5. Exploratory fixed-threshold / score-shift chart (clearly labelled post-hoc validation).
6. Track B and D as bounded failed/limited experiments; one structural equation + TRAIN12 row.
7. Integrated comparison matrix: measured vs pending, unequal costs explicit, literature-only rows separated.
8. Conclusions, limitations, exact next gate, and project future work.

Detailed evidence is in the snapshot dossier plus the TRAIN12 addendum; this roadmap is not a replacement for either. Current full software regression after these additions: **539 passed, 1 CUDA-dependent test skipped in 73.08 s**. `git diff --check` is clean. Tests support code integrity only, not efficacy. The editable eight-slide draft is `deliverables/PROJECT_PRESENTATION_DRAFT.pptx`; reproducibility helpers are in `scripts/build_presentation_draft.py` and optional dependencies in `requirements-presentation.txt`.

## Follow-up: execution tooling and final-test gate

A deterministic 50-image VAL `bounded289` empirical capacity sweep is now implemented at `scripts/sweep_bounded_capacity_validation.py`; test fixture passes. It is intentionally not reported as an ISIC finding until run against all required real prepared VAL pixels. The exact final TEST protocol has been drafted in root `FREEZE_PROTOCOL.md`, with Track A's 15,000-row matrix, Track C's 5,000-row matrix, an EditGuard inclusion switch, and a strict one-time return audit policy. It requires explicit sign-off and final data/compute access; no test predictions have been made.

The Google Drive/repository search found no original fixed ISIC image files (only metadata, labels, and returned hash/preparation receipts), and local PyTorch reports CUDA unavailable. Thus no amount of parallel code edits can validly produce the held-out results in this runtime. To close the actual empirical items, provide the exact original 2,500 selected images via an accessible Drive folder/archive and an authorized GPU runtime for the EditGuard handoff. After the matched VAL return is audited, record the user's A/B decision in the frozen protocol and run TEST once. Do not replace missing pixels with similarly named mirrors or manufacture a pass from receipts.

Current complete regression after capacity-sweep tests and protocol docs: **540 passed, 1 CUDA-dependent skipped in 93.20 s**; `git diff --check` clean. The capacity sweep and notebook are ready; they are not experiment results.
```

```text
# Corrected academic upgrade implementation plan

**Purpose:** turn the independent review and the attached panel proposal into a safe, evidence-led execution plan. This plan is not itself evidence that any proposed experiment has run or that a panel has approved its claims.

**Current constraint (status 2026-10-19 UTC):** the former 2026-11-24 date on this note is future-dated and not accepted chronology. The matched EditGuard return passed structural/arithmetic/signature checks using a separately supplied public key; this remains receipt-level evidence, not independent pixel/model replay. T1 is now returned/ingested on fixed TRAIN/validation only and includes SSIM/MSE/PSNR for EditGuard-marked validation images; details are in `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. Historical `isic-study-v2` exact test membership is unrecoverable; overlap with the later 500-image cohort is unknown. That cohort is non-confirmatory and closed to further outcome analysis. See `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md` and `deliverables/GATE0_PROVENANCE_STATUS.md`. Future confirmation requires a new provenance-audited cohort and frozen protocol.

## Executive decision

Use the attached plan as a list of candidate upgrades, **not as an approved scientific conclusion**. The matched EditGuard return audit and T1 multi-architecture validation ingestion are complete at the receipt level. The historical split issue is dispositioned: old membership is unrecoverable, and the current 500-image cohort is non-confirmatory. The key status/claims/presentation documents now reflect this; keep any remaining unimplemented hybrid, anatomical attack, or ROI/RONI idea labelled as a conditional proposal, not a result.

The defensible short-term framing is a comparative study of distinct contracts—detached integrity/localisation, operator-associated classifier changes, and a bounded embedding feasibility pilot. The proposed hybrid and ROI/RONI method remain unimplemented hypotheses until their experiments succeed.

## Corrections required before implementation

1. **Keep four modes separate, but do not promise a hybrid win.** Signing an EditGuard-marked image with `bounded289` is not zero-distortion relative to the source image. It is zero additional distortion relative to that already-watermarked representation. A hybrid may improve some coverage cases; it does not “strictly dominate” either component until a matched experiment demonstrates that.
2. **Tile localisation is not pixel-exact localisation.** The full-grid baselines identify failed 4×4/8×8 tiles and expand them to pixels. Their maps include extra pixels: the returned local IoUs are below 1.0. State the exact tile-level guarantee, map availability, missed pixels and false highlights; do not call the expanded pixel maps exact.
3. **Fallback never upgrades authentication.** In a hybrid, a missing, invalid or stripped sidecar remains `UNAUTHENTICATED`/`FAILED_INTEGRITY`. EditGuard may supply a *probabilistic supplemental mask*, not a cryptographic verification or a `VERIFIED` decision. Preserve the Stage-1 result and report fallback confidence/coverage separately.
4. **Do not assume EditGuard payload BER is available.** The current adapter embeds a 64-bit message but its localization interface returns a residual-derived mask; it does not currently expose a tested message decoder/BER output. Add bit accuracy only if the exact decoder, payload protocol, and corresponding ground truth are implemented and validated. The present return can support mask metrics and recorded MSE/PSNR receipts; it does not contain source/marked images for independent visual replay or SSIM calculation.
5. **No “SOTA” or mechanism claims by aspiration.** A local adapted comparison is not SOTA. Track C reports the original one-DenseNet operator-associated shift; T1 now adds two architectures on the same fixed validation cohort, so it is a cross-architecture validation comparison but not an independent-cohort replication. Neither result establishes a causal smoothing mechanism, clinical effect, or Grad-CAM explanation fidelity.
6. **ROI masks need independent validation.** ISIC 2019 labels are diagnostic labels, not automatically lesion segmentation masks. A classical threshold mask plus visual review on 20 images is not enough to call every attack anatomically lesion-centred. If independently validated masks or annotators are unavailable, call the operation a synthetic central/ROI-proxy edit—not realistic melanoma concealment.
7. **A self-embedded ROI digest is not automatically trusted.** Any ROI hash/MAC used as an integrity claim needs a trusted key/signature binding and a stable ROI definition. A freely recomputable digest embedded in the same image is not an authentication guarantee. Do not promise 100% lesion-change detection before testing mask error, payload extraction error, and attack cases.
8. **Close the test-membership recovery attempt.** The original `isic-study-v2` runner evaluated 1,000 test images. The uploaded collection ZIP contains only aggregate receipts; no test-ID manifest/verdict array exists in the available evidence. By decision, treat exact membership as unrecoverable and overlap with the later holdout as unknown. The later 500-image cohort is non-confirmatory; do not call it pristine or inspect further outcomes.
9. **Resolve time/source anomalies before using legacy run results.** Future-dated receipts and unavailable source commits are provenance blockers. Preserve and hash them; do not erase or silently relabel them as false. If they cannot be resolved, mark them legacy/unverified and keep them out of headline tables.
10. **Keep evidence caveats, but write them for the audience.** Avoid conversational “the user reports” phrasing in academic prose. However, retain an accurate methodology/limitations sentence explaining what was recomputed from returned records versus replayed from pixels. Unit-test counts belong in a short implementation note, not the abstract or headline findings.

## Work packages and gates

### Gate 0 — Evaluation-state disposition (current status 2026-10-19; no new GPU job in this provenance audit)

**Completed**
- The matched EditGuard validation return was audited against the exact Drive notebook and passed receipt, map-arithmetic and 500/500 Signed4 signature checks. It remains receipt-level evidence; no pixels or models were replayed. See `deliverables/MATCHED_EDITGUARD_VALIDATION_RETURN_REVIEW.md`.
- The uploaded v2 seed-0 bundle was integrity-checked and found to contain only aggregate receipts and its collection manifest. The actual manifest and per-image verdict ID arrays are absent.
- The historical v2 `test_n=1000` evaluation is confirmed. Exact membership recovery is closed by decision; overlap with the later 500-image cohort remains unknown. The later cohort is classified as non-confirmatory development evidence and must not be accessed for further outcome analysis.

**Remaining provenance work**
- Keep unavailable source commits and run records labelled legacy/unverified; do not present them as source-reproducible.
- A future confirmatory evaluation requires a new cohort audited against recoverable prior image/lesion membership and a new frozen protocol. Do not repurpose the current internal holdout.

**Disposition record:** `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`; detailed audit state: `deliverables/GATE0_PROVENANCE_STATUS.md`.

### WP1 — Matched EditGuard validation comparison (completed; no TEST)

**Status:** the separate 500-image × 10-case matched return was produced and receipt-audited; no additional run is required to fill that table. T1 also returned separate EditGuard-paired classifier contrasts and SSIM/MSE/PSNR. Neither return was independently replayed from original image pixels/models. Any old instruction below to run the matched validation notebook is historical; do not start another run solely to reproduce these receipts or touch TEST.

The earlier matched runner stores per-row mask confusion, map artifacts, same-seed pairing, and classifier probability contrasts when the classifier is attached.

**From a valid return, compute and report**
- Pixel precision, recall, F1 and IoU directly from returned TP/FP/FN; intact false-positive rate and image-positive rate; conditional localization metrics together with map coverage/abstention.
- Per-case latency and explicit byte costs. The existing reader comparison adds a **524,378-byte Full4 sidecar** to EditGuard; it is not equal-storage or native EditGuard cost parity.
- The separate matched EditGuard archive's fidelity fields are limited to its own recorded setup receipts; the T1 return reports marking-fidelity MSE/PSNR/SSIM over validation images. Do not conflate those two archives or claim independent recomputation from pixels. Do not claim payload BER unless the decoder is exposed and tested.
- Diagnostic contrasts already specified by the runner: original vs. marked, original vs. operated original, operated original vs. operated marked, and original vs. operated marked. Recompute the paired class-stratified intervals and report the imperfect classifier/validation-selection limits.

**Do not proceed to the hybrid until** the matched EditGuard return passes review and the hybrid decision states are frozen. For the hybrid, sign the EditGuard-marked image, `I_w`, with `bounded289`; track MSE/PSNR relative to the original `I` and relative to `I_w` separately. Use the same 500 inputs and operator seeds across pure EditGuard, pure `bounded289`, and hybrid. Stage-1 tile maps must be called tile-level; Stage-2 masks remain statistical. On sidecar loss or invalidity, Stage 2 may output a supplemental mask but never change the integrity status to verified. Report fallback rate, coverage, errors, storage, runtime, and distortion. Do not claim dominance unless paired results establish it.

**Deliverables if the run supports them**
- `deliverables/tables/table_editguard_metrics.csv`
- `deliverables/tables/table_editguard_diagnostic_impact.csv`
- `scripts/evaluate_hybrid_verifier.py` and `deliverables/tables/table_hybrid_vs_components.csv` only after the pure baseline audit and new protocol freeze.

### WP2 — Forensics metrics and existing validation analysis (CPU/local; no new model training)

1. Add one deterministic metrics script that reads the existing row-level files and writes CSV/JSON with definitions, denominators, missing-map coverage, and per-case values. For each method/case report TP/FP/FN/TN-derived pixel precision/recall/F1/IoU, intact FPR, map coverage, and image-level decision counts. Do not pool abstentions into zero error.
2. Correct the exact Track A count everywhere: the row files contain 1,503 verified and 13,497 failed records because `ISIC_0069696`’s `local_blur32_surrogate` changed zero RGB pixels; there is one verified no-op row per method. Preserve that case rather than relabelling it a successful manipulation.
3. For the QIM pilot, compute per-image PSNR from each image’s MSE before taking means/medians. For `ISIC_0061070`, MSE 0.0764401754 corresponds to about **59.30 dB**, and 5.8180987040 to about **40.48 dB**. The 59.90 dB value is the PSNR calculated from the *aggregate mean fixed-QIM MSE* 0.0665994, not the first image’s PSNR. Label these estimands clearly. Bit accuracy is reported only for the actual decoded 4,096-bit payload; the corresponding nominal capacity is 0.015625 bpp at 512×512.
4. Reuse the existing `scripts/sweep_bounded_capacity_validation.py` for the specified area sweep; do not create a second competing capacity runner. Run it only when exact prepared validation pixels are available, under a fresh output path, and keep it validation/development evidence. Existing synthetic profiling is not a real-image throughput result.
5. Reuse `scripts/analyse_class_sensitive_drift.py` and its fixed-threshold outputs where they answer the question. Label its 90%-specificity threshold as exploratory/post-hoc validation. Sub-source ID-range composition is a proxy, not an institution label. Run pHash only from the exact authorized source images; pHash does not establish patient independence. Do not inspect the unresolved TEST split for subgroup effects.

**Deliverables**
- `scripts/compute_forensics_metrics.py`
- `deliverables/tables/table_trackA_forensics_metrics.csv`
- `deliverables/tables/table_trackC_drift.csv`
- `deliverables/tables/table_trackD_psnr_payload.csv`
- `deliverables/tables/table_cohort_provenance_phash.csv` (only after exact source pixels are available; otherwise mark pHash unavailable).

### WP3 — Anatomical-ROI experiments and Grad-CAM (conditional, not a one-week blocker)

**Prerequisites:** authorized exact images, independently validated lesion masks, a frozen classifier checkpoint, and an evaluation split not selected using these outputs. First determine whether this fixed ISIC 2019 cohort actually has usable lesion masks. Diagnostic labels alone do not satisfy this prerequisite.

If verified masks/qualified annotation are available:
- Implement a deterministic ROI loader/validator and visually QA a preregistered sample; report agreement/coverage and fallback counts. A centered fallback box must be labelled a proxy, never an anatomical ground truth mask.
- Freeze lesion-centred blur/copy/inpaint operators and seeds before model evaluation. Describe these as controlled synthetic interventions, not observed clinical concealment events.
- Use ROI masks—not DenseNet gradients—to place edits or partition ROI/RONI. Grad-CAM may be measured after the fact using predeclared cosine/top-20%-IoU/ROI-mass metrics, with no claim that map stability equals clinical explanation fidelity.
- Keep this an exploratory validation analysis. No TEST-derived mask tuning or outcome inspection.

If masks or trustworthy annotation are not available, defer anatomical claims and omit this package rather than manufacturing lesion masks or calling central patches “clinical attacks.”

### WP4 — Figures and presentation after analysis freezes

Build figures only from audited tables and authorized image examples. Include four contract modes as *separate paradigms*, and label the hybrid/ROI-RONI cells pending until run. Show storage, distortion, coverage/abstention, runtime and class-sensitive changes together. Do not show “100% exact pixel map” for tile-expanded outputs. Do not show source ISIC pixels in public Drive/Git artifacts unless the license and permissions permit it. Use 300-DPI PNG/PDF as presentation formatting, not as evidence quality.

### WP5 — Stronger/multiple classifiers (next-level, after core gates)

This is a new exploratory study, not a patch to the existing DenseNet result. Keep the current frozen DenseNet as a baseline. If compute/data/licensing permit, train an augmented DenseNet121 and one distinct architecture on exactly the same TRAIN/VAL memberships. Freeze augmentations, class weighting, optimizer, checkpoint rule, class order and operator suite before fitting. Do **not** use JPEG augmentation if compression is the test exposure. Select only on VAL and report paired class-sensitive changes with intervals. This does not clean or reopen the old test. No new model runs while the active Kaggle job is using the available GPU.

### WP6 — ROI/RONI transform-domain reference (research extension, not a promised result)

Do not implement the full DWT-SVD method until the cited methods are verified and WP3 has a valid ROI definition. Start with a tiny synthetic engineering prototype; then predeclare a validation parameter sweep over payload, PSNR/SSIM, ROI-MSE, BER and localization. Do not tune against a target “BER < 15–20%” and then present that same validation value as independent performance; show the complete curve and use a genuinely independent evaluation only after split integrity is resolved.

If a digest binds untouched ROI pixels, authenticate the digest under a trusted signature/MAC and define how the exact ROI mask/version is trusted. Distinguish RONI embedding distortion from ROI-MSE. Do not claim 100% ROI tamper sensitivity, JPEG resilience, or reproduction of Prakash/PFF-WM before independent execution and source-level protocol comparison.

### WP7 — Repository provenance, attribution and release hygiene (after results freeze)

- Keep a hash manifest and provenance index for every retained run. Quarantine legacy evidence through explicit labels/status files first; only move files after all references, hashes, and links are updated. Do not delete evidence to make the repository look clean.
- Add a project-specific source/data/model attribution file. Verify every bibliography entry, publication year/DOI and upstream license. Do not grant an MIT licence to third-party files/data. Select a root code licence only with the author/owner’s approval and exclude restricted data/weights.
- Curate tracked source, tests, deliverables, and small audit receipts; exclude raw ISIC pixels, checkpoints, caches, and restricted upstream code. The current checkout has many untracked files, so a fresh clone/PR must be checked explicitly before calling it reproducible.
- Replace conversational status wording in academic prose with accurate study-state language. Keep audit limits in the methods/limitations appendix; do not remove them. Remove unqualified “SOTA,” “untouched TEST,” “causal smoothing,” “clinical preservation,” and “novel” claims unless new evidence earns them.
- Do not create a `v1.0-academic-final` tag until all mandatory gates pass, the chosen thesis scope is signed off, and the release snapshot is reproducible. Any commit/tag remains on the current active branch; never switch branches.

## Proposed order and realistic schedule

| Order | Work | Compute | Exit condition |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Current Kaggle completion, archive preservation, return audit, old-test/provenance reconciliation | Existing job only; no duplicate GPU | Complete return reviewed; old test/overlap status explicit |
| 1 | Existing-row metrics, no-op correction, QIM PSNR and table repairs | CPU | Every published metric traces to a row/artifact and has the right denominator |
| 2 | Decide whether a fresh final holdout is defensible; otherwise declare no confirmatory TEST | Data/provenance work | No unsupported “untouched” statement; no test run before freeze |
| 3 | Finish EditGuard analysis and, only if justified, implement/run hybrid on matched IDs | One approved GPU or sufficient CPU; sequential | Full paired comparison, explicit trust/fallback states, all costs and limitations |
| 4 | ROI/mask feasibility and selected figures | CPU plus authorized images | ROI definition validated or ROI claims explicitly deferred |
| 5 | Stronger classifiers, then ROI/RONI research prototype if time remains | Additional GPU time; separate preregistration | New results are clearly exploratory and not mixed into previous tests |
| 6 | Attribution, source packaging, doc cleanup and final release snapshot | CPU | Clean reproducible clone; permissions confirmed; no unsupported headline claim |

A realistic first-week target is Gates 0–2, the already-running EditGuard audit, corrected metrics, and a transparent presentation. WP3/WP5/WP6 may require additional weeks, annotation, and GPU access; do not imply they are finished to meet a calendar target.

## Acceptance criteria for any final academic package

- Every result has a data split, method version, source/checkpoint hash, command/environment, per-image denominator, and a status label.
- TEST status and overlap are stated precisely; if old test membership cannot be recovered, no confirmatory claim uses an unverified overlapping holdout.
- Hybrid results never claim zero MSE relative to the unmarked source, never call tile maps pixel-exact, and never let a neural fallback authenticate an image.
- ROI/RONI results have a trusted ROI/reference contract and measured—not assumed—distortion/robustness.
- EditGuard, stronger classifiers, ROI, Grad-CAM and RONI entries stay “pending” until their own outputs exist and pass audit.
- The final thesis contribution is either a measured contract-aware comparison or a demonstrated learned-method improvement—not an unvalidated combination of every implemented module.
```

```text
# Study to implemented candidate: evidence review

**Current status correction (2026-10-19 UTC):** The former 2026-11-24 date in this document is later than the current date and is not accepted as valid chronology. Later-dated receipts/addenda in this project are future-dated and their timestamps are unauthenticated. This long-form note includes older planning snapshots; read this correction and `PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md` first. T1 classifier validation is now returned/ingested on TRAIN and validation only; see `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. The matched EditGuard return is receipt-audited only (no source-pixel/model replay). Historical `isic-study-v2` test IDs are unrecoverable from retained evidence; overlap with the later 500-image cohort is unknown. That later cohort is non-confirmatory and closed to additional outcome analysis. PH2 is not treated as a clean external cohort. Any confirmatory work requires a new provenance-audited cohort.

## What is complete

The 500-image classical validation was reanalysed from five returned archives (15,000 records). Compact evidence uses 18,620 bytes versus 524,378 for full4. Both provided maps for all 500 copy and all 500 local-blur cases, with matching localisation maps. Compact evidence supplied no maps for the seven global operators, and verification was approximately 1.81 seconds slower per local manipulation. These are storage/coverage/latency tradeoffs, not an overall superiority result. Receipt consistency is not an independent full pixel/operator replay.

Refiner failure diagnostics did not rescue the learned models: selected shared-rule outputs remained all-background on seven synthetic fixtures. Training-only threshold changes introduced intact-image false positives. This motivates stronger controls and explicit failure reporting, not a claim that attention solves localisation.

Neither result measures the clinical effect of watermark embedding. In particular, the compact authenticated method does not modify the image. A defensible narrative is: these studies identified competing engineering requirements and insufficient evidence for learned claims; we consequently implemented a separately testable, payload-constrained diagnostic correction hypothesis. It is not defensible to say that a measured clinical failure caused this design.

## Implemented candidate

`src/diagnostic_constrained_embedding.py` embeds a binary message using pair-difference QIM in fixed red-channel carrier samples. A frozen classifier guides bounded integer-RGB corrections on **non-carrier samples only**. The decoder and carrier values remain fixed. This is a research prototype, not a secure or novel watermark claim.

The pilot runner `scripts/experiment_diagnostic_embedding.py` compares fixed QIM, weaker uniform QIM, diagnostic correction and energy-matched random-direction correction. It restricts exploration to the first bounded number of TRAIN images, records source/checkpoint hashes and predictions, and evaluates recovery separately from full signed RGB authentication. An independent evaluator is optional in code but necessary for meaningful transfer evidence. The later 12-image guide-TRAIN pilot was executed and is summarized below; it is a receipt-reviewed feasibility pilot, not transfer evidence.

## Executed mechanism check

`deliverables/verification-evidence/diagnostic-embedding-mechanism-v1.json` records an explicitly synthetic 8×8 example:

| Variant | MSE | Guide KL | Unseen-model KL | Clean bit errors |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Fixed | 0.166667 | 0.048425 | 0.048425 | 0 |
| Weaker | 0.041667 | 0.012253 | 0.012253 | 0 |
| Correction | 0.25 | 0 | 0.185144 | 0 |
| Energy-matched random | 0.25 | 0 | 0.185144 | 0 |

The correction successfully preserves carriers and improves its guide objective, **but worsens the unseen model**. The random control ties it in this fixture. Thus this demonstrates mechanism feasibility and an explicit transfer failure, not efficacy. Correction also increases distortion. A non-carrier edit preserves message recovery but fails separate signed authentication: message recovery alone cannot authenticate the image.

## Prior art and claim boundary

- PFF-WM: https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2667305326000591 — local extracted paper `project-context/papers/pff-wm.txt`, saliency embedding around lines 505–526 and classifier-explanation alignment around 820–850. Generic diagnostic-aware watermarking is already covered.
- MedSign: https://arxiv.org/html/2503.08346v1 — primary page reviewed; pathology localization, adaptive watermark strength and diagnostic-region preservation for generated medical images already exist. Its generation setting differs, but that does not establish our novelty.

The narrower fixed-carrier correction formulation is an implemented candidate for further comparison, not established differentiation from all prior work. No SOTA, clinical safety or novelty claim is currently supported.

## Verification and remaining gates

Historical local regression counts in this section refer to the earlier implementation snapshot, not a current full-suite run. Unit and fixture integration tests do not establish medical performance. The original sent classifier notebook remains unchanged. The later 500-image holdout is not a valid untouched test: historical `isic-study-v2` test access is confirmed, exact IDs are unavailable, overlap is unknown, and no further holdout outcome analysis is allowed.

Classifier impact return review: the original DenseNet121 checkpoint selected epoch 3 with validation BA 0.7440, ROC-AUC 0.8454, and MEL sensitivity 0.5893 (33/56). Stored prediction arithmetic/source-protocol consistency were checked; no local source-image/model replay was performed. The earlier statement that the operator-impact notebook was still running is superseded: Track C and T1 results are available. T1’s two additional models and validation-only results are summarized in `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`; do not blend its separate protocol/intervals with Track C without naming the source.

Before claiming a useful contribution: run the bounded training pilot; obtain independent-model evaluation; account for all distortion, payload margin, compute and authentication costs; freeze a validation/final-test protocol; execute actual matched baseline comparisons. Report failures and null results. Clinical fidelity requires evidence beyond guide confidence or KL. `REVIEW_READINESS_PLAN.md` ranks the gaps, fair controls and go/no-go criteria.

Machine-readable claim status and artifact SHA-256 hashes: `deliverables/verification-evidence/study-to-candidate-evidence-ledger-v1.json`. Rebuild with `.venv/bin/python scripts/build_research_evidence_ledger.py`; rerun the mechanism with `OMP_NUM_THREADS=2 MKL_NUM_THREADS=2 .venv/bin/python scripts/smoke_diagnostic_embedding.py --out deliverables/verification-evidence/diagnostic-embedding-mechanism-v1.json`.

## Stronger controls and structural finding (v2)

Implemented `src/diagnostic_correction_controls.py` and upgraded the unexecuted TRAIN pilot to protocol v2 with six variants. Existing sent/running notebooks are unchanged. New controls are forward-query-matched best-of-K random search and a strict pixel-distortion gate. The pilot records query counts, random search traces and rejection reasons. Forward-query matching is **not** wall-clock/gradient-compute matching: guided search additionally uses backward passes. The conservative gate reuses the already computed guided candidate and therefore does not avoid optimisation cost.

A structural limit is now explicit. Let C be the locked carrier set, b the fixed embedding and x the original. Since b equals x outside C, any corrected y with y[C]=b[C] satisfies:

`||y-x||² = ||b-x||² + ||(y-x)[outside C]||²`.

Therefore every nonzero non-carrier correction strictly increases total squared distortion. The equal-distortion gate must return the baseline for every such correction; it is a limitation detector, not an efficacy method. Any future useful claim must explicitly justify a diagnostic/distortion tradeoff, or investigate a separately versioned formulation allowing carrier adjustments within decoding constraints. The latter is not implemented, proven robust or established novel here; it must not replace fixed-carrier results silently.

Executed receipt: `deliverables/verification-evidence/correction-controls-v2.json`. The synthetic comparison used 19 forward queries in each search; guided also used 8 backward passes. Random and guided both reached guide KL zero. The gate rejected the guided candidate (squared error 48 versus baseline 32). All returned clean payloads decoded. No medical efficacy follows. Regression after v2: **509 passed, 1 skipped**, 65.72 seconds; diff checks passed.

This changes the recommendation: keep the candidate experimental rather than advertising an equal-distortion improvement. A medical pilot may measure a tradeoff; it cannot overturn the algebraic constraint.

## Actual TRAIN12 pilot return reviewed (supersedes pending-pilot statements)

`diagnostic-embedding-train12-v2-results.zip` SHA256 `2ac8e65a9f1703ec186a2acbf744f5d0290c7bb5da5a5d11b51ed254fa81ae71`: 12 TRAIN images (10 nonMEL/2 MEL), 72 rows, 720 recovery entries, 72 signatures checked, exact sent-source/preparation/cohort identity and recomputed summary checks passed. All variants retain all12 guide labels; these TRAIN examples were already correctly classified. Guided correction returns fixed outputs on11/12, changes one image with increased distortion, and demonstrates no class-decision benefit. Mean MSE fixed .0665994, weaker .0163891, guided .545071, query-matched random2.768288. Mean guide KL fixed .000544013, weaker .0000469812, guided .000466016, query-random .000407102. Gate rejects the sole nonzero guided correction. JPEG70 payload BER is about50% across variants; clean decoding is not JPEG robustness.

Decision: **do not scale this unchanged candidate to validation/test**. Preserve as an evaluated experimental extension with negative/limited findings; prioritise independent review and pending matched baseline evidence. Any redesign requires a new protocol and prior-art/constraint review first. Independent evaluator still absent. These are receipt/signature checks, NOT independent image or attack replay: pixels were not returned. Full text addendum: `deliverables/DIAGNOSTIC_EMBEDDING_PILOT_REVIEW_ADDENDUM.txt`; evidence in `deliverables/verification-evidence/diagnostic-embedding-train12-return-v1/`. The earlier independent-review dossier remains a historical snapshot and should be shared with this addendum.

## Additional finalisation review (2026-10-19)

Adapted checklist and evidence boundaries are consolidated in `docs/ADAPTED_FINALISATION_ROADMAP.md`. Post-hoc VAL class-score shifts and a clean-derived fixed threshold are in `deliverables/verification-evidence/class-sensitive-drift-v2/`; this is descriptive, not causal or preregistered. # Faculty framing and completed classifier return
```

```text
> **Status note — 2026-10-19 UTC:** This file reviews the original adapted DenseNet classifier and prior-art framing; it is not the complete current classifier status. T1 later added DenseNet121-Aug and ResNet50-Aug validation-only results on the fixed cohort; see `deliverables/T1_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`. T1 did not access TEST and does not establish external or clinical generalization. Historical test membership and timestamp provenance remain unresolved as described in `PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md`.

## Faculty: relevant public evidence, not guessed preferences

The supplied Scholar profile identifies Dr. Choudhary Shyam Prakash, NIT Andhra Pradesh, and lists image forensics, medical image watermarking and medical image authentication. His institutional page independently lists those interests and relevant publications.

Sources:
- https://scholar.google.co.in/citations?user=u8lyCQQAAAAJ&hl=en
- https://nitandhra.ac.in/dept/cse/23034 (institutional listing returned by search)
- https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-025-11023-9 (primary abstract/introduction reviewed)

The 2025 Scientific Reports paper coauthored by him, *Ensuring integrity and security of medical image transmission in IoMT using highly imperceptible and robust watermarking approach*, describes ROI/RONI separation, no ROI embedding, localized tamper detection/recovery bits and DWT-SVD RONI embedding. Its abstract reports high PSNR/SSIM and low BER; these are published claims on its protocol, not our reproduced results. Full algorithm/results review and a matched implementation remain pending. Do not claim that protecting diagnostic regions is our new idea. The faculty also has passive medical authentication and copy-move/splicing publications; active and passive methods must not be conflated.

### Questions to prepare (our inference, not stated faculty demands)

1. What is the precise threat model, and is the method active watermarking, passive forensics or signed sidecar authentication?
2. What, specifically, differs from ROI/RONI protection, PFF-WM and MedSign?
3. Does decoded message recovery actually authenticate all image content? Our counterexample says no.
4. What is counted in payload, packet size, keys, verification latency and map coverage?
5. Are JPEG/resize accepted operations or integrity violations? Exact RGB signatures reject them; compact map abstention is not correct localisation.
6. Are localisation maps assessed against exact changed pixels? How are abstentions counted?
7. Were models and thresholds frozen before evaluation? Are validation and final test separated?
8. Why should classifier agreement mean clinical preservation? It does not; show class-wise baseline quality and transfer failures.

## Returned classifier: verified results

Drive return `adapted-isic2500-classifier-v1-results.zip` (26,951,310 bytes), SHA256 `ee481eaad49387678cc74662af9debb0b0fc8146f54be80cbff0214f6a6f2f70`.

Checkpoint `best.pt` is inside the return: SHA256 `38d52ca825472cb525c4c2745f9f7e2e047ddcbc34a6fe6cdcbfbdc0786ba57e`.

The review checked archive CRC/membership, exact code against the sent notebook, cohort/preparation identity, train/validation membership and lesion separation, all 20 epochs, first-best balanced-accuracy selection, stored prediction arithmetic and safe tensor checkpoint loading. Selected epoch: **3**. A reviewer bug comparing torch tuples against JSON lists was corrected by canonical JSON comparison; no training artefacts or notebook were changed. The fixture now exercises that serialization difference. Targeted reviewer tests: **3 passed**.

| Validation metric | Result |
|---|---:|
| Images: nonMEL / MEL | 444 / 56 |
| Accuracy | 86.40% |
| Balanced accuracy | 74.40% |
| Macro F1 | 0.7070 |
| ROC AUC | 0.8454 |
| Melanoma sensitivity | 58.93% (33/56) |
| Specificity | 89.86% (399/444) |
| False negatives / false positives | 23 / 45 |

The always-nonMEL reference has **88.80% accuracy**, higher than the model, but only **50% balanced accuracy** and zero melanoma sensitivity. Therefore do not headline raw accuracy. The class-stratified image-bootstrap interval for model balanced accuracy is approximately 67.48–81.00%; melanoma sensitivity 44.64–71.43%. These are descriptive intervals on checkpoint-selection validation data, not independent final performance.

The separately downloaded `densenet121-imagenet-compatible.zip` is a torch serialized weights archive despite its suffix, not an additional result package to extract. Its SHA256 matches the compatible-initialization receipt (`2bf68431a4b7e515afe55cb4ee8fb1bc263b55267041a6f9b28bd090285dc2b6`). Original publisher weights bytes were not supplied/rehashed; do not claim full independent source verification.

Evidence lives in `deliverables/verification-evidence/adapted-classifier-return-v1/`. Stored validation probabilities were checked arithmetically, not independently rerun against image files in this environment. The classifier is a limited research probe, not clinically ready. Test evaluation is false in the reviewed receipts and sent code; the final test was not run here.

## Next executable step

`deliverables/adapted_classifier_impact.ipynb` is generated with the reviewed checkpoint SHA256 pinned. It runs the frozen classifier on **validation only**, with ten existing operators and adapted FGSM/PGD, and records paired diagnostic impacts. Attach the saved classifier output directory containing `best.pt` and `summary.json` plus the original ISIC2019 dataset. This is an unexecuted handoff, not new results. It neither retrains nor evaluates test images and does not alter the original training notebook.

Embedding efficacy needs the separate training-only candidate pilot and later independent-model controls. The impact notebook does not execute that pilot. Do not present the classifier baseline as evidence that watermarking harmed diagnosis, or the candidate as already superior.
```

```text
DIAGNOSTIC EMBEDDING TRAIN12 V2 — REVIEW ADDENDUM
Read with PROJECT_INDEPENDENT_REVIEW_SNAPSHOT.txt (v1). This addendum supersedes only its pending TRAIN-pilot status. Earlier conclusions are not retroactively rewritten.

1. STATUS AND CHECKS
The actual 12-image TRAIN pilot has now completed and its returned receipts were reviewed. There are 10 nonMEL and 2 MEL images, fixed first12 training rows, not selected for favourable outcomes. Those images belong to the guide classifier's TRAIN set, so this is not independent evaluation.

Checks passed: ZIP CRC and exact member set; exact bundled Python source against the sent notebook; original cohort and preparation receipt identity; reviewed checkpoint/protocol and settings; first12 TRAIN identity/order; 72 unique rows and 720 recovery entries; deterministic payload seed/hash; probability validity; intact recovery and declared integrity outcomes; carrier equality claims; matched-energy/query receipts; strict gate returning fixed outputs; 72 Ed25519 packet signatures, lengths and headers; all aggregate diagnostic metrics and 1,000-draw bootstrap intervals recomputed from stored probabilities.

Limits: no original/received pixels were returned. MSE, carrier equality, bit recovery, image hashes and operator outputs cannot be independently replayed from this ZIP. Packet signatures validate against the returned key, not an independently authenticated issuer, and do not verify the association with unavailable image bytes. No clinical safety, independent-model transfer, generalisation, robustness or novelty result follows.

2. MAIN RESULT — DO NOT SCALE THE UNCHANGED METHOD
Guided correction returns the fixed output on11/12 images; guide KL improves on only1/12. Every variant preserves all12 predicted classes. Original and received class metrics are all perfect on this tiny guide-TRAIN subset; there is no class-decision improvement available to demonstrate here. This does not contradict the earlier weaker500-image validation baseline; it is a different, training-selected subset.

Guided mean MSE is about8.18 times fixed, concentrated in one changed image. On ISIC_0061070 (MEL), fixed MSE .0764401754 rises to5.8180987040 under correction (about76.1 times); original MEL probability .6242184, fixed .6472858, guided .6140608. All three predict MEL correctly. This is guide-probability adjustment, not a clinical/classification rescue.

The distortion gate accepts11 no-op outputs and rejects the one nonzero correction, leaving fixed output on all12. Do not advertise11 accepted cases as11 successful useful corrections.

Query-matched random search also reduces mean guide KL, but at substantially greater distortion; it matches forward-query counts, not gradient cost or energy. Weaker QIM has lower mean distortion AND lower mean guide KL than guided here, but reduces decoding margin. Therefore this is not a matched-robustness superiority result for weaker QIM either.

3. AGGREGATE RESULTS
KL below is recomputed after normalising tiny softmax-sum roundoff; it may differ slightly from torch trace KL. MSE uses RGB level units squared, not normalised[0,1] units. Values are image means over12, not a clinical endpoint.
Variant | Mean MSE | Mean guide KL | Label flips | Outputs identical to fixed
fixed_qim | 0.066599422031 | 0.000544013379 | 0 | 12/12
uniform_weaker_qim | 0.016389105055 | 0.000046981210 | 0 | 0/12
energy_matched_random | 0.545070966085 | 0.001223473681 | 0 | 11/12
diagnostic_correction | 0.545070966085 | 0.000466016444 | 0 | 11/12
query_matched_random | 2.768288188510 | 0.000407102085 | 0 | 7/12
distortion_gated_correction | 0.066599422031 | 0.000544013379 | 0 | 12/12

4. PAYLOAD RECOVERY AND AUTHENTICATION ARE DIFFERENT
All72 clean outputs report zero bit errors and VERIFIED signed-RGB status. Across manipulated outputs, signed integrity failures are consistent with recorded changed-pixel counts. This is receipt consistency, not independent operator/pixel replay.

JPEG70 payload bit-error fractions are approximately50% for all six variants. Thus clean payload preservation must NOT be described as robust message recovery under JPEG. Signed-RGB checking detects a content change independently of the fragile payload reader; it does not repair the payload.

5. WHAT THIS ADDS TO THE PROJECT
A medical-image numerical feasibility run now exists, with retained negative outcomes and fairer controls. The candidate does not yet demonstrate a useful diagnostic advantage. It should be shown as an evaluated experimental extension, not a successful novel medical watermark.

The distortion identity remains valid: fixed carrier values plus changes only to previously untouched non-carrier samples necessarily add squared distortion. This pilot cannot overturn that constraint. A lower-KL search on TRAIN alone does not justify a larger unchanged study.

Recommended next decision: stop unchanged-candidate scaling; obtain the independent review; prioritise the already-pending matched baseline validation. If a separately versioned carrier-flexible or other redesign is proposed, review its prior-art overlap, exact payload/margin constraints and decisive controls BEFORE further GPU work. Such a redesign is not implemented or validated in this addendum. An independent evaluator remains necessary for any future transfer-preservation claim, but is not by itself a remedy for weak current utility.

No request to rerun this notebook. **Status correction (2026-11-24):** an earlier ISIC-study-v2 run evaluated 1,000 test images; their IDs are unrecoverable from available retained evidence, and overlap with the later 500-image cohort is unknown. The later cohort is non-confirmatory development evidence; do not call it untouched or inspect it for additional outcomes. This addendum is a historical record of the pilot review, not evidence about split independence. See `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`.
```
