The matched EditGuard return has passed receipt/map/signature audit only; no source-pixel/model replay was performed. The older `isic-study-v2` run receipt reports `test_n=1000`; exact membership is unrecoverable from retained evidence, so overlap with the later 500-image cohort is unknown. Recovery against available artifacts is closed; the later cohort is non-confirmatory development evidence and closed to additional outcome analysis. Dates later than 2026-10-19 appearing in retained receipts/addenda are future-dated and unauthenticated; the former 2026-11-24 override was not a valid current chronology. Current controlling documents: `PROJECT_CONTEXT_MASTER.md`, `deliverables/TEST_SPLIT_RECOVERY_DECISION.md`, `deliverables/GATE0_PROVENANCE_STATUS.md`, and `deliverables/CLAIMS_EVIDENCE_LIMITS.md`.

Provisional descriptive title:
Medical Image Integrity and Localisation under Evidence Constraints, with a Diagnostic-Constrained Embedding Feasibility Extension

READING GUIDE
Sections 1–15 explain the entire project, what would be presented to faculty, exact scope, negative results and remaining work. The appended evidence sections contain automatically extracted numerical tables, original compact JSON receipts and SHA256 identifiers. The reviewer need not read our conversation. Repository paths are relative to chmurali2006/Major-Project. Drive links require access; permissions have not been made public by this document.

EVIDENCE LABELS
MEASURED-VALIDATION: results from the fixed 500-image validation development cohort.
MEASURED-DEVELOPMENT: earlier small experiments, not held-out generalisation evidence.
SYNTHETIC: generated fixtures; not medical performance.
IMPLEMENTED/TESTED: source or fixture tests exist; actual medical execution may still be pending.
PENDING: not completed/returned/reviewed at this snapshot.
LITERATURE: externally published method or claim; not our reproduced result.

1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY AND HONEST CURRENT POSITION

The student wants both a rigorous comparative study and a differentiated implemented contribution. The project investigates three distinct questions:
A. Can exact image-content integrity be verified under declared trust assumptions?
B. How much localisation can be provided at a given evidence-storage budget, and at what latency?
C. What diagnostic-classifier effects accompany image operations or embedding modifications?

The strongest completed medical-image evidence is a 500-image, 15,000-record comparison of three detached signed-evidence methods. Compact evidence saves storage but costs verification time and loses fine localisation coverage for several global operations. A separate frozen-classifier evaluation covers 500 images and 12 operations, with 6,000 records. It shows that overall accuracy can improve while melanoma sensitivity deteriorates.

The new experimental extension is implemented: standard fixed QIM carriers plus classifier-guided correction of non-carrier pixels. It preserves tested clean carrier samples and message recovery, but has NOT demonstrated medical benefit or novelty. Synthetic controls expose increased distortion, an unseen-model deterioration counterexample, and random search matching the guided objective. The bounded medical TRAIN pilot notebook is prepared and uploaded; no pilot result has been reviewed at this snapshot. Do not describe that pilot as completed or assume it is running.

There is currently NO demonstrated novel/SOTA-superior main method, NO clinical-safety evidence, and NO independent final-test result. The dossier deliberately includes this because an independent review should evaluate the actual research gap, not a sales narrative.

2. WHY THIS PROJECT EXISTS AND WHAT WOULD BE USEFUL

Medical images transmitted or stored outside the acquisition system may change. A system may need to detect exact changes, localise them, or tolerate specified processing operations. These are different contracts. A visually harmless JPEG conversion is still a different decoded RGB image; conversely, a small malicious edit may leave a global classifier prediction unchanged.

The practical research question is whether useful localisation can be provided with less external evidence, and how that tradeoff interacts with runtime and diagnostic measurements. Digital signatures, hashing, watermarking, tile manifests and segmentation already exist; combining them is not automatically novel.

A useful outcome could be an experimentally characterised evidence/coverage/latency tradeoff and, if supported later, a constrained embedding improvement. It is also scientifically acceptable to conclude the candidate is not advantageous. Faculty appreciation cannot be guaranteed and is not an evidence criterion.

3. SYSTEM STRUCTURE: RELATED EXPERIMENTS, NOT ONE FINISHED PIPELINE

TRACK A — STRICT INTEGRITY / LOCALISATION STUDY
Input: prepared 512x512 uint8 RGB image.
Issuer: produces full4, full8 or compact bounded289 evidence and signs it.
Receiver: uses a separately trusted public key and expected image/version context, verifies evidence, and attempts localisation.
Outputs: integrity verdict, localisation map where available, abstention/coverage, packet size and timings.
Important: these detached methods do not embed or alter image pixels; their embedding MSE is zero. They must not be depicted as if they need the diagnostic-correction extension.

TRACK B — LEARNED LOCALISATION INVESTIGATION
Earlier main and attention-enhanced refiners were investigated. Current selected learned rules failed. They are not successful components of the final method. The project retains their results and synthetic failure diagnostics instead of tuning evaluation thresholds into apparent success.

TRACK C — FROZEN CLASSIFIER IMPACT STUDY
Input: original prepared images and explicitly defined operations.
Frozen DenseNet121 predicts before/after each operation.
Outputs: confusion matrices, accuracy, balanced accuracy, macro F1, melanoma sensitivity, AUC, prediction flips and paired intervals.
This measures original-image operator effects, NOT embedding-attributable effects.

TRACK D — CANDIDATE EMBEDDING EXTENSION
Input: original RGB image, binary message, frozen guiding classifier.
Standard pair-difference QIM embeds one bit per spatial block in two red-channel samples. Guided correction adjusts only non-carrier samples, within integer RGB bounds, to reduce prediction KL divergence from the original image.
Blind message decoding is separate from signed full-RGB authentication. A non-carrier edit can leave the message intact while changing content.
Outputs: recovery, distortion, guide/evaluator predictions, search/query receipts, authentication costs and failure reports.
This is implemented research code, not established robust or secure medical watermarking.

4. DATA, SPLITS AND ADAPTATIONS

Dataset/cohort: adapted ISIC2019 cohort of 2,500 images; fixed seed 1301.
TRAIN: 1,500 images, including 170 melanoma.
VALIDATION: 500 images, including 56 melanoma and 444 non-melanoma.
TEST: 500 images, including 57 melanoma; final test remains unevaluated in these studies.
Binary class order: [nonMEL, MEL]. This is not an eight-class diagnosis study.
Cohort construction is lesion-disjoint; patient independence has NOT been established.
Preparation review found 2,500 unique prepared hashes and no cross-split duplicate hashes in returned receipts; source pixels were not independently re-decoded or compared against an official source checksum here. That does not establish absence of all near-duplicates, patient overlap or acquisition-domain leakage.
Preprocessing: explicit RGB conversion and Pillow bicubic resize to 512x512; no EXIF-based processing in the reviewed preparation. These are local adaptations, not proof of exact paper preprocessing.

Cohort SHA256:
c524a43fce2951dbd68514a94d60610a8daa4f88fd40b8b93c53ee1f03f5aa42
Metadata SHA256:
d93994a8ed201d474de9a7af7e17ec30929cbc4a5220659ecbde17cbe83e9316
Labels SHA256:

Dataset licence, authorised acquisition and redistribution conditions require formal verification before any public release. No raw medical pixels or model weights are embedded in this text dossier.

Earlier 224-pixel mirror experiments used different provenance and resizing. They are not substituted for the reviewed original-source 512 preparation or pooled with validation500.

5. METHODS AND SECURITY CONTRACT

Full4: detached signed grid over 4x4 RGB tiles; 524,378 bytes/image.
Full8: detached signed grid over 8x8 RGB tiles; 131,162 bytes/image.
Compact bounded289: 18,620 bytes/image; attempts bounded change recovery from compact evidence. Fine localisation is conditional on successful decoding. Read the exact source/returned protocol for the construction; its name is not a universal capacity guarantee.

Full grid evidence uses library Ed25519 signatures and SHA256 with geometry/version/position binding. Standard primitives and signed manifests are prior art, not a new cryptographic construction. Binary full-grid packet accounting includes a 26-byte header, 32-byte tile digests and a 64-byte signature.

Authentication assumptions and exclusions:
- Public key must be independently trusted; accepting a key supplied by an attacker with a sidecar proves nothing about legitimate origin.
- Expected image/version identity and replay/freshness policy must come from an external trusted workflow.
- A valid signature establishes consistency with signed bytes under that key, not acquisition authenticity, correct patient identity or medical truth.
- Key custody, revocation, source compromise, complete metadata binding and a deployment identity system are not solved.
- This is decoded-RGB research code, not a DICOM PS3.15 signature-profile implementation or regulatory certification.
- No general proof of resistance to adaptive attacks or colluding authorised issuers is established.
- Earlier shared-secret HMAC carriers have different trust assumptions from Ed25519 sidecars and must not be ranked as equivalent systems without explanation.
- Legacy luma/brightness carrier collisions were found during development; those legacy designs are not certified by current results.

Metrics:
Pixel truth is exact changed decoded-RGB pixels, not semantic lesion removal or clinician annotations. Expanded tile maps may include unchanged pixels. Map IoU is conditional on availability unless explicitly otherwise stated. Abstention is not zero error. Global operations described as paper-benign remain integrity violations under the exact-RGB contract.

6. COMPLETED CLASSICAL VALIDATION STUDY — MEASURED-VALIDATION

Five result ZIP parts were reviewed: combined 397,513,266 bytes.
500 images x 10 cases x 3 methods = 15,000 records.
1,500 packet signatures and 11,500 maps checked.
Reviewed outcomes: 1,500 intact VERIFIED records with no false-positive changed-pixel maps; 13,500 manipulated records FAILED_INTEGRITY; zero reported integrity invariants violated in the receipt audit.

Operations: intact; random disjoint copy32; local_blur32_surrogate; JPEG70/85/95 (subsampling 0); resize to 90% and restore using bicubic; contrast 0.9/1.1; Gaussian sigma0.5. Local blur is a random 32-square Gaussian-blurred patch, NOT verified lesion removal. Exact operation settings appear in the appended protocol/settings receipts.

Key results:
- Compact versus full4 evidence: 18,620 vs 524,378 bytes; 96.4491% reduction.
- Compact versus full8: 85.8038% reduction.
- Copy32 map availability: 500/500 for every method.
- Local blur map availability: 500/500 for every method.
- Compact and full4 maps are identical on those two local cases.
- Mean copy IoU: compact/full4 0.8373135073; full8 0.6796539323.
- Mean blur IoU: compact/full4 0.7946093823; full8 0.6435743429.
- No missed changed pixels in available maps for those local cases.
- Seven global operators: compact 0/3500 fine maps; full4/full8 have maps. Do NOT describe compact abstention as benign-operation tolerance or successful localisation.
- Mean copy verification seconds: compact 1.8694778719; full4 0.0567432067; full8 0.0213499214.
- Mean blur verification seconds: compact 1.8730140015; full4 0.0563990597; full8 0.0214323064.
- Paired compact-minus-full4 verification penalty: copy 1.8127346652 seconds, 95% interval [1.8028399352,1.8225432267]; blur 1.8166149418 [1.8026288740,1.8295447782].
- Mean signing seconds: approximately compact 1.09588, full4 .05057, full8 .01970.

Classical intervals use 10,000 paired source-image bootstrap draws, seed5101, not patients. Timings describe that run's environment; they are not machine-independent deployment guarantees. The review checked receipts/signatures/maps and summary consistency; it was not an independent full image/operator replay or proof of issuer trust.

Conclusion supported: a storage/coverage/latency tradeoff, not a universal winner or novelty/SOTA result.

7. CLASSIFIER TRAINING RETURN — MEASURED-VALIDATION

Model: torchvision DenseNet121, compatible ImageNet initialisation, binary head.
Adapted training: 20 epochs; Adam lr1e-5; batch size8; no augmentation; CUDA AMP; class weights computed on TRAIN only. Input normalisation mean [.485,.456,.406], std [.229,.224,.225]. This differs from author-specific clinical initialisation and is not exact PFF-WM reproduction.
Selection: first epoch attaining maximum validation balanced accuracy, epoch3.

Accuracy .864; balanced accuracy .7439671815; macroF1 .7070076867; ROC AUC .8453989704; melanoma sensitivity .5892857143; specificity .8986486486.
Always-nonMEL baseline: accuracy .888, BA .5, melanoma sensitivity0. Thus the model's accuracy is lower than the majority reference despite better class-balanced performance.
Descriptive class-stratified image bootstrap95: BA [.6747888514,.8099501287], MEL sensitivity [.4464285714,.7142857143], AUC [.7946720158,.8922558719]. Validation selected the checkpoint; these are selection-biased development intervals, not final performance.

8. COMPLETED FROZEN CLASSIFIER IMPACT — MEASURED-VALIDATION

500 validation images x12 cases =6,000 records. Same checkpoint; no retraining, threshold tuning or test evaluation. Ten existing operators plus adapted FGSM4 and PGD4. Original-image outputs, not watermarked inputs.

All records/source identity/protocol checks passed and all summary metrics and 2,000-repetition paired class-stratified image-bootstrap intervals were recomputed. Max clean probability difference versus training receipt: 9.5367431640625e-07. Stored predictions were not independently rerun against image files here.

Full numerical table is appended from reviewed evidence. Headline findings:
- Local copy:4 prediction changes; BA74.17%; MEL sensitivity unchanged33/56.
- Local blur surrogate:1 change; BA74.51%; MEL sensitivity unchanged33/56.
- JPEG70: BA67.39%; MEL sensitivity22/56 (39.29%), but overall accuracy89.2%, UP from86.4%.
- JPEG70 BA change:-7.0061 percentage points, descriptive paired95[-13.1435,-1.3111]. Its 14 correct-to-wrong and28 wrong-to-correct changes demonstrate why accuracy alone conceals class-dependent harm.
- Adapted FGSM: BA41.55%; MEL sensitivity1/56;80/432 initially correct cases become wrong (18.52%); overall138/500 wrong after attack.
- Adapted PGD: BA0.2252%; MEL sensitivity0/56;430/432 initially correct cases become wrong (99.54%); overall498/500 wrong; received AUC0.

Attack assumptions: white-box input gradients and ground-truth labels; untargeted cross-entropy ascent; clean start; RGB epsilon4/255; PGD3 steps of1/255; integer-RGB output evaluated. These are adapted attacks, not exact author reproduction, not black-box attacks, and not a demonstrated universal security failure of all medical classifiers.

No multiplicity correction; no equivalence or noninferiority claim follows when an interval contains zero. Small local changes causing few classifier flips do not prove safety or reduce the need for authenticity. JPEG70 effects do not establish watermark-induced harm. Classifier performance is not a clinician outcome.

9. EARLIER DEVELOPMENT AND FAILED APPROACHES

These experiments guided engineering decisions but must not be pooled with validation500 or represented as independent final evaluations.

Earlier compact coding development:32-image experiments, matched budgets and weak-hash/index controls explored evidence capacity and false/missing localisation. Larger evidence savings came with coverage limits. A public 8-bit collision probe found a false-empty result after241 tries; this is a weakness of that short-fingerprint control, not an Ed25519 signature break. Index stopping-set experiments exposed missed multiple-tile changes. Earlier acceleration preserved sampled outputs but does not replace fresh target-environment timing.

EditGuard: official clean checkpoint adapter achieved strict loading after recorded compatibility changes. Earlier synthetic, upstream-example and small real-image development runs were executed, with mixed localisation and distortion results. A small mirror pilot had non-original224-pixel inputs explicitly resized; its provenance differs from the present cohort. It is not an official AGE-Set/AIGC reproduction. Clean-checkpoint JPEG behaviour must not be generalised to a degradation-trained checkpoint. Some early runs detect local replacements but miss one-bit/global brightness changes; contracts differ from exact integrity checking. No matched medical SOTA-superiority conclusion is available. A matched-container validation return has since passed receipt, map-arithmetic and packet-signature checks; it has not been independently replayed from source pixels or models. Adding signed4 costs524378 bytes/image and changes the system; equal containers do not imply equal cost or native end-to-end parity.

Learned refiners: earlier main and attention approaches failed under the selected shared rule. A seven-fixture synthetic20-epoch diagnostic selected epochs6/7; all7 pixel maps were background, meanIoU0 for both. Lower TRAIN-only pixel threshold .35 increased meanIoU to .25412/.38927 but created112/1157 intact false-positive pixels. This was not accepted as a fix. Shared-stem block/pixel gradient cosine was negative on4/7 fixtures for each model; that observation is not causal proof of why learning failed. No revised successful refiner is claimed.

Earlier Haar/luma carriers, signed-grid controls, sparse/index/count schemes and feature-removal experiments remain historical probes. Removing features is not the same as physically removing embedded evidence. A learned-mask-guided comparison needs an image-only control with identical final post-masking; that stronger comparison is not completed. These historical branches of investigation should be discussed briefly as failed alternatives, not presented as many successful novel methods.

10. IMPLEMENTED EXTENSION: DIAGNOSTIC-CONSTRAINED EMBEDDING

Source: src/diagnostic_constrained_embedding.py.
Defaults: block8, QIM step8; two red carrier samples per block;4096 message bits per512 image; correction bound4 RGB levels;12 steps of1 RGB level. Standard QIM is established prior art. Carrier locations are public, so targeted message tampering is possible. This is not a robust authentication construction.

Guided objective: KL(original guide probabilities || corrected guide probabilities), using frozen classifier weights and input gradients. Candidate outputs are rounded uint8, carriers restored exactly, and a strict lower-KL selection rule includes fixed embedding as the fallback. Guide improvement is partly built into selection; it is not an independent efficacy endpoint. Original predictions can be wrong.

Six controls in TRAIN pilot v2:
1. Fixed QIM.
2. Uniform weaker QIM: changes embedding margin, not equal-robustness control.
3. Energy-matched random directions: exact per-sample distortion magnitudes, not query/compute matched; uses guided output magnitudes.
4. Guided correction.
5. Forward-query-matched random search: same original/baseline/search forward budget, same carrier locks and non-carrier Linf allowance; not energy, wall-clock or gradient-compute matched.
6. Strict distortion-gated correction: evaluates the already generated guided output and falls back to fixed if squared error increases. It does not save optimisation cost.

At12 steps, guided optimisation uses27 forward and12 backward queries; random search uses27 forward and0 backward queries, excluding separate reporting/evaluator predictions and shared data preparation. Reported counts refer to optimisation, not total program calls.

A full signed4 sidecar authenticates each final RGB image independently; its entire524378 bytes is counted. The included public key is a reproducibility aid, not a deployment trust anchor. Payload recovery and exact signed integrity are evaluated separately under operators.

STRUCTURAL LIMIT — NOT JUST AN EMPIRICAL FAILURE:
Let C be locked carriers, x original and b fixed embedding. b equals x outside C. For any y with y[C]=b[C],
||y-x||^2 = ||b-x||^2 + ||(y-x)[outside C]||^2.
Every nonzero non-carrier correction therefore strictly increases squared distortion over b. Equal-distortion improvement over this fixed baseline is impossible under these constraints. The strict gate necessarily rejects nonzero correction. A useful claim must justify a tradeoff or test a separately versioned formulation; no such replacement formulation is implemented/proven here.

Executed SYNTHETIC8x8 example: baseline MSE.166667, guideKL.048425, unseenKL.048425. Guided MSE.25, guideKL0, unseenKL.185144. Energy-matched random tied this fixture. A stronger19-forward-query random search also attained guideKL0; guided additionally used8 backward queries. The distortion gate rejected correction, squared error48 vs32. All returned clean payloads decoded. This is an explicit counterexample to automatic guide-to-unseen-model transfer and evidence against treating this toy as efficacy. A non-carrier edit leaves payload recovery unchanged but fails separate signed RGB verification.

PENDING MEDICAL PILOT:
diagnostic_embedding_train12_v2.ipynb uploaded with the reviewed guide hash. First12 fixed TRAIN images, not outcome-selected. Expected72 image-variant records and720 operator-recovery entries;72 full4 packets total37,755,216 bytes before compression. No independent evaluator is available in this handoff; independent_evaluator_tested must be false. Same guide TRAIN images are not independent efficacy evidence. Preparation may reconstruct the whole cohort, but only TRAIN images enter pilot predictions. No medical result is yet available/reviewed. GPU runtime not measured; do not promise a fixed duration.

11. PRIOR ART AND NOVELTY BOUNDARY

Faculty: Dr. Choudhary Shyam Prakash, NIT Andhra Pradesh. Public interests directly include image forensics, medical watermarking and medical authentication. Public profile does not establish personal review criteria.
Profile: https://scholar.google.co.in/citations?user=u8lyCQQAAAAJ&hl=en

Primary abstract/introduction reviewed: ROI/RONI split, no ROI embedding, local tamper detection/recovery bits, DWT-SVD RONI embedding. Published fidelity/security claims are not our reproduced results. Full methods/result reproduction remains pending. Diagnostic-region protection cannot be claimed as our new idea.
- PFF-WM: https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2667305326000591
Local extracted paper reviewed, including saliency-aware embedding and classifier/Grad-CAM explanation alignment. Generic diagnostic-aware watermarking already overlaps. Full bibliographic details need final formal verification.
- MedSign, 'Pathology-Aware Adaptive Watermarking for Text-Driven Medical Image Synthesis': https://arxiv.org/html/2503.08346v1
Primary page reviewed: pathology-localisation adaptive strength and preservation in generated-image watermarking. Different setting, but diagnostic preservation itself is not new.
- EditGuard, CVPR2024: https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2024/html/Zhang_EditGuard_Versatile_Image_Watermarking_for_Tamper_Localization_and_Copyright_Protection_CVPR_2024_paper.html
Proactive watermark-based localisation/copyright protection already exists. Official checkpoint locally adapted/executed in development; not matched final reproduction.
- OmniGuard, CVPR2025: https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2025/html/Zhang_OmniGuard_Hybrid_Manipulation_Localization_via_Augmented_Versatile_Deep_Image_Watermarking_CVPR_2025_paper.html
Hybrid proactive/passive and degradation-aware directions already exist; not executed as our matched baseline.
- WAM: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07231 ; localised watermark messages/region segmentation differ from exact changed-RGB truth.
- DICOM PS3.15: https://dicom.nema.org/medical/dicom/current/output/html/part15.html ; existing medical signature profiles.
- RFC8032: https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc8032/ ; standard EdDSA, not our invention.
- Related nonembedding medical authentication: https://doi.org/10.3390/electronics15010007 ; literature reference, not reproduced/compliance evidence.

Search leads only, not fully reviewed: adversarial watermark/recognition interaction https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.16056 and fidelity-preserving perturbation modulation https://arxiv.org/html/2411.11688v1 . No novelty conclusion should be based on their search excerpts.

The narrower payload-locked correction may be an engineering formulation worth testing, but exhaustive prior-art novelty is not established. No 'first', 'unique' or SOTA-superior claim is justified.

12. CHRONOLOGY AND CLAIMS WE MUST NOT RETROFIT

Earlier compact/learned studies exposed storage, coverage, trainability and attribution problems. These motivated separating integrity from localisation and proposing diagnostic constraints. The correction code was implemented BEFORE the completed classifier-impact return was analysed. Therefore it is false to say the JPEG70 result originally caused that implementation. It can inform subsequent experiments and evaluation priorities, but not be fabricated as retrospective motivation.

Neither the compact zero-distortion study nor the classifier's original-image operator study establishes clinical harm caused by watermark embedding. The synthetic guide-transfer failure disproves automatic preservation guarantees, not the possibility of a future useful constrained method.

13. WHAT WE WOULD SHOW FACULTY — PROPOSED EIGHT-PART STORY

1. Problem: exact integrity, localisation and diagnosis are different requirements.
2. Prior work and strongest alternatives: ROI/RONI, PFF-WM, MedSign, EditGuard, signed manifests. Separate literature from executed comparisons.
3. Protocol: fixed1500/500/500, lesion-disjoint not proven patient-independent; explicit512RGB adaptations and label order.
4. Completed evidence tradeoff: storage saving beside verification penalty, local IoU and global abstention; no cherry-picked winner score.
5. Negative learned results and controls: show why attention/threshold tuning did not solve the problem.
6. Completed diagnostic study: include clean baseline, JPEG accuracy/sensitivity paradox, white-box attack assumptions and paired intervals.
7. Implemented candidate: equations, fixed carriers, six controls, structural distortion limitation and unseen-guide counterexample. Add medical pilot results ONLY after review; currently label pending.
8. Conclusions: supported findings, unresolved novelty/utility, limitations, remaining validation and final-test freeze.

Proposed demo, not a claim already performed live: intact verification, local changed-pixel/tile localisation, compact global abstention, payload-surviving non-carrier edit caught by signed RGB authentication. Use labelled synthetic examples or authorised images; never imply a toy demonstrates clinical success. Keep a saved failure trace if a live execution fails.

14. REMAINING WORK AND STOP/GO GATES

A. Execute/review the bounded TRAIN pilot. Check complete matrix, correct hashes, payload invariants, distortion, random-control advantage and failure rates. If there is no useful benefit over controls, do not scale unchanged.
B. Obtain a genuinely separate evaluator before any guide-transfer efficacy claim. Same classifier agreement is insufficient; another seed is weaker than a separate architecture/domain. No independent evaluator currently available.
C. Execute matched EditGuard validation and account for full added evidence and trust differences. Existing runner fixture does not close this gap.
D. Freeze methods, thresholds, primary endpoints, failure treatment and source/model hashes. Only then evaluate the final held-out test. Do not repeatedly tune on final test.
E. Final audit of code/data/metric consistency, clean reproducibility, licensing, literature differentiation and deliverables. Report missing evidence explicitly.

There is no defensible single completion percentage: comparative study is substantially developed; a useful new-method claim remains unestablished. No reliable completion date or guaranteed faculty approval can be inferred. Software tests do not substitute for independent statistical, cryptographic or clinical review.

15. QUESTIONS FOR THE INDEPENDENT REVIEWER

Please assess candidly:
1. Is the research question coherent, or does the extension dilute the evidence-budget study?
2. Is the current scope sufficient as a rigorous study, and what exact additional evidence would establish a useful method contribution?
3. Is the fixed-carrier extension worth continuing given its distortion identity and current random/transfer counterexamples?
4. Which closer primary prior art might already cover constrained classifier-preserving correction?
5. Are the baselines, payload/evidence accounting and integrity/localisation contracts fair?
6. Are cohort independence, model selection and image-bootstrap limits correctly stated? What hidden leakage checks are missing?
7. Does the JPEG70 accuracy/sensitivity observation warrant the stated interpretation without a clinical claim?
8. Which conclusions should be removed, weakened or independently replayed before faculty presentation?
9. Is the pending independent evaluator essential now, or should we first stop this extension after its bounded feasibility test?
10. What is the smallest decisive next experiment, rather than a list of expensive speculative models?

Please distinguish a concern supported by this evidence from a hypothesis requiring code/data inspection. The dossier is an internally assembled snapshot, not itself an independent review. Hashes establish file identity, not truth or trust.
